dilation
z\to\delta_{t}(z)=tz
. Replacing
r
by
tr
, and

A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}|_{U_{2r}}

by its conjugate by
\delta_{t}^{*}
, we may as well assume that

\left\|B_{0}^{(\geq 1)}\right\|_{C^{b}\left(U_{2r}\right)}
is arbitrary small.

We will show by
induction that for
1\leq i\leq n
, there are smooth sections

J_{i}
of
\mathrm{Aut}^{0}(\Lambda\left(\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{n}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D)
, and antiholomorphic superconnections

\overline{\partial}+B_{i}
on
U_{r}
such that
(J_{i},B_{i})
do not contain the Grassmannian variable
d\overline{z}^{j},j\leq i
, and that

(5.16)

J_{i}^{-1}\left(\overline{\partial}+B_{i-1}\right)J_{i}=\overline{\partial}+B_{i}.

If
J=J_{1}\cdots J_{n}
, we will obtain part of our theorem by conjugating

A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
by
J^{-1}
.

For
i=1
, we need to find

J_{1}
such that
J_{1}
and

\overline{\partial}J_{1}+B_{0}J_{1}
do not contain
d\overline{z}^{1}
, in which case

(5.17)

\displaystyle B_{1}=J_{1}^{-1}\left(\overline{\partial}J_{1}\right)+J_{1}^{-1}B_{0}J_{1}.

In the sequel we view
\Lambda\left(\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{n-1}\right)

as being generated by

d\overline{z}^{2},\ldots,d\overline{z}^{n}
.
If
z=(z^{1},z^{\prime})\in U_{r}
, with
z^{\prime}\in\mathbf{C}^{n-1}
fixed for the moment, we have to find an invertible section
J_{1}\in C^{\infty}(U_{r},\Lambda(\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{n-1})\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}(D))
of degree
0
such that

(5.18)

\frac{\partial J_{1}}{\partial\overline{z}^{1}}+\left(i_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}^{1}}}B_{0}\right)J_{1}=0.

Let
\phi\in C^{\infty,c}\left(\mathbf{C}^{n},[0,1]\right)
be such
that
\phi=1
on
U_{r}
, the support of which is included in
U_{2r}
. It is enough
to show that for
|z^{\prime}|
small
enough, there is an invertible section
j_{z^{\prime}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{C},\Lambda(\mathbf{C}^{n-1})\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}(D))
of total degree
0
, which depends smoothly on
z^{\prime}
, such that

(5.19)

\frac{\partial j_{z^{\prime}}}{\partial\overline{z}^{1}}+\phi\left(i_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}^{1}}}B_{0}\right)j_{z^{\prime}}=0.

If
j_{z^{\prime}}=1+g_{z^{\prime}}
, equation (
5.19
) is equivalent to

(5.20)

\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}^{1}}+\phi i_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}^{1}}}B_{0}\right)g_{z^{\prime}}+\phi i_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}^{1}}}B_{0}=0.

The form
\frac{dz^{1}}{2i\pi z^{1}}
on
\mathbf{C}
is locally integrable, and
by Poincaré-Lelong (
2.25
), we have the equation of currents,

(5.21)

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}\frac{dz^{1}}{{2i\pi}z^{1}}=\delta_{0}.

Let
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
be the two canonical projections
\mathbf{C}^{2}\to\mathbf{C}
. Let
\pi:\mathbf{C}^{2}\to\mathbf{C}
be given by
\left(z^{1},z^{1\prime}\right)=z^{1}+z^{1\prime}
.
Let
C^{c}\left(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{C}\right)
be the vector space of continuous
complex functions on
\mathbf{C}
with compact support. If
f\in C^{c}\left(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{C}\right)
, put

(5.22)

Rf=\pi_{*}\left[\pi_{1}^{*}fd\overline{z}^{1}\pi_{2}^{*}\frac{dz^{1}}{2i\pi z^{1}}\right].

Equation (
5.22
) gives the convolution of two
distributions. Let
C^{b}\left(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{C}\right)
be the vector space of
bounded continuous functions from
\mathbf{C}
into itself. Then
Rf\in C^{b}\left(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{C}\right)
.
By (
5.21
), we get

(5.23)

\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}^{1}}Rf=f.

Let

C^{0,b}\left(\mathbf{C},\Lambda\left(\mathbf{C}^{n-1}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)\right)
be the space of bounded continuous sections of degree
0
of
\Lambda\left(\mathbf{C}^{n-1}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
. Let
F_{z^{\prime}}
be the bounded operator acting on

C^{0,b}\left(\mathbf{C},\Lambda(\mathbf{C}^{n-1})\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}(D)\right)
,

(5.24)

F_{z^{\prime}}=R\phi i_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}^{1}}}B_{0}.

Since
B_{0}
is of degree
1
,
F_{z^{\prime}}
is of degree
0
.

By (
5.23
), we get

(5.25)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{1}}F_{z^{\prime}}=\phi i_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}^{1}}}B_{0}.

By (
5.22
), (
5.24
), there is
C>0
such that

(5.26)

\|F_{z^{\prime}}\|\leq C\left\|B_{0}^{(\geq 1)}\right\|_{C^{b}(U_{2r})}.

Using (
5.25
), equation (
5.20
) can be
written in the form

(5.27)

\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}^{1}}\left[\left(1+F_{z^{\prime}}\right)g_{z^{\prime}}+F_{z^{\prime}}1\right]=0.

We may and we will assume that
\left\|B_{0}^{(\geq 1)}\right\|_{C^{b}\left(U_{2r}\right)}
is
small enough, so that, when using (
5.26
),
\left\|F_{z^{\prime}}\right\|<1
. Then

1+F_{z^{\prime}}
is
invertible with uniformly bounded inverse.

Put

(5.28)

\displaystyle g_{z^{\prime}}=-(1+F_{z^{\prime}})^{-1}F_{z^{\prime}}1.

Then
g_{z^{\prime}}
is a solution of degree
0
of (
5.27
), and so it solves
(
5.20
). Also it is obviously smooth in all variables.
Moreover,

(5.29)

\|g_{z^{\prime}}\|\leq\frac{\|F_{z^{\prime}}\|}{1-\|F_{z^{\prime}}\|}.

If
\left\|B_{0}^{(\geq 1)}\right\|_{C^{b}\left(U_{2r}\right)}
is small enough so that

\|F_{z^{\prime}}\|\leq 1/3
, then
\|g_{z^{\prime}}\|\leq 1/2
. In particular,
j_{z^{\prime}}=1+g_{z^{\prime}}
is invertible.

Assume that
i\geq 1
and that we have constructed
(J_{k},B_{k})
for

k\leq i-1
. By (
5.16
), we have to find
J_{i}
such
that
J_{i}
and
\overline{\partial}J_{i}+B_{i-1}J_{i}

do not contain
d\overline{z}^{1},\ldots,d\overline{z}^{i}
. This
is equivalent to the fact that

J_{i}
is holomorphic in
z^{1},\ldots,z^{i-1}
, and

(5.30)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial J_{i}}{\partial\overline{z}^{i}}+\left(i_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}^{i}}}B_{i-1}\right)J_{i}=0.

By the argument given in (
5.16
)–(
5.29
), using
again a dilation
\delta_{t},\left|t\right|<1
,we may
assume that

\left\|B_{i-1}^{(\geq 1)}\right\|_{C^{b}\left(U_{2r}\right)}
is small enough, so that
(
5.30
) can be solved. Since

\left(\overline{\partial}+B_{i-1}\right)^{2}=0
and since
B_{i-1}
does not contain

d\overline{z}^{1},\ldots,d\overline{z}^{i-1}
,
B_{i-1}
is
a holomorphic function of

z^{1},\ldots,z^{i-1}
. The arguments that were used before show that
the solution
J_{i}
is also holomorphic in
z^{1},\ldots,z^{i-1}
.

As explained after (
5.16
), for
r>0
small enough, on

U=U_{r}
,
there exists a smooth section
\underline{v}_{0}
of

\mathrm{End}^{0}\left(D\right)
such that

(5.31)

J^{-1}A^{E_{0},\prime\prime}|_{U}J=\overline{\partial}+\underline{v}_{0}.

Let
J^{(0)}
be the component of
J
of degree
0
in

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
. By (
5.6
), (
5.31
), we get

(5.32)

\left[J^{(0)}\right]^{-1}v_{0}J^{(0)}=\underline{v}_{0}.

Put

(5.33)

\displaystyle K=J\left[J^{(0)}\right]^{-1},

\displaystyle\underline{\nabla}^{D|_{U}\prime\prime}=J^{(0)}\overline{\partial}\left[J^{(0)}\right]^{-1}.

Then
\underline{\nabla}^{D|_{U}\prime\prime}
is a holomorphic structure
on
D|_{U}
. By (
5.31
)–(
5.33
), we get

(5.34)

K^{-1}A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}K=\underline{\nabla}^{D|_{U}\prime\prime}+v_{0},

which completes the proof of our theorem.
∎

5.3.
Antiholomorphic superconnections and coherent sheafs

Let
F
be a smooth vector bundle on
X
, and let

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}\left(F\right)
be
the sheaf of smooth sections of
F
.

Definition 5.4
.

Let

\mathscr{E}
be the sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-complexes

\left(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}\left(E\right),A^{E\prime\prime}\right)
, and let

\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
denote
its cohomology.
For
r\geq 0
, let
\left(\mathscr{E}_{r},d_{r}\right)
denote the spectral
sequence of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-sheaves associated with the
filtration by

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\right)
.

Let

HD
be the cohomology of
\left(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}\left(D\right),v_{0}\right)
.

The sheaf
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
is a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded filtered sheaf of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules.
Also

(5.35)

\displaystyle\left(\mathscr{E}_{0},d_{0}\right)=\left(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D\right),v_{0}\right),

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{1}=\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}}HD.

The differential
d_{1}
on
\mathscr{E}_{1}
is such that

d_{1}^{2}=0
. Also it verifies Leibniz rule with respect to
multiplication by
\Omega^{0,\scriptsize\bullet}\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
.
Therefore
d_{1}
induces a holomorphic structure on the

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-module
HD
, which will
also be denoted

\nabla^{HD\prime\prime}
.

Remark 5.5
.

Assume that
HD
has locally constant rank. Then
HD
is a smooth
vector bundle on
X
. Then
\nabla^{HD\prime\prime}
defines a canonical
holomorphic structure on
HD
.

When fixing a splitting in (
5.2
), using (
5.3
), and writing

A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
as in (
5.6
), from the three equations in
(
5.7
), we can also derive a construction of
\nabla^{HD\prime\prime}
. Indeed, by (
5.7
), the connection
\nabla^{D\prime\prime}
induces a holomorphic
structure on the
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-module
HD
, which is just
d_{1}=\nabla^{HD\prime\prime}
. This holomorphic structure is canonical, and does not
depend on the splitting in (
5.2
), as is also clear from
(
5.11
).

Definition 5.6
.

Let

\mathscr{H}HD
be the cohomology
of the complex of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
\left(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}}HD,\nabla^{HD\prime\prime}\right)
.

Then
\mathscr{H}HD
is a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module. By
(
5.35
),

(5.36)

\mathscr{E}_{2}=\mathscr{H}HD.

We recall a fundamental result of Block
[
Bl10
, Lemma 4.5]
.

Theorem 5.7
.

The complex
\mathscr{E}
has
\mathbf{Z}
-graded coherent cohomology
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
. The spectral sequence
\mathscr{E}_{r}
degenerates at

\mathscr{E}_{2}=\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
, and
F^{1}\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}=0
.

For
i\geq 1
,

(5.37)

\mathscr{H}^{i}HD=0.

We have the identity of
\mathbf{Z}
-graded coherent sheaves,

(5.38)

\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{H}HD,

and also the identity of
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-modules,

(5.39)

\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}=HD.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.3
, if
U
is sufficiently
small, after conjugation by a smooth section of

\mathrm{Aut}^{0}_{0}\left(E_{0}\right)
on
U
, we may and we
will assume that on
U
,
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
has the form

(5.40)

A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}=v_{0}+\nabla^{D\prime\prime}.

In (
5.40
),
\nabla^{D\prime\prime}
is a holomorphic
structure on
D|_{U}
that preserves the
\mathbf{Z}
-grading and is
such that

(5.41)

\nabla^{D\prime\prime}v_{0}=0.

In (
5.40
),
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
now preserves the
decreasing filtration associated with the degree in
D
, which was
not the case in the original form of
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
. We
will compute
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
using the associated spectral
sequence.

Let
\mathscr{H}D
be the cohomology of

\left(\mathcal{O}_{U}\left(D\right),v_{0}\right)
.
By
[
D09
, Theorems II.3.13 and II.3.14]
,
\mathscr{H}D
is a coherent sheaf.
Using the Poincaré lemma for Dolbeault cohomology
[
D09
, Lemma
I.3.29]
, if
V
is a small polydisk,
then

(5.42)

\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}\left(V\right)=\mathscr{H}D\left(V\right),

so that

(5.43)

\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{H}D.

Therefore
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
is a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded coherent sheaf. Also,
the natural
filtration
F
is trivial on
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
, so that
F^{1}\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}=0
, a property which is intrinsic to
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
, and does not depend on the choice of a conjugation.

By
[
Ma67
, Corollary VI.1.12]
,
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}

is flat on
\mathcal{O}_{X}
, so that the map

(5.44)

\rho:\mathscr{H}D\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{U}}\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}\to HD

is an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}
-modules. Moreover,
the action of
d_{1}
on
HD
is just
\nabla^{D\prime\prime}
. Using the Poincaré lemma, we deduce that for
i\geq 1
,
\mathscr{H}^{i}HD=0
, and also that

(5.45)

\mathscr{H}D=\mathscr{H}HD.

By the above, the
cohomology of
\left(\mathscr{E}_{1},d_{1}\right)
coincides with
\mathscr{H}D=\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
. Therefore, the original
spectral sequence degenerates at
\mathscr{E}_{2}
, and
\mathscr{E}_{2}=\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
. Using (
5.43
), (
5.44
), we get
(
5.39
). The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 5.8
.

Assume that
HD
has locally constant rank. As we saw in Remark

5.5
,
HD
is equipped with a canonical holomorphic
structure
\nabla^{HD\prime\prime}
. By Theorem
5.7
,
we get

(5.46)

\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}=\mathcal{O}_{X}\left(HD\right).

In general,
HD
depends only on
D,v_{0}
, and
\mathscr{H}HD

depends only on
D,v_{0},\nabla^{D\prime\prime}
. By (
5.38
),

\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
depends only on
D,v_{0},\nabla^{D\prime\prime}
, and
not on the full
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
.

5.4.
The determinant line bundle

Since
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{H}HD
is a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded coherent
sheaf, by Knudsen-Mumford
[
KnM76
]
, its determinant

\det\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
is a canonically defined holomorphic
line bundle on
X
.

Let

\det D
denote the smooth determinant line bundle,

(5.47)

\det D=\bigotimes_{r}^{r^{\prime}}\left(\det D^{i}\right)^{\left(-1\right)^{i}}.

Now we fix identifications in (
5.2
), (
5.3
),
and we write
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
as in (
5.6
).
The
antiholomorphic connection
\nabla^{D\prime\prime}
on
D
induces a
corresponding antiholomorphic connection

\nabla^{\det D\prime\prime}
on
\det D
.

Theorem 5.9
.

The antiholomorphic connection
\nabla^{\det D\prime\prime}
on

\det D
does not depend on the splitting in (
5.2
),
(
5.3
). It defines a holomorphic structure on
\det D
.
Finally, we have the canonical isomorphism of holomorphic line
bundles on
X
,

(5.48)

\det D\simeq\det\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}.

Proof.

We use the notation in (
5.10
), (
5.11
). Let

\underline{\nabla}^{\det D\prime\prime}
denote the antiholomorphic
connection on
\det D
associated with
\underline{\nabla}^{D}
. By
(
5.11
), we obtain

(5.49)

\underline{\nabla}^{\det D\prime\prime}=\nabla^{\det D\prime\prime}+\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\left[\alpha,v_{0}\right]\right].

Since supertraces vanish on supercommutators, by (
5.49
), we
get

(5.50)

\underline{\nabla}^{\det D\prime\prime}=\nabla^{\det D\prime\prime},

which proves the first part of our proposition. Also

(5.51)

\nabla^{\det D\prime\prime,2}=\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\nabla^{D\prime\prime,2}\right].

Using the third identity in (
5.7
), (
5.51
), and the
fact that supertraces vanish on supercommutators, we get

(5.52)

\nabla^{\det D\prime\prime,2}=0.

so that
\nabla^{\det D\prime\prime}
is a holomorphic structure on
\det D
.

Now we use the notation in the proof of Theorem
5.7
. On a small open set
U
in
X
, after a local conjugation by a smooth
section of
\mathrm{Aut}^{0}_{0}\left(E_{0}\right)
, we can write
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
in the form (
5.40
),
\nabla^{D\prime\prime}

being now a
holomorphic structure on
D|_{U}
. By Knudsen-Mumford
[
KnM76
]
, on
U
,
the holomorphic line bundle
\det\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
is just the
holomorphic line bundle
\det D
equipped with the holomorphic
structure induced by this specific
\nabla^{D\prime\prime}
. As we saw before, this
is just our original
\nabla^{\det D\prime\prime}
whose construction
does not depend on the conjugation. The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

5.5.
The case where
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}=0

We will now give conditions under which
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}=0
.

Theorem 5.10
.

The
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
vanishes if
and only if there exists a smooth section
k_{0}
of degree
-1

of
\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
such that

(5.53)

\left[v_{0},k_{0}\right]=1.

An
equivalent condition is that
for any
x\in X
, the complex

\left(D,v_{0}\right)_{x}
is exact.

Another equivalent condition is that there exists a smooth section
k
of

\mathrm{End}\left(E\right)
of degree
-1
such that

(5.54)

\left[A^{E\prime\prime},k\right]=1.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.7
,
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}=0
if and only if
we have the identity of
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-modules

HD=0
. Since
D
is soft, by
[
D09
, Proposition
IV.4.14]
, this is equivalent to the exactness of the
complex
\left(C^{\infty}\left(X,D\right),v_{0}\right)
. Since

C^{\infty}\left(X,D\right)
is projective over
C^{\infty}\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
, this is
equivalent to the existence of a smooth section
k_{0}
of

\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
of degree
-1
such that

\left[v_{0},k_{0}\right]=1
, which implies that for any
x\in X
,
the complex
\left(D,v_{0}\right)_{x}
is exact. Conversely, if this last
assumption is verified, we find easily there is a global smooth

k_{0}
such that
\left[v_{0},k_{0}\right]=1
, from which we deduce
that
HD=0
.

To complete the proof of our theorem, we need to show that if
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}=0
, then
k

exists. We use a splitting as in (
5.2
), (
5.3
),
we replace
E
by
E_{0}
, and we write
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
as
in (
5.6
). We write
k
in the form

(5.55)

k=\sum_{i\geq 0}k_{i},

with
k_{i}
a smooth section of

\Lambda^{i}\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
of degree
-1
. We choose

k_{0}
as before.

If
\alpha
is a smooth section of

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
, we denote by
\alpha^{(\leq i)}

the sum of the components of
\alpha
whose degree in

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
is
\leq i
, and by
\alpha^{(i)}

the component that lies in

\Lambda^{i}\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
.

Assume that we
found
k_{j},j\leq i
such that

(5.56)

\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},\sum_{j\leq i}k_{j}\right]^{(\leq i)}=1.

We will show how to construct
k_{i+1}
so that (
5.56
) is
solved with
i
replaced by
i+1
. Assuming that
(
5.56
) has been solved, the corresponding equation with
i

replaced by
i+1
can be written in the form,

(5.57)

\left[v_{0},k_{i+1}\right]+\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},\sum_{j\leq i}k_{j}\right]^{(i+1)}=0.

Because of (
5.56
), we get

(5.58)

\left[v_{0},\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},\sum_{j\leq i}k_{j}\right]\right]^{(i+1)}=\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},\sum_{j\leq i}k_{j}\right]\right]^{(i+1)}=0.

By (
5.58
), to solve (
5.57
), we can take

(5.59)

k_{i+1}=-\left[k_{0},\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},\sum_{j\leq i}k_{j}\right]^{(i+1)}\right].

This completes the proof of the existence of
k
.

Another more direct proof is as follows. The vector bundle

\mathrm{End}\left(E\right)
can be equipped with the antiholomorphic
superconnection
\mathrm{ad}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)
. Moreover,

1
is a section of
\mathrm{End}\left(E\right)
such that

(5.60)

\left[A^{E\prime\prime},1\right]=0.

We claim that the complex
\left(C^{\infty}\left(X,\mathrm{End}\left(E\right)\right),\mathrm{ad}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)\right)
is exact,
which implies the existence of
k
.
Indeed let
L\left(k_{0}\right)
denote
left multiplication by
k_{0}
acting on
\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
. Then

(5.61)

\left[\mathrm{ad}\left(v_{0}\right),L\left(k_{0}\right)\right]=1,

so that the complex

\left(\mathrm{End}\left(D\right),\mathrm{ad}\left(v_{0}\right)\right)
is exact. Consider the global spectral
sequence associated with the filtration
F
. By (
5.61
), the first term of the
spectral sequence vanishes identically, so that the complex

\left(C^{\infty}\left(X,\mathrm{End}\left(E\right)\right),\mathrm{ad}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)\right)
is exact.
The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

5.6.
Superconnections, morphisms, and cones

Let
\left(\underline{E},A^{\underline{E}\prime\prime}\right)
be
another couple similar
to
\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right)
. The objects associated with

\underline{E}
will be underlined. A morphism
\phi:E\to\underline{E}
is a smooth morphism of degree
0
of

\mathbf{Z}
-graded
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
-vector bundles,
which is such that
A^{\underline{E}\prime\prime}\phi=\phi A^{E\prime\prime}
. Then
\phi
induces a morphism of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-complexes

\mathscr{E}\to\underline{\mathscr{E}}
that commutes with
multiplication by
\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{X}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\right)
. Then
\phi
induces a
morphism
E_{0}\to\underline{E}_{0}
, a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-complexes

\left(D,v_{0}\right)\to\left(\underline{D},\underline{v}_{0}\right)
, a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-complexes

\left(HD,\nabla^{HD\prime\prime}\right)\to\left(H\underline{D},\nabla^{H\underline{D}\prime\prime}\right)
, and
morphisms
of coherent sheaves

\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{H}\underline{\mathscr{E}}
.

Here, we follow the conventions of Subsection
4.3
. We form the cone

(5.62)

\mathscr{C}=\mathrm{cone}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right).

By (
4.26
), the underlying vector bundle
C
is such that

(5.63)

C^{\scriptsize\bullet}=E^{\scriptsize\bullet+1}\oplus\underline{E}^{\scriptsize\bullet}.

Then
C
is an object similar to
E
. The corresponding
antiholomorphic superconnection is denoted
A^{C\prime\prime}_{\phi}
,
and as in (
4.27
), we have the identity,

(5.64)

A^{C\prime\prime}_{\phi}=\begin{bmatrix}A^{E\prime\prime}&0\\
\phi\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}&A^{\underline{E}\prime\prime}.\end{bmatrix}

As in (
4.28
), we have the exact sequence of complexes,

(5.65)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\mathscr{E}}^{\scriptsize\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{C}^{\scriptsize\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{E}^{\scriptsize\bullet+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

from which we get the long exact sequence of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules,

(5.66)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ldots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{H}^{\scriptsize\bullet}\underline{\mathscr{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.84726pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 77.84726pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{H}^{\scriptsize\bullet}\mathscr{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 127.16672pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 127.16672pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{H}^{\scriptsize\bullet+1}\mathscr{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 158.08195pt\raise 7.05058pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.30058pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi\left(-1\right)^{\cdot+1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 186.19458pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 186.19458pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{H}^{\scriptsize\bullet+1}\underline{\mathscr{E}}\ldots}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

We have corresponding exact sequences involving the
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-modules
HD
and their pointwise version.

If
\phi:\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{H}\underline{\mathscr{E}}
is an isomorphism, then
\phi
is said to be a
quasi-isomorphism. As we saw in Subsection
4.3
, this
is equivalent to

(5.67)

\mathscr{H}\mathscr{C}=0.

Using Theorem
5.10
, we find that
\phi
is
a quasi-isomorphism if and only if for any
x\in X
,

\phi:D_{x}\to\underline{D}_{x}
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Given
t\in\mathbf{C}
, we can still define
M_{t}\in\mathrm{End}\left(C\right)
as
in (
4.30
). Namely,
M_{t}
acts by multiplication by
1
on

E
, and by multiplication by
t
on
\underline{E}
. As in
(
4.31
), for
t\neq 0
, we have the identity

(5.68)

A^{C\prime\prime}_{t\phi}=M_{t}A^{C\prime\prime}_{\phi}M_{t}^{-1}.

5.7.
Pull-backs

Let
Y
be a compact complex manifold, and let
f:X\to Y
be a
holomorphic map. If
H
is a vector bundle on
Y
,
f^{*}H
denote the
pull-back of
H
to
X
. Then
df^{*}
maps
f^{*}T^{*}Y
into
T^{*}X
.
Therefore

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
is a

\Lambda\left(\overline{f^{*}T^{*}Y}\right)
-module.

We use the notation of Subsection
5.1
, except that now

\left(F,A^{F\prime\prime}\right)
is an antiholomorphic
superconnection on
Y
with diagonal bundle
D_{F}
. Put

(5.69)

E=\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{\Lambda\left(\overline{f^{*}T^{*}Y}\right)}f^{*}F.

Then
E
is a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded vector bundle on
X
, the degree in
E

being the sum of the degrees in the two factors of the right
hand-side. Also
E
is a

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
-module, so that it is
equipped with the corresponding filtration.

By (
4.6
),
we get

(5.70)

E_{0}^{p,q}=\Lambda^{p}\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}f^{*}D_{F}^{q}.

In particular,
E
on
X
has exactly the same properties as
F
on

Y
, and the associated diagonal bundle is
D_{E}=f^{*}D_{F}
.

Let
\mu
be the canonical morphism
f^{*}F\to E
. Then
\mu
induces a
corresponding map
C^{\infty}\left(Y,F\right)\to C^{\infty}\left(X,E\right)
which is such that if
\alpha\in\Omega^{0,\scriptsize\bullet}\left(Y,\mathbf{C}\right),s\in C^{\infty}\left(Y,F\right)
, then

(5.71)

\mu\left(\alpha s\right)=df^{*}\left(\alpha\right)\mu s.

Proposition 5.11
.

There is a unique antiholomorphic superconnection
A^{E\prime\prime}

on
E
such that if
s\in C^{\infty}\left(Y,F\right)
, then

(5.72)

A^{E\prime\prime}\left(\mu s\right)=\mu\left(A^{F\prime\prime}s\right).

Proof.

Let
\alpha\in\Omega^{0,\scriptsize\bullet}\left(Y,\mathbf{C}\right)
. By
(
5.4
), (
5.71
), we get

(5.73)

\mu\left(A^{F\prime\prime}\alpha s\right)=\overline{\partial}^{X}\left(df^{*}\alpha\right)\mu\left(s\right)+\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}\,\alpha}\left(df^{*}\alpha\right)\mu\left(A^{F\prime\prime}s\right).

In particular, if
df^{*}\alpha=0
, (
5.73
) vanishes. This
completes the proof of our proposition.
∎

We will use the notation

(5.74)

E=f^{*}_{b}F.

The notation emphasizes the fact that
f^{*}_{b}F
is not the
classical pull-back
f^{*}F
.

Similarly, if
\mathscr{E}=\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right),\mathscr{F}=\left(F,A^{F\prime\prime}\right)
, we use the notation

(5.75)

\mathscr{E}=f^{*}_{b}\mathscr{F}.

There is an associated morphism of
\mathbf{Z}
-graded

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{Y}}f^{-1}\mathscr{H}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
.

If
\phi:\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
is a morphism, it
induces a morphism
f^{*}_{b}\phi:f^{*}_{b}\mathscr{F}\to f^{*}_{b}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
.

We fix a smooth splitting as in (
5.2
), that induces the
non-canonical identification
F\simeq F_{0}
in (
5.3
). This choice induces a corresponding
identification
E\simeq E_{0}
. Also,
\mu
induces a canonical map
f^{*}F_{0}\to E_{0}
.
We write

A^{F_{0}\prime\prime}

as in (
5.9
).

The connection

\nabla^{D_{F}\prime\prime}
induces a connection
\nabla^{f^{*}D_{F}\prime\prime}
on
f^{*}D_{F}
. Let
\mu B
be the section of

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(f^{*}D_{F}\right)
deduced
from
B
by the above pull-back map. Then

(5.76)

A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}=\nabla^{f^{*}D_{F}\prime\prime}+\mu B.

If
\alpha\in\Omega^{0,\scriptsize\bullet}\left(Y,\mathbf{C}\right)
, from
now on, we will also use the
notation
f^{*}\alpha
instead of
df^{*}\alpha
.

5.8.
Tensor products

Let
\mathscr{E}=\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right),\mathscr{F}=\left(F,A^{F\prime\prime}\right)
be two antiholomorphic superconnections on
X
, with associated diagonal vector bundles

D_{E},D_{F}
. Put

(5.77)

E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}F=E\widehat{\otimes}_{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}F.

This defines an antiholomorphic superconnection

\mathscr{E}\widehat{\otimes}_{b}\mathscr{F}=\left(E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}F,A^{E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}F\prime\prime}\right)
. The corresponding
diagonal vector bundle is
D_{E}\widehat{\otimes}D_{F}
.

6.
An equivalence of categories

The purpose of this Section is to establish a result of Block

[
Bl10
]
, who showed there is an equivalence of categories
between the homotopy
category of antiholomorphic superconnections on
X
and

\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
.

This Section is organized as follows. In Subsection

6.1
, if
\mathscr{F}
is an object in

\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
, we construct a corresponding

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-complex of
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-modules

\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}
, and we show it is
quasi-isomorphic to
\mathscr{F}
.

In Subsection
6.2
, we introduce the
\mathrm{dg}
-category

\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dg}}\left(X\right)
of the antiholomorphic
superconnections
\mathscr{E}
, the
0
-cycle category

\mathrm{B}\left(X\right)
, and the homotopy category

\underline{\mathrm{B}}\left(X\right)
. In the next Subsections, we construct
an equivalence of categories between
\underline{\mathrm{B}}\left(X\right)

and
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
.

In Subsection
6.3
, we show that the natural functor

F_{X}:\mathrm{B}\left(X\right)\to\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
is essentially
surjective.

In Subsection
6.4
, we describe in more detail the
homotopy category
\underline{\mathrm{B}}\left(X\right)
.

In Subsection
6.5
, we show that the induced functor

\underline{F}_{X}=\underline{\mathrm{B}}\left(X\right)\to\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
is an equivalence of categories.

In Subsection
6.6
, we show the above equivalence of
categories is compatible with pull-backs.

In Subsection
6.7
, we prove the compatibility of this
equivalence with tensor products.

Finally, in Subsection
6.8
, we consider the case of
direct images.

6.1.
Some properties of
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)

Let
\mathscr{F}\in{\rm D_{\mathrm{coh}}^{b}}(X)
, and let
d^{\mathscr{F}}

be its differential.
Set

(6.1)

\mathscr{F}^{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathscr{F}.

Then
\mathscr{F}^{\infty}
is a perfect
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-complex. Let
d^{\mathscr{F}^{\infty}}
be the
differential on
\mathscr{F}^{\infty}
, and let
H\mathscr{F}^{\infty}
be the cohomology of
\mathscr{F}^{\infty}
.
Since
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{X}

[
Ma67
, Corollary
VI.1.12]
, we have

(6.2)

H\mathscr{F}^{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathscr{HF}.

Put

(6.3)

\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathscr{F}.

Since
\mathscr{F}
is a complex of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules, we can
equip
\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}
with the differential

d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}
given by

(6.4)

d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}=\overline{\partial}^{X}+d^{\mathscr{F}}.

By Poincaré Lemma and by a theorem of
Malgrange
[
Ma67
, Corollary
VI.1.12]
, we have a quasi-isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-complexes,

(6.5)

\mathscr{F}\to\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}.

Equivalently
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{F},\mathscr{H}\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}
denote the corresponding cohomology sheaves, we
have the canonical isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules,

(6.6)

\mathscr{H}\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{H}\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}.

Also
\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}
is equipped with the filtration
induced by
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
. Let

\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}_{r},r\geq 0
denote the corresponding
spectral sequence of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules.

Then

(6.7)

\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}_{1}=\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{X}}H\mathscr{F}^{\infty}.

Also
\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}_{1}
inherits a differential
d_{1}
, which can be viewed as a holomorphic
structure
\nabla^{H\mathscr{F}^{\infty}\prime\prime}
on
H\mathscr{F}^{\infty}
. Let
\mathscr{H}\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}_{1}
denote the cohomology of
\left(\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}_{1},d_{1}\right)
. Then

(6.8)

\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}_{2}=\mathscr{H}\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}_{1}.

Proposition 6.1
.

The spectral sequence
\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}_{r}

degenerates at
\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}_{2}
, and

F^{1}\mathscr{H}\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}=0
.

For
i\geq 1
,

(6.9)

\mathscr{H}^{i}\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}_{1}=0.

We have the identity of
\mathbf{Z}
-graded coherent sheaves,

(6.10)

\mathscr{H}\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{H}\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}=\mathscr{H}^{0}\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}_{1}.

Proof.

Using the special form of
d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}
in
(
6.4
), the proof is essentially the same as the proof of
Theorem
5.7
. It is left to the reader.
∎

6.2.
Three categories

Let
X
be a compact complex manifold. If
\mathscr{E}=\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right)
is an antiholomorphic superconnection on
X
,
put

(6.11)

\mathscr{E}_{X}=C^{\infty}\left(X,E\right).

Let
\mathscr{E}=\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right),\underline{\mathscr{E}}=\left(\underline{E},A^{\underline{E}\prime\prime}\right)
be
antiholomorphic superconnections on
X
. Let

\mathrm{Hom}\left(E,\underline{E}\right)
be the vector bundle of

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
-morphisms from
E
into

\underline{E}
. Then
\mathrm{Hom}\left(E,\underline{E}\right)
is a

\mathbf{Z}
-graded vector bundle. If
k\in\mathbf{Z}
,

\mathrm{Hom}^{k}\left(E,\underline{E}\right)
consists of the morphisms
that increase the degree by
k
. Then
\mathrm{Hom}\left(E,\underline{E}\right)
is also
equipped with the induced antiholomorphic superconnection

A^{\mathrm{Hom}\left(E,\underline{E}\right)\prime\prime}
. We use the
notation
\mathrm{Hom}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)=\left(\mathrm{Hom}\left(E,\underline{E}\right),A^{\mathrm{Hom}\left(E,\underline{E}\right)\prime\prime}\right)
.

Following
[
Ke06
, Subsection 2.2]
, we define
\mathrm{B}_{\rm dg}(X)
to be the
\mathrm{dg}
-category whose objects are the

\mathscr{E}_{X}
, and the morphisms
\mathrm{Hom}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)_{X}
.

Let

\mathrm{B}(X)=Z^{0}\left(\mathrm{B}\left(X\right)\right)
be the
0
-cycle
category associated with
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dg}}(X)
. Its objects coincide with
the objects of
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dg}}(X)
, and if

\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}
are taken as before, its
morphisms are given by

(6.12)

Z^{0}\mathrm{Hom}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)_{X}=\left\{\phi\in\mathrm{Hom}^{0}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)_{X},A^{\mathrm{Hom}\left(E,\underline{E}\right)\prime\prime}\phi=0\right\}.

Also
\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}
are said to be isomorphic
if there exists
\phi\in Z^{0}\mathrm{Hom}\left(\mathscr{E}_{X},\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{X}\right),\psi\in Z^{0}\mathrm{Hom}\left(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{X},\mathscr{E}_{X}\right)
such that

(6.13)

\displaystyle\psi\phi=1_{\mathscr{E}_{X}},

\displaystyle\phi\psi=1_{\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{X}}.

Let

\underline{\mathrm{B}}(X)
be the homotopy category associated
with

\mathrm{B}(X)
. Its objects coincide with
the objects of
\mathrm{B}(X)
, and the morphisms are given by
the morphisms of
H^{0}\mathrm{Hom}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)_{X}
, the cohomology group of
degree
0
associated with the complex
\left(\mathrm{Hom}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)_{X},A^{\mathrm{Hom}\left(E,\underline{E}\right)\prime\prime}\right)
.

The aim of this section is to establish an equivalence of categories between
\underline{\mathrm{B}}(X)
and
{\rm D_{\mathrm{coh}}^{b}}(X)
. The proof
consists of the following three steps:

(1)

Construct an essentially surjective functor
F_{X}:\mathrm{B}(X)\to{\rm D_{\mathrm{coh}}^{b}}(X)
.

(2)

Prove that the above functor factors though a functor

\underline{F}_{X}:\underline{\mathrm{B}}(X)\to{\rm D_{\mathrm{coh}}^{b}}(X)
.

(3)

Show that
\underline{F}_{X}
is fully faithful.

6.3.
Essential surjectivity

Let
\mathscr{E}=\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right)
be an antiholomorphic
superconnection. By Theorem

5.7
,

\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
. We obtain this way a
functor

F_{X}:\mathrm{B}\left(X\right)\to\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
.

Theorem 6.2
.

The functor
F_{X}
is essentially
surjective, i.e., if
\mathscr{F}\in{\rm D^{b}_{\rm coh}}(X)
, there is
\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{B}(X)

and an isomorphism
F_{X}\left(\mathscr{E}\right)\simeq\mathscr{F}
in
{\rm D^{b}_{\rm coh}}(X)
.

Proof.

The proof of our theorem is divided into the next four steps.
∎

The following result is established by Illusie in
[
Col71
, Proposition
II.2.3.2]
. We give a proof for completeness.

Proposition 6.3
.

There exists a bounded complex of finite
dimensional complex smooth vector bundles
\left(Q,d^{Q}\right)

and a quasi-isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-complexes

\phi_{0}:\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{X}Q\to\mathscr{F}^{\infty}
.

Proof.

We may and we will assume that for
i<0
,
\mathscr{F}^{i}=0
. Put

(6.14)

k=\sup\left\{i\geq 0,\mathscr{H}^{i}\mathscr{F}\neq 0\right\}.

We will show our result by induction on
k
, while also proving that
we may take
Q^{i}=0
for
i>k
.

\bullet

The case
k=0

Assume first that
k=0
. Since
\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{F}
is a
coherent sheaf, if
x\in X
, there exist an open neighborhood
U

of
x
, a bounded holomorphic complex

R_{U}
of trivial holomorphic vector bundles in nonpositive
degree on
U
, and a holomorphic morphism

r_{U}:\mathcal{O}_{U}R_{U}^{0}\to\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{F}|_{U}
such that we have the exact sequence of

\mathcal{O}_{U}
-modules,

(6.15)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{U}R_{U}^{-\ell_{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ldots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{U}R_{U}^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{U}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{F}|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Since
\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}
is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{U}
, we get the exact sequence of

\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}
-modules

(6.16)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}R_{U}^{-\ell_{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ldots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}R_{U}^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{U}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{F}^{\infty}|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Since
X
is compact, there is a finite cover
\mathcal{U}
of
X
by such

U
. Set

(6.17)

\displaystyle\ell=\sup_{U\in\mathcal{U}}\ell_{U}.

We will establish our result by induction on
\ell
. If
\ell=0
, then
\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{F}
is locally
free, i.e., there is a smooth holomorphic bundle
F
on
X
such that

\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{F}=\mathcal{O}_{X}F
. We consider
Q=F
as a trivial
complex concentrated in degree
0
and
r_{0}:\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{X}Q\to\mathscr{F}^{\infty}
to be the canonical injection.

Assume now that
\ell\geq 1
, and that our proposition holds if
the corresponding length defined in (
6.17
) is
\leq\ell-1
.
From the exact sequence (
6.16
), we get a morphism of
complexes
\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}R_{U}\to\mathscr{F}^{\infty}|_{U}
.
Put

(6.18)

\mathscr{C}_{U}=\mathrm{cone}\left(\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}R_{U},\mathscr{F}^{\infty}|_{U}\right).

Since
k=0
,

\mathscr{C}_{U}
is exact. Observe that
\mathscr{C}_{U}
is just
the complex

(6.19)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}R_{U}^{-\ell_{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ldots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}R_{U}^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{U}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{F}^{0,\infty}|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{F}^{1,\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
.

Since
R^{0}_{U}
is trivial on
U
, we can extend
R^{0}_{U}
to a trivial vector bundle on
X
. Set

(6.20)

\displaystyle R=\bigoplus_{U\in\mathcal{U}}R^{0}_{U}.

Then,
R
is a trivial vector bundle on
X
, which we
identify with a trivial complex in
degree
0
. Also we have an obvious morphism of complexes

R|_{U}\to R_{U}
.

Let
\left(\varphi_{U}|_{U\in\mathcal{U}}\right)
be a smooth partition of unity
subordinated to
\mathcal{U}
. Put

(6.21)

r=\sum_{U\in\mathcal{U}}\varphi_{U}r_{U}.

Then
r
defines a
morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-complexes

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}R\to\mathscr{F}^{\infty}
. Moreover, for
any
U
,
r
induces surjections
r|_{U}:\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}R\to\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{F}^{\infty}|_{U}
.

We claim that
r|_{U}
lifts to a smooth
morphism of complexes
R|_{U}\to R_{U}

such that the following diagram commutes:

(6.22)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 14.9601pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 38.9601pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 57.26929pt\raise-15.33333pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{r_{U}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern Let us prove this claim. Since
\mathscr{F}^{\infty}|_{U}
is a subcomplex of
\mathscr{C}_{U}
, from the morphism
r|_{U}:\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}R\to\mathscr{F}^{\infty}|_{U}
, we get a corresponding
morphism
s_{U}:\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{U}R\to\mathscr{C}_{U}
, which is
also clear by (
6.19
).

Since
\mathscr{C}_{U}
is exact and
since
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}R
is free and concentrated
in degree
0
, by
[
BGS88c
, Lemma
3.3]
, there is a null-homotopy

h_{U}:\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}R^{\scriptsize\bullet}\to\mathscr{C}_{U}^{\scriptsize\bullet-1}
such that

(6.23)

s_{U}=d^{\mathscr{C}_{U}}h_{U}.

Inspection of (
6.19
) shows that
h_{U}
provides the
desired lift in (
6.22
).

Since
r_{U}
is a quasi-isomorphism,
by (
6.22
) and
[
KaS90
, Proposition 1.4.4]
, we
get a quasi-isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}
-complexes,

(6.24)

\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{U}{\rm cone}(R|_{U},R_{U})\to{\rm cone}(\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{U}R,\mathscr{F}^{\infty}|_{U}).

We use the notation
\mathscr{R}_{U},\mathscr{R}|_{U}
to designate the sheaves
\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{U}R_{U},\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{U}R
.
By proceeding as in (
6.19
), we can rewrite
(
6.24
) as a
quasi-isomorphism of complexes of
\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{U}
-modules,

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{R}_{U}^{-\ell_{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{R}^{-1}_{U}\oplus\mathscr{R}|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{\mathscr{R}_{U}^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{R}|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{F}^{0,\infty}|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{F}^{1,\infty}|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots,}

so that the corresponding bicomplex is exact.
By construction, the cohomology of the second row in
(
6.3
) is concentrated in degree
-1
, and will be denoted

H^{-1}
. This forces
\gamma

to be a surjective morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\infty}
-modules.
Since
\mathscr{R}_{U}
and
\mathscr{R}
are free, for any

,

\gamma_{x}
is surjective, and
\ker\gamma
is a
sheaf defined by a smooth vector bundle
K_{U}
on

U
. Since

is contractible,
K_{U}
is a trivial vector bundle, so that the complex

(6.29)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{R}_{U}^{-\ell_{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{R}_{U}^{-2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ker\gamma\to 0}

is a resolution of
H^{-1}
. Since here
\ell
is replaced here by

\ell-1
, by our induction argument, there exists a bounded complex of finite
dimensional complex vector bundles
\left(Q^{\prime},d^{Q^{\prime}}\right)

with
Q^{\prime i}=0
if

, such that if
\mathscr{Q}^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{X}Q^{\prime}
, we have a quasi-isomorphism of

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-complexes,

(6.30)

\mathscr{Q}^{\prime}\to{\rm cone}(\mathscr{R},\mathscr{F}^{\infty}).

By (
4.28
), we have a canonical morphism
\rm{cone}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(\mathscr{R},\mathscr{F}^{\infty})\to\mathscr{R}^{\scriptsize\bullet+1}
.
We obtain a tautological diagram

(6.31)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 10.34029pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.34029pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{Q}^{\prime\scriptsize\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.34029pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.38885pt\raise-24.7925pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 34.34029pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm cone}^{\scriptsize\bullet}(\mathscr{R},\mathscr{F}^{\infty})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.8195pt\raise-24.27777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.74005pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 51.38885pt\raise-31.74005pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet+1}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Since the horizontal
morphism in (

) is a quasi-isomorphism, by

KaS90
, Proposition

, we get a

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-complexes

{\rm cone}(\mathscr{Q}^{\prime\scriptsize\bullet},\mathscr{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet+1})\to{\rm cone}({\rm cone}^{\scriptsize\bullet}(\mathscr{R},\mathscr{F}^{\infty}),\mathscr{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet+1}).

Put

(6.33)

\displaystyle Q^{\scriptsize\bullet}=\mathrm{cone}\left(Q^{\prime\scriptsize\bullet-1},R^{\scriptsize\bullet}\right),

\displaystyle\mathscr{Q}^{\scriptsize\bullet}={\rm cone}(\mathscr{Q}^{\prime\scriptscriptstyle\bullet-1},\mathscr{R}^{\scriptsize\bullet}),

so that
\mathscr{Q}=\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{X}Q
. Observe
that for
i>0
,
Q^{i}=0
.

By
[
KaS90
, Proposition 1.4.4
(TR3)]
, we have a homotopy equivalence of
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-complexes

(6.34)

{\rm cone}({\rm cone}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet-1}(\mathscr{R},\mathscr{F}^{\infty}),\mathscr{R}^{\scriptsize\bullet})\to\mathscr{F}^{\infty}.

By (
6.32
)–(
6.34
), we get a quasi-isomorphism,

(6.35)

\mathscr{Q}\to\mathscr{F}^{\infty},

which completes the proof of our theorem when
k=0
.

\bullet

The case
k\geq 1

Assume that

and that our theorem holds for

. Consider the truncated complex

Then

is a subcomplex of

.
The cohomology of

. By

of complex

for

, and that

\mathscr{Q}_{1}=\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{X}Q_{1}

\mathscr{Q}_{1}\to\tau_{\leq k-1}\mathscr{F}^{\infty}.

By composition with the inclusion

\mathscr{Q}_{1}\to\mathscr{F}^{\infty}.

The cohomology of

.

Let

be the differential of
\mathrm{cone(\mathscr{Q}_{1}},\mathscr{F}^{\infty})
. Consider the truncated complex

\tau_{\geq k}{\rm cone}(\mathscr{Q}_{1},\mathscr{F}^{\infty}):0\to{\rm cone}^{k}(\mathscr{Q}_{1},\mathscr{F}^{\infty})/d\mathrm{cone}^{k-1}(\mathscr{Q}_{1},\mathscr{F}^{\infty})\to{\rm cone}^{k+1}\to\ldots

By our induction hypothesis, there exists a smooth complex of vector bundles

such that

for

, and that if

, we have a quasi-isomorphism

By proceeding as in (

), (

) the morphism

) lifts to a quasi-isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathscr{Q}_{2}\to{\rm cone}(\mathscr{Q}_{1},\mathscr{F}^{\infty}).

Put

\displaystyle Q=\mathrm{cone}\left(Q_{2}^{\scriptsize\bullet-1},Q_{1}^{\scriptsize\bullet}\right),

\displaystyle\mathscr{Q}=\mathrm{cone}\left(\mathscr{Q}_{2}^{\scriptsize\bullet-1},\mathscr{Q}_{1}^{\scriptsize\bullet}\right),

so that
\mathscr{Q}=\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{X}Q
. Then
\mathscr{Q}^{i}=0

.
By proceeding as in (

)-(

), from (

), we get

\displaystyle\mathscr{Q}\to\mathscr{F}^{\infty},

which completes the proof of our proposition.
∎

We take
Q,\phi_{0}
as in Proposition

6.3
.
Put

(6.44)

\mathscr{C}=\mathrm{cone}\left(\mathscr{Q},\mathscr{F}^{\infty}\right).

As in
(

)–(

), we have the identity

(6.45)

\mathscr{C}^{\scriptsize\bullet}=\mathscr{Q}^{\scriptsize\bullet+1}\oplus\mathscr{F}^{\infty\scriptsize\bullet},

the differential
d^{\mathscr{C}}
is given by

(6.46)

d^{\mathscr{C}}=\begin{bmatrix}d^{\mathscr{Q}}&0\\
\phi_{0}(-1)^{\rm deg}&d^{\mathscr{F}^{\infty}}\end{bmatrix},

and we have the exact sequence of complexes,

(6.47)

0\to\mathscr{F}^{\infty\scriptsize\bullet}\to\mathscr{C}^{\scriptsize\bullet}\to\mathscr{Q}^{\scriptsize\bullet+1}\to 0.

Since
\phi_{0}
is a quasi-isomorphism,
\mathscr{C}
is exact.

As in (
6.3
), if
\mathscr{A}
is a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-module, we use the notation

(6.48)

\overline{\mathscr{A}}=\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathscr{A}.

Put

(6.49)

\overline{Q}=\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}Q.

Then
\overline{\mathscr{Q}}
is the
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-module of smooth sections of the vector bundle
\overline{Q}
.

Since
\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{X}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\right)
is flat over

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
,

(6.50)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}:\overline{\mathscr{Q}}\to\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}

is still a quasi-isomorphism, and we have the exact sequence

(6.51)

0\to\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty\scriptsize\bullet}\to\overline{\mathscr{C}}^{\scriptsize\bullet}\to\overline{\mathscr{Q}}^{\scriptsize\bullet+1}\to 0.

Also
\overline{\mathscr{C}}
is exact.

Put

(6.52)

\mathscr{R}=\mathrm{Hom}\left(\overline{\mathscr{Q}}^{\scriptsize\bullet+1},\overline{\mathscr{C}}^{\scriptsize\bullet}\right).

Let
d^{\mathscr{R}}
be the differential on
\mathscr{R}
which comes
from
d^{Q},d^{C}
. Also the
supercommutator with

is a differential on
\mathrm{End}\left(\overline{\mathscr{C}}\right)
.

Let
A\in\mathrm{End}\left(\overline{\mathscr{C}}\right)
be a lower
triangular. We
write
A
as a matrix with respect to the splitting (
6.45
),

(6.53)

A=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha&0\\
\beta&\gamma\end{bmatrix}.

Put

(6.54)

\underline{A}=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha\\
\beta\end{bmatrix}.

Then
\underline{A}\in\mathscr{R}
.

Proposition 6.4
.

The following identity holds:

(6.55)

\underline{\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}},A\right]}=d^{\mathscr{R}}\underline{A}-\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}\left(A\right)}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\gamma\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}\end{bmatrix}.

Proof.

We only need to establish (
6.55
) when
\alpha=0,\beta=0
, which
is easy.
∎

As before, we denote by

\mathscr{R}_{X}
the global
sections of
\mathscr{R}
on
X
.

Proposition 6.5
.

The complexes
\mathscr{R}
and
\mathscr{R}_{X}
are
exact.

Proof.

We know that
\overline{\mathscr{C}}
is exact. Also

\overline{\mathscr{Q}}
is locally free. It is now easy to
see that
\mathscr{R}
is exact. Since
\mathscr{R}
is soft, the functor
\mathscr{R}\to\mathscr{R}_{X}
is exact, and we obtain the corresponding
result for
\mathscr{R}_{X}
.
∎

Remark 6.6
.

For a related argument, we refer to

[
St20
,
Tag
0647
]
.

We will establish a fundamental result of Block
[
Bl10
, Lemma
4.6]
.

Theorem 6.7
.

Given
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
, there exists
\mathscr{E}=\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right)\in{\rm B}(X)
and a morphism of

\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{X}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\right)
-modules

\phi:\mathscr{E}\to\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}
, which is a quasi-isomorphism of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-complexes, and induces a quasi-isomorphism of

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-complexes

\left(D,\overline{v}_{0}\right)\to\mathscr{F}^{\infty}
.
In particular,
\mathscr{E}
and
\mathscr{F}
are isomorphic in
{\rm D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}(X)
.

Proof.

We use the notation introduced before.
We will
reformulate our problem as being the construction of a generalized
antiholomorphic superconnection

d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}
on
\overline{\mathscr{C}}
which will have the structure

(6.56)

d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}=\begin{bmatrix}A^{\overline{E}\prime\prime}&0\\
\phi(-1)^{\rm deg}&d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}\end{bmatrix}.

such that if

(6.57)

d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}=\sum_{k\in\mathbf{N}}d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{k}

is the canonical expansion of
d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}
with
respect to
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
, then

(6.58)

d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{0}=d^{\mathscr{C}}.

By (
6.4
), for
k\geq 2
,

(6.59)

d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}_{k}=0.

By (
6.56
), we get

(6.60)

d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{k}=\begin{bmatrix}v_{k}&0\\
\phi_{k}(-1)^{\rm deg}&d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}_{k}\end{bmatrix}.

In particular, for
k=0
, we get

(6.61)

\displaystyle v_{0}=d^{Q},

\displaystyle d_{0}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}=d^{\mathscr{F}^{\infty}}.

For
i\geq 0
, put

(6.62)

d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i}=\sum_{k=0}^{i}d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{k}.

We will use a similar notation for other related expressions.
We will construct
d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{i}
by induction so
that for
i\geq 1
,

(6.63)

\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i}\right]^{2}_{\leq i}=0.

For
i=0
, we take
d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{0}=d^{\mathscr{C}}
. For
i\geq 0
, we will show how to
construct
d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i+1}
from
d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i}
. We have the
identity

(6.64)

d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i+1}=d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i}+d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{i+1}.

Then

(6.65)

\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i+1}\right]^{2}_{\leq i+1}=\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i},d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{i+1}\right]_{\leq i+1}+\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i}\right]^{2}_{\leq i+1}.

Clearly,

(6.66)

\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i},d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{i+1}\right]_{\leq i+1}=\left[d^{\mathscr{C}},d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{i+1}\right].

For (
6.65
) to vanish, using (
6.66
), we should have

(6.67)

\left[d^{\mathscr{C}},d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{i+1}\right]+\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i}\right]^{2}_{\leq i+1}=0.

By (
6.63
), (
6.67
) can also be written in the form

(6.68)

\left[d^{\mathscr{C}},d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{i+1}\right]+\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i}\right]^{2}_{i+1}=0.

Since
d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty},2}=0
18
18

18

By (
6.59
), the expansion of

d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}
terminates at

k=1
. Here, this fact will be ignored.
, we have the identity

(6.69)

\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}_{\leq i+1}\right]^{2}_{\leq i+1}=0.

By (
6.68
), (
6.69
), we deduce that

(6.70)

\left[d^{\mathscr{F}^{\infty}},d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}_{i+1}\right]+\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}_{\leq i}\right]^{2}_{i+1}=0.

First, we solve (
6.68
) for
i=0
. Namely, we need to find

d_{1}^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}
such that

(6.71)

\left[d^{\mathscr{C}},d_{1}^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}\right]=0.

By Proposition
6.4
, equation (
6.71
) can be written in the
form

(6.72)

d^{\mathscr{R}}\underline{d_{1}^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}}+\begin{bmatrix}0\\
d_{1}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}\end{bmatrix}=0.

Recall that by (
6.61
),
v_{0}=d^{Q}
. Then

(6.73)

d^{\mathscr{R}}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
d_{1}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
d_{0}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}_{1}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}-d_{1}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}v_{0}\end{bmatrix}.

Since
\left[d_{0}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}},d_{1}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}\right]=0
, and
d_{0}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}\phi_{0}=\phi_{0}v_{0}
, and since
v_{0}
is odd, by (
6.73
), we get

(6.74)

d^{\mathscr{R}}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
d_{1}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}\end{bmatrix}=0.

Let

\nabla^{Q\prime\prime}
be an antiholomorphic
connection on
Q
.
Put

(6.75)

\displaystyle e_{1}^{\mathscr{R}}=\underline{d_{1}^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}}-\begin{bmatrix}\nabla^{Q\prime\prime}\\
0\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\mathscr{R}}=d^{\mathscr{R}}\begin{bmatrix}\nabla^{Q\prime\prime}\\
0\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}0\\
d_{1}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}\end{bmatrix}.

An elementary computation shows that

(6.76)

f_{1}^{\mathscr{R}}=\begin{bmatrix}\nabla^{Q\prime\prime}v_{0}\\
\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}\nabla^{Q\prime\prime}+d_{1}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}\end{bmatrix}.

Differentiation on
X
having disappeared,
e_{1}^{\mathscr{R}},f_{1}^{\mathscr{R}}
lie
in
\mathscr{R}_{X}
.

By (
6.74
), (
6.75
), solving equation
(
6.72
) is equivalent to finding
e_{1}^{\mathscr{R}}\in\mathscr{R}_{X}
such that

(6.77)

d^{\mathscr{R}}e_{1}^{\mathscr{R}}+f_{1}^{\mathscr{R}}=0.

By (
6.74
), (
6.75
), we get

(6.78)

d^{\mathscr{R}}f_{1}^{\mathscr{R}}=0.

By Proposition
6.5
, the complex
\mathscr{R}_{X}
is exact. Using (
6.78
), there is a solution

e_{1}^{\mathscr{R}}
to (
6.77
), which gives the existence of a solution
for (
6.71
).

Now we assume that
i\geq 1
. We
will still find a solution for (
6.68
).
By Proposition
6.4
, we get

(6.79)

\underline{\left[d^{\mathscr{C}},d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{i+1}\right]}=d^{\mathscr{R}}\underline{d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{i+1}}+\begin{bmatrix}0\\
d_{i+1}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}\end{bmatrix}.

By (
6.68
), (
6.79
), we obtain,

(6.80)

d^{\mathscr{R}}\underline{d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{i+1}}+\underline{\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i}\right]^{2}_{i+1}}+\begin{bmatrix}0\\
d_{i+1}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}\end{bmatrix}=0.

Using (
6.63
), we get

(6.81)

\left[d^{\mathscr{C}},\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i}\right]^{2}_{i+1}\right]=\left[d^{\mathscr{C}},\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i}\right]^{2}\right]_{i+1}=\left[d^{\mathscr{C}}_{\leq i},d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}},2}_{\leq i}\right]_{i+1}=0.

By Proposition
6.4
and by (
6.81
), we get

(6.82)

d^{\mathscr{R}}\underline{\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i}\right]^{2}_{i+1}}-\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}_{\leq i}\right]^{2}_{i+1}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}\end{bmatrix}=0.

An easy computation shows that

(6.83)

d^{\mathscr{R}}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}_{i+1}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
d^{\mathscr{F}^{\infty}}d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}_{i+1}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}-d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}_{i+1}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}v_{0}\end{bmatrix}.

Since
\phi_{0}
is a morphism of complexes, and since
v_{0}
is odd, using
(
6.70
), we can rewrite (
6.83
) in the form

(6.84)

d^{\mathscr{R}}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
d^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}_{i+1}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}\end{bmatrix}=-\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\left[d_{\leq i}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty},2}\right]_{i+1}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}\end{bmatrix}

By combining (
6.82
) and (
6.84
), we get

(6.85)

d^{\mathscr{R}}\left[\underline{\left[d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{\leq i}\right]^{2}_{i+1}}+\begin{bmatrix}0\\
d_{i+1}^{\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}}\phi_{0}\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}}\end{bmatrix}\right]=0.

Since the complex
\mathscr{R}_{X}
is exact, using
(
6.85
), equation
(
6.80
) for
\underline{d^{\overline{\mathscr{C}}}_{i+1}}
can be
solved.

In summary, we have constructed
\mathscr{E}=(E,A^{E\prime\prime})\in{\rm B}(X)
and a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-complexes

\phi:\mathscr{E}\to\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}
. Therefore

\phi
induces a morphism

(6.86)

\phi:\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{H}\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}.

By construction,
\phi
induces an isomorphism of

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-modules,

(6.87)

HD\simeq H\mathscr{F}^{\infty}.

Both sheaves in (
6.87
) are equipped with antiholomorphic connections, which
necessarily correspond by
\phi
. By combining equation
(
5.38
) in Theorem

5.7
and equation (
6.10
) in Proposition

6.1
, we deduce that in (
6.86
),
\phi
is an
isomorphism. The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

6.4.
The homotopy category

Let
\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}

be two objects in
\mathrm{B}(X)
. Note that
\mathrm{Hom}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)
is an object in
\mathrm{B}\left(X\right)
.
Recall that

Z^{0}\mathrm{Hom}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)_{X}
was defined in (
6.12
). A morphism
\phi\in Z^{0}\mathrm{Hom}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)_{X}
is said to be null-homotopic if there exists
\psi\in\mathrm{Hom}^{-1}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)_{X}
such that

(6.88)

\phi=A^{\mathrm{Hom}\left(E,\underline{E}\right)\prime\prime}\psi.

Also
\phi\in Z^{0}\mathrm{Hom}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)_{X}
is said to be a homotopy equivalence if there is
\psi\in Z^{0}\mathrm{Hom}\left(\underline{\mathscr{E}},\mathscr{E}\right)_{X}
such that
\psi\phi-1|_{\mathscr{E}}
is null-homotopic
in
Z^{0}\mathrm{Hom}\left(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{E}\right)_{X}
, and

\phi\psi-1|_{\underline{\mathscr{E}}}
is null-homotopic in

Z^{0}\mathrm{Hom}\left(\underline{\mathscr{E}},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)_{X}
.

If
\phi\in Z^{0}\mathrm{Hom}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)_{X}
is null-homotopic, the image of this morphism in

\mathrm{C}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}\left(X\right)
is also
null-homotopic. In particular, the functor
F_{X}:{\rm B}(X)\to{\rm D_{\mathrm{coh}}^{b}}(X)
factorizes
through a functor

\underline{F}_{X}:\underline{{\rm B}}(X)\to{\rm D_{\mathrm{coh}}^{b}}(X)
.
By Theorem
6.2
,
\underline{F}_{X}
is essentially surjective.

Proposition 6.8
.

If
\phi\in Z^{0}\mathrm{Hom}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)_{X}
, the
following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

\phi
is a homotopy equivalence,

(2)

\phi
induces a quasi-isomorphism
\mathscr{E}\to\underline{\mathscr{E}}
,

(3)

For any
x\in X
,
\phi
induces a quasi-isomorphism
D_{x}\to\underline{D}_{x}
.

(4)

\phi
induces a quasi-isomorphism
\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{X}\left(D\right)\to\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{X}\left(\underline{D}\right)
.

(5)

\phi
induces a homotopy equivalence
C^{\infty}(X,D)\to C^{\infty}(X,\underline{D})
.

Proof.

This is an easy consequence of Theorem
5.10
and of the
construction of
\mathrm{cone}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)
. ∎

6.5.
An equivalence of categories

We have a fundamental theorem of Block
[
Bl10
, Theorem 4.3]
.

Theorem 6.9
.

The functor

\underline{F}_{X}:\underline{\mathrm{B}}(X)\to{\rm D}_{\rm coh}^{\rm b}(X)
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

Proof.

Since
\underline{F}_{X}
is essentially surjective, we only need
to show that
\underline{F}_{X}
is fully faithful. Equivalently, if
\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\in\underline{\mathrm{B}}\left(X\right)
,
we have to show that the corresponding map

\mathrm{Hom}_{\underline{\rm B}(X)}(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}})\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\rm D_{\mathrm{coh}}^{b}(X)}(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}})
is bijective.

Let us prove surjectivity. A morphism in
\mathrm{Hom}_{\rm D_{\mathrm{coh}}^{b}(X)}(\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}})
is represented
by the diagram of morphisms of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-complexes,

6.7
, there exists

\underline{\underline{\mathscr{E}}}\in{\rm B}(X)
and a quasi-isomorphism

\underline{\underline{\mathscr{E}}}\to\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}
. We can replace (
6.5
) by

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.40279pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.40279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{E}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 30.40279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underline{\underline{\mathscr{E}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.40279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.90625pt\raise 6.03473pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.67363pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{q.i.}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.40279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.40279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underline{\mathscr{E}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

the morphisms in (
6.5
) being morphisms of
{\rm B}(X)
. By
Proposition
6.8
, the
first morphism in (
6.5
) is a
homotopy equivalence. Using its homotopic inverse, we get the
desired morphism from
\mathscr{E}\to\underline{\mathscr{E}}
.

We now prove injectivity. Let
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}:\mathscr{E}\to\underline{\mathscr{E}}
be two morphisms in

{\rm B}(X)
, whose images coincide in
{\rm D_{\mathrm{coh}}^{b}}(X)
. By definition,
there is
\mathscr{F}\in{\rm D_{\mathrm{coh}}^{b}}(X)
and a quasi-isomorphism

\phi:\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{E}
,
such that
\phi_{1}\phi=\phi_{2}\phi
up to homotopy in

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{C^{b}_{coh}\left(X\right)}}\left(\mathscr{F},\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)
.

Using again Theorem
6.7
, there exists

\underline{\underline{\mathscr{E}}}\in\mathrm{B}\left(X\right)
and a
quasi-isomorphism
\underline{\underline{\mathscr{E}}}\to\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}
. Therefore, in the above
construction, we may as well replace
\mathscr{F}
by

\underline{\underline{\mathscr{E}}}
, so that
\phi
is a
quasi-isomorphism
\underline{\underline{\mathscr{E}}}\to\mathscr{E}
, and
\phi_{1}\phi=\phi_{2}\phi
up to homotopy in

Z^{0}\mathrm{Hom}\left(\mathscr{E},\underline{\underline{\mathscr{E}}}\right)_{X}
. Since
\phi
is a quasi-isomorphism, this is a
homotopy equivalence, so that

\phi_{1}=\phi_{2}\,\mathrm{in}\,\underline{\mathrm{B}}\left(X\right)
, which completes the proof of our theorem.
∎

6.6.
Compatibility with pull-backs

We make the same assumptions as in Subsection
5.7

and we use the corresponding notation.
By the results of that Subsection, we have defined a
pull-back functor
f^{*}_{b}:\mathrm{B}\left(Y\right)\to\mathrm{B}\left(X\right)
. Recall
that the definition of the left-derived functor

Lf^{*}:\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(Y\right)\to\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
was given in
Subsection
3.2
.

Proposition 6.10
.

The following diagram commutes up to isomorphism:

(6.95)

\textstyle{\underline{\mathrm{B}}\left(Y\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underline{F}_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{f_{b}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(Y\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Lf^{*}}
\textstyle{\underline{\mathrm{B}}\left(X\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underline{F}_{X}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)}

Proof.

If
\mathscr{E}
is an object in

\underline{\mathrm{B}}\left(Y\right)
, if

\mathcal{E}=\underline{F}_{Y}\left(\mathscr{E}\right)
, then
\mathcal{E}
is
an object in

\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(Y\right)
. Also
f^{*}\mathcal{E}
is given by
(
3.4
). Since
\mathcal{E}
is a bounded
complex of flat
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules, by (
3.5
), we have

(6.96)

\displaystyle Lf^{*}\mathcal{E}=f^{*}\mathcal{E}.

By Proposition
5.11
, there is a morphism of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-complexes
\mu:f^{*}\mathcal{E}\to f^{*}_{b}\mathscr{E}
. Using (
3.5
),
to establish our proposition, we only need to prove that if
x\in X
, the morphism
\mu
induces a
quasi-isomorphism of the corresponding stalks at
x
.

Take
x\in X,y=f\left(x\right)\in Y
. By Theorem
5.3
, to establish our theorem, we may and we
will assume that on a small open neighborhood
V
of
y
,
D
is a
trivial holomorphic vector bundle,

E=\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}V}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D
, and

A^{E\prime\prime}|_{V}=v+\nabla^{D\prime\prime}
. Put

U=f^{-1}V
.
By proceeding as in the proof of Theorem
5.7
, we get

(6.97)

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}f^{*}\mathcal{E}=\mathscr{H}\left(f^{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D),f^{*}v\right),

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}f^{*}_{b}\mathscr{E}|_{U}=\mathscr{H}\left(\mathcal{O}_{U}f^{*}D,f^{*}v|_{U}\right).

Also note that

(6.98)

f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\otimes_{f^{-1}O_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{X}=\mathcal{O}_{X}.

Since
D
is trivial on
V
, by (
6.98
), we get

(6.99)

f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\left(D\right)\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{X}|_{U}=\mathcal{O}_{X}\left(f^{*}D\right)|_{U}.

By (
6.97
), (
6.99
), we obtain

(6.100)

\mathscr{H}f^{*}\mathcal{E}=\mathscr{H}f_{b}^{*}\mathscr{E}.

This completes the proof that
\mu
is a quasi-isomorphism.
∎

6.7.
Compatibility with tensor products

Let
\mathscr{E},\mathscr{F}
be objects in

\underline{\mathrm{B}}\left(X\right)
. We use the notation of Subsections

3.3
and
5.7
.

Proposition 6.11
.

We have a canonical isomorphism,

(6.101)

\underline{F}_{X}\left(\mathscr{E}\widehat{\otimes}_{b}\mathscr{F}\right)\simeq\underline{F}_{X}\left(\mathscr{E}\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}^{\mathrm{L}}\underline{F}_{X}\left(\mathscr{F}\right)\,\mathrm{in}\,\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right).

Proof.

We will identify
\mathscr{E},\mathscr{F}
with their images by

\underline{F}_{X}
in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
.
Since
\mathscr{E},\mathscr{F}
are flat over

\mathcal{O}_{X}
, by (
3.10
), we get

(6.102)

\mathscr{E}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{E}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathscr{F}.

There is a natural morphism

\phi:\mathscr{E}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{E}\widehat{\otimes}_{b}\mathscr{F}
of objects in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
. We will show that it
is a quasi-isomorphism.

We write
\mathscr{E},\mathscr{F}
in the form,

(6.103)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}=\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right),

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}=\left(F,A^{F\prime\prime}\right).

Let
D_{E},D_{F}
be the diagonal vector bundles associated with

\mathscr{E},\mathscr{F}
, and let
v_{0,E},v_{0,F}
be the corresponding
endomorphisms of
D_{E},D_{F}
. The objects associated with

E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}F
are denoted in the same way. In particular,

(6.104)

\displaystyle D_{E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}F}=D_{E}\widehat{\otimes}D_{F},

\displaystyle v_{0,E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}F}=v_{0,E}\widehat{\otimes}1+1\widehat{\otimes}v_{0,F}.

By Theorem
5.3
, if
U\subset X
is a small open set, after
conjugation, we may assume that

(6.105)

\displaystyle A^{E\prime\prime}|_{U}=v_{0,E}+\nabla^{D_{E}|_{U}\prime\prime},

\displaystyle A^{F\prime\prime}|_{U}=v_{0,F}+\nabla^{D_{F}|_{U}\prime\prime}.

In particular
D_{E}|_{U},D_{F}|_{U}
are holomorphic vector
bundles, so that
D_{E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}F}|_{U}
is also a
holomorphic vector bundle. Let
\nabla^{D_{E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}F}|_{U}\prime\prime}
be the corresponding
holomorphic structure. By (
6.105
), we get

(6.106)

A^{E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}F\prime\prime}|_{U}=v_{0,E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}F}+\nabla^{D_{E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}F}|_{U}\prime\prime}.

By Theorem
5.7
, the morphisms
\left(\mathcal{O}_{U}\left(D_{E}\right),v_{0,E}\right)\to\mathscr{E}|_{U}
,

\left(\mathcal{O}_{U}\left(D_{F}\right),v_{0,F}\right)\to\mathscr{F}|_{U}
are
quasi-isomorphisms. Since
\mathscr{E}|_{U}
and
\mathcal{O}_{U}\left(D_{E}\right)
are flat
over
\mathcal{O}_{U}
, and the same is true for the objects
associated with
F
, the obvious morphism

(6.107)

\left(\mathcal{O}_{U}\left(D_{E}\right),v_{0,E}\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{U}}\left(\mathcal{O}_{U}\left(D_{F}\right),v_{0,F}\right)\to\mathscr{E}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{U}}\mathscr{F}

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Observe that

(6.108)

\left(\mathcal{O}_{U}\left(D_{E}\right),v_{0,E}\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{U}}\left(\mathcal{O}_{U}\left(D_{F}\right),v_{0,F}\right)=\left(\mathcal{O}_{U}\left(D_{E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}F}\right),v_{0,E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}F}\right).

It is now clear that
\phi
is a quasi-isomorphism of objects in

\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
. The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

6.8.
Direct images

Let
Y
be a compact complex manifold, and let
f:X\to Y
be a
holomorphic map.

Let
\mathscr{E}
be an object in

\underline{\mathrm{B}}\left(X\right)
, so that
\underline{F}_{X}\left(\mathscr{E}\right)
is an object in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
. Since
\mathscr{E}
is
a bounded complex of soft
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\infty}
-modules, by
(
3.11
), we get

(6.109)

Rf_{*}\mathscr{E}=f_{*}\mathscr{E}.

Also
f^{*}
maps
\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{Y}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}Y}\right)\right)
to

f_{*}\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{X}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\right)
. The
differential of
f_{*}\mathscr{E}
verifies Leibniz’s rule with
respect to multiplication by
\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}Y}\right)\right)
, so that

f_{*}\mathscr{E}
is a complex of
\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{Y}\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}Y}\right)
-modules.

Proposition 6.12
.

There exists
\underline{\mathscr{E}}=\left(\underline{E},A^{\underline{E}\prime\prime}\right)\in\mathrm{B}\left(Y\right)
and a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}Y}\right)\right)
-modules

\phi:\underline{\mathscr{E}}\to f_{*}\mathscr{E}
, which is also
a quasi-isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-complexes.

Proof.

We use the notation in Subsection
6.1
. In
particular, as in (
6.3
), we get

(6.110)

\overline{f_{*}\mathscr{E}}^{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}Y}\right)\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}f_{*}\mathscr{E}.

Also

\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}Y}\right)\right)
acts on
f_{*}\mathscr{E}
. We get this way a morphism of complexes
\psi:\overline{f_{*}\mathscr{E}}^{\infty}\to f_{*}\mathscr{E}
, which is also a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}Y}\right)\right)
-modules. By (
6.5
), the map
f_{*}\mathscr{E}\to\overline{f_{*}\mathscr{E}}^{\infty}
is a quasi-isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-complexes. These maps fit into the triangle

(6.111)

\textstyle{f_{*}\mathscr{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\overline{f_{*}\mathscr{E}}^{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{f_{*}\mathscr{E}.}

From (
6.111
), we deduce that
\psi
is a quasi-isomorphism.

By a theorem of Grauert
[
GrR84
, Theorem 10.4.6]
,

f_{*}\mathscr{E}
defines an object in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(Y\right)
. By
Theorem
6.7
, there exists an object
\underline{\mathscr{E}}=\left(\underline{E},A^{\underline{E}\prime\prime}\right)
in

\mathrm{B}\left(Y\right)
and a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}Y}\right)\right)
-modules
\rho:\underline{\mathscr{E}}\to\overline{f_{*}\mathscr{E}}^{\infty}
, which is a quasi-isomorphism of

\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-complexes. Then

\phi=\psi\rho:\underline{\mathscr{E}}\to f_{*}\mathscr{E}
is the
quasi-isomorphism we are looking for, which completes our proof. ∎

7.
Antiholomorphic superconnections and generalized metrics

In this Section, if
\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right)
is an
antiholomorphic superconnection, if
E
is equipped with a splitting
so that
E\simeq E_{0}
, if
h
is a generalized metric on
D
, we
define the adjoint superconnection
A^{E_{0}\prime}
of

A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
with respect
to
h
. This is just the obvious analogue of the construction of the
holomorphic part of the Chern connection on a holomorphic Hermitian
vector bundle, which we briefly reviewed in
Subsection
2.4
.

This Section is organized as follows. In Subsection

7.1
, we construct
A^{E_{0}\prime}
as the formal
adjoint of
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
with respect to a
non-degenerate Hermitian form
\theta_{h}
.

In Subsection
7.2
, we conclude the Section by
introducing the curvature of the
superconnection
A^{E_{0}}=A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}+A^{E_{0}\prime}
.

7.1.
