The adjoint of an antiholomorphic superconnection

We make the same assumptions as in Subsection
5.1
and we
use the corresponding notation.

We define the antiautomorphism

\widetilde{}
of

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
as in Subsection
4.4
,
so that equation (
4.33
) holds. Also we define

\mathrm{deg}_{-}

on
\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
as in (
4.38
). If

\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
denote the vector space of smooth forms on

X
, we denote by

\Omega^{-,i}\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
the vector space
of the
\alpha\in\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
such that

\mathrm{deg}_{-}\,\alpha=i
.

Here, we follow
[
B13
, Section 6.6]
. If
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
,
put

(7.1)

\theta\left(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\right)=\frac{i^{n}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}\int_{X}\widetilde{\alpha}\wedge\overline{\alpha^{\prime}}.

One verifies easily that
\theta
defines a non-degenerate Hermitian form on

\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
, and that the

\Omega^{-,i}\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
are mutually orthogonal. If
B\in\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
acts on

\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
by exterior
multiplication, we denote by
B^{{\dagger}}
the adjoint of

B
with respect to
\theta
. An easy computation, which also
follows from
[
B13
, Proposition 6.5.1]
shows that if
f\in T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X
, then

(7.2)

f^{{\dagger}}\wedge=-\overline{f}\wedge

By (
7.2
), we deduce that if
B\in\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
,
B^{{\dagger}}
is just the
‘adjoint’
B^{*}
we
defined in Subsection
4.4
.

Since
\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)
is a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded holomorphic vector bundle on
X
, using
the results of Subsection
5.8
,

\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}E
has the same properties as
E
, i.e., it can be
equipped with an antiholomorphic superconnection

A^{\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}E\prime\prime}
, and it
is also a
\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
-module. If
\alpha\in\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right),s\in C^{\infty}\left(X,\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}E\right)
, the obvious extension of (
5.4
)
holds, i.e.,

(7.3)

A^{\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}E\prime\prime}\left(\alpha s\right)=\left(\overline{\partial}^{X}\alpha\right)s+\left(-1\right)^{\mathrm{deg}\alpha}\alpha A^{\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}E\prime\prime}s.

In the sequel, we will still use the notation
A^{E\prime\prime}
instead of
A^{\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}E\prime\prime}
.

We fix a splitting of
E
as in (
5.2
), (
5.3
),
so that

(7.4)

\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}E\simeq\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}D.

Put

(7.5)

\Omega\left(X,D\right)=C^{\infty}\left(X,\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}D\right).

By (
7.4
), (
7.5
), we deduce that

(7.6)

\Omega\left(X,D\right)=C^{\infty}\left(X,\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}E\right).

We equip

\Omega\left(X,D\right)
with the total degree
associated with the degree
\mathrm{deg}_{-}
on
\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
and with the
degree in
D
. Let

\Omega^{-,i}\left(X,D\right)
be the vector
space of the
s\in\Omega\left(X,D\right)
such that
\mathrm{deg}\,s=i
.

In the sequel, we write
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
instead
of
A^{\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}E_{0}\prime\prime}
.

Definition 7.1
.

Put

(7.7)

\displaystyle E^{*i}_{0}=\bigoplus_{p+q=i}\Lambda^{p}\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\otimes D^{q*},

\displaystyle E_{0}^{*}=\bigoplus_{i=r}^{r^{\prime}}E_{0}^{i*}.

Then

(7.8)

C^{\infty}\left(X,\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}E^{*}_{0}\right)=\Omega\left(X,D^{*}\right).

If
s\in\Omega\left(X,D\right),s^{\prime}\in\Omega\left(X,\overline{D}^{*}\right)
, we can write
s,s^{\prime}
in the form

(7.9)

\displaystyle s=\sum\alpha_{i}r_{i},

\displaystyle s^{\prime}=\sum\beta_{j}t_{j},

with
\alpha_{i},\beta_{j}\in\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
, and

r_{i}\in C^{\infty}\left(X,D\right),t_{j}\in C^{\infty}\left(X,\overline{D}^{*}\right)
.

Definition 7.2
.

Put

(7.10)

\theta\left(s,s^{\prime}\right)=\frac{i^{n}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}\sum\int_{X}\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}\wedge\overline{\beta}_{j}\left\langle r_{i},\overline{t}_{j}\right\rangle.

Note that given
i,j
, the expression in the right-hand side of
(
7.10
) is nonzero if and only if

\mathrm{deg}_{-}\alpha_{i}=\mathrm{deg}_{-}\beta_{j},\mathrm{deg}\,r_{i}=\mathrm{deg}\,t_{j}
, so that

\mathrm{deg}_{-}\alpha_{i}\mathrm{deg}\,r_{i}=\mathrm{deg}_{-}\,\beta_{j}\mathrm{deg}\,t_{j}
.

If
s,s^{\prime}
are given instead by

(7.11)

\displaystyle s=\sum r_{i}\alpha_{i},

\displaystyle s^{\prime}=\sum t_{j}\beta_{j},

from (
7.10
), and from the previous considerations, we
deduce that
\theta\left(s,s^{\prime}\right)
is still given by the right-hand side
of (
7.10
).

If
k
is a
smooth section of
\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
, let
\widetilde{k}
be its
transpose, which is a
section of
\mathrm{End}\left(D^{*}\right)
. It follows from the previous
considerations that the adjoint of
k
with respect to
\theta
is
given by
\overline{\widetilde{k}}
, i.e., it is just the action of
the classical expected adjoint.

We define the smooth bundle
\mathscr{M}^{D}
of generalized metrics
on
D
as in Subsection
4.4
.
As in Subsection
4.4
, we will say that a smooth section

h
of
\mathscr{M}^{D}
is pure if
h=h_{0}
.

Let
h
be a smooth section of
\mathscr{M}^{D}
. Then
h
defines an
invertible
morphism from
\Omega\left(X,D\right)
into

\Omega\left(X,\overline{D}^{*}\right)
.

Definition 7.3
.

If
s,s^{\prime}\in\Omega\left(X,D\right)
, put

(7.12)

\theta_{h}\left(s,s^{\prime}\right)=\theta\left(s,hs^{\prime}\right).

Then
\theta_{h}
is a non-degenerate Hermitian form on

\Omega\left(X,D\right)
, and the
\Omega^{-,i}\left(X,D\right)

are mutually orthogonal with respect to
\theta_{h}
.

Definition 7.4
.

Let

A^{E_{0}\prime}
denote the formal adjoint of
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}

with respect to
\theta_{h}
.

Then
A^{E_{0}\prime}
verifies Leibniz rule when replacing

\overline{\partial}^{X}
by
\partial^{X}
, and it is of degree
-1
. Also

(7.13)

A^{E_{0}\prime,2}=0.

Let
A^{\overline{E}^{*}_{0}\prime}
denote the formal adjoint of

A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
with respect to
\theta
.
Then

(7.14)

A^{E_{0}\prime}=h^{-1}A^{\overline{E}_{0}^{*}\prime}h.

The above constructions are compatible with the conventions in

[
BL95
, Subsections 1 (c) and 1 (d)]
and in
[
B13
, Section
3.5]
. In
[
Q16
, Proposition 2.17]
, Qiang followed the
same lines to define the adjoint
A^{E_{0}\prime}
when
h
is a
pure metric.

Let

\nabla^{D\prime}
be the adjoint of
\nabla^{D\prime\prime}
with
respect to
\theta_{h}
. If
i=0,\mathrm{or}\,i\geq 2
, let
v_{i}^{*}
be the adjoint of
v_{i}

with respect to
\theta_{h}
. By (
5.6
), we obtain

(7.15)

A^{E_{0}\prime}=v_{0}^{*}+\nabla^{D\prime}+\sum_{i\geq 2}v_{i}^{*}.

Recall that

B
was defined in (
5.8
). Its adjoint
B^{*}
is given by

(7.16)

B^{*}=v_{0}^{*}+\sum_{i\geq 2}v_{i}^{*}.

Then equation (
7.15
) can be written in the form

(7.17)

A^{E_{0}\prime}=\nabla^{D\prime}+B^{*}.

Let

\nabla^{D\prime}_{0}
be the adjoint of
\nabla^{D\prime\prime}

when
h
is replaced by the pure metric
h_{0}
. Then there is a
smooth section
\gamma
of total degree
-1
in

\Lambda^{(\geq 2)}\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
such that

(7.18)

\nabla^{D\prime}=\nabla^{D\prime}_{0}+\gamma.

Put

(7.19)

\nabla^{D}_{0}=\nabla^{D\prime\prime}+\nabla^{D\prime}_{0}.

Then

\nabla^{D}_{0}
is a classical unitary connection on
D
with respect
to
h_{0}
19
19

19

In general,

\nabla^{D\prime\prime}
does not define a holomorphic structure on

D
, so that
\nabla^{D}_{0}
is not a Chern connection.
.

The Hermitian metric
h_{0}
on
D
induces a classical Hermitian metric

h^{\det D}
on
\det D
. Let
\nabla^{\det D}
be the connection on

\det D
that is induced by
\nabla^{D}_{0}
. Then
\nabla^{\det D}
is
a unitary connection with respect to
h^{\det D}
. By Theorem

5.9
,
\nabla^{\det D\prime\prime}
is a holomorphic
structure on
\det D
.

Proposition 7.5
.

The connection
\nabla^{\det D}
coincides with the Chern
connection on
\left(\det D,h^{\det D}\right)
.

Proof.

Let
\nabla^{\det D\prime}
be the holomorphic connection on
\det D
that is induced by
\nabla^{D\prime}_{0}
. By construction,

\nabla^{\det D\prime}
is the adjoint of
\nabla^{\det D\prime\prime}
with respect to
h^{\det D}
, which gives our proposition.
∎

7.2.
Curvature

We make the same assumptions as in Subsection
7.1
,
and we use again the superconnection formalism of Quillen

[
Qu85
]
.

Let

A^{E_{0}}
be the superconnection on
E_{0}
,

(7.20)

A^{E_{0}}=A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}+A^{E_{0}\prime}.

Set

(7.21)

\displaystyle\nabla^{D}=\nabla^{D\prime\prime}+\nabla^{D\prime},

\displaystyle C=B+B^{*}.

By (
5.9
), (
7.17
), (
7.20
), and
(
7.21
), we get

(7.22)

A^{E_{0}}=\nabla^{D}+C.

The curvature of
A^{E_{0}}
is given by

(7.23)

A^{E_{0},2}=\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},A^{E_{0}\prime}\right].

Then
A^{E_{0},2}
is a smooth section of

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
of degree

0
. Also, it verifies Bianchi’s identities,

(7.24)

\displaystyle\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},A^{E_{0},2}\right]=0,

\displaystyle\left[A^{E_{0}\prime},A^{E_{0},2}\right]=0.

Proposition 7.6
.

The following identity holds:

(7.25)

\left[A^{E_{0},2}\right]^{*}=A^{E_{0},2}.

Proof.

This is the obvious consequence of the fact that
A^{E_{0},\prime}=\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}\right]^{*}
.
∎

8.
Generalized metrics and Chern character forms

In this Section, given an antiholomorphic superconnection and a
generalized metric, we construct the corresponding Chern character
forms, and we prove that their Bott-Chern class does not depend on
the metric. Also, we show that the Chern character extends to

\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
.

As we explained in the introduction, some results which are proved in
the present Section were already stated by Qiang in

[
Q16
,
Q17
]
. We will refer to them in more detail in the
text.

This Section is organized as follows. In Subsection

8.1
, we construct the Chern character forms, and we
establish their main properties.

In Subsection
8.2
, we give a trivial example
involving Kähler forms, that will be of special importance in
Sections
13
–
16
.

In Subsection
8.3
, we describe the behavior of the
Chern character forms under pull-backs.

In Subsection
8.4
, we evaluate the Chern character
of a tensor product.

In Subsection
8.5
, we consider the case where
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
is locally free.

In Subsection
8.6
, we study the behaviour of
the Chern character form under a suitable scaling of the metric. Also
we show that in degree
(1,1)
, the Chern character is just the first
Chern class of the Knudsen-Mumford determinant line bundle.

In Subsection
8.7
, we evaluate the Chern character of
a cone.

In Subsection
8.8
, using the results of Section
6
,
we show that the Chern character defines a map

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}:\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)\to H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
.

In Subsection
8.9
, we show that
\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}
factors through

K\left(X\right)
, i.e., it induces a map
K\left(X\right)\to H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
.

Finally, in Subsection
8.10
, we introduce spectral
truncations for antiholomorphic superconnections. The results
obtained there will be used in an infinite-dimensional context in
Section
11
.

We make the same assumptions as in Sections
5
and

7
, and we use the corresponding notation.

8.1.
The Chern character forms

We fix a square root of
i=\sqrt{-1}
. Our formulas will not depend on
this choice. Let

\varphi
denote the morphism of

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
that maps
\alpha
to

\left(2i\pi\right)^{-\mathrm{deg}\alpha/2}\alpha
.

Recall that by Proposition
4.3
, there is a well defined
supertrace
\mathrm{Tr_{s}}:\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(E\right)\to\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
, that vanishes on supercommutators.
Here, we may as well view
\mathrm{Tr_{s}}
as a map from

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
to

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
.

Let
\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right)
be an antiholomorphic
superconnection on
X
. We fix splittings as in (
5.2
),
(
5.3
).
Let
h\in\mathscr{M}^{D}
be a generalized metric, and let

A^{E_{0}}
be the associated superconnection defined in
(
7.20
).

Definition 8.1
.

Set

(8.1)

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)=\varphi\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)\right].

Then
\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)
is a smooth form on

X
.

Let

d^{\mathscr{M}^{D}}
denote the de Rham operator on
\mathscr{M}^{D}
. Then
h^{-1}d^{\mathscr{M}^{D}}h
is a
1
-form on
\mathscr{M}^{D}
with values in morphisms in

\left[\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(E_{0}\right)\right]^{0}
that
are self-adjoint with respect to
h
.

Recall that the classical metric
h_{0}
on
D
was defined in
(
4.39
).

We will extend the result of Bott-Chern
[
BoC65
, Proposition 3.28]

described in Proposition
2.3
, and results of Quillen
[
Qu85
, Section 2]
, and
Bismut-Gillet-Soulé
[
BGS88a
, Theorems 1.15 and
1.24]
on superconnection forms, and reestablish a result
proved earlier by Qiang
[
Q16
]
. Some formal
aspects of the proof are closely related to the proof
of Proposition
2.3
.

Theorem 8.2
.

The form
\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)
lies in

\Omega^{(=)}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
, it is closed, and its Bott-Chern
cohomology class does not depend on
h
.
More precisely,
\varphi\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[h^{-1}d^{\mathscr{M}^{D}}h\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)\right]
is a
1
-form on
\mathscr{M}^{D}
with values in
\Omega^{(=)}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
, and
moreover,

(8.2)

d^{\mathscr{M}^{D}}\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)=-\frac{\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}}{2i\pi}\varphi\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[h^{-1}d^{\mathscr{M}^{D}}h\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)\right].

If
g
is a smooth
section of
\mathrm{Aut}^{0}\left(E_{0}\right)
, the Bott-Chern cohomology
class of
\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)
is unchanged when
replacing
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
by
gA^{E_{0}\prime\prime}g^{-1}
. In particular, the Bott-Chern cohomology class of

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)
does not depend on the
splitting in (
5.2
), and depends only
on
A^{E\prime\prime}
.

Finally,

(8.3)

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)^{\leq 2}=\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h_{0}\right)^{\leq 2}.

Proof.

Let

N^{D}\in\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
count the degree in
D
, and
let

N_{-}^{\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)}
count the degree in

\Lambda\left(T_{\mathbf{C}}^{*}X\right)
as the difference of the
antiholomorphic and the holomorphic degrees. Since
A^{E_{0},2}
has total degree
0
,
for
t>0
, we get

(8.4)

e^{tN_{-}^{\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)}}\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)e^{-tN_{-}^{\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)}}=e^{-tN^{D}}\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)e^{tN^{D}}.

Since
e^{-tN^{D}}
is an even operator, and since supertraces vanish
on supercommutators, by (
8.1
), (
8.4
), we get

(8.5)

e^{tN_{-}^{\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)}}\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)=\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right),

which just says that
\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)
is a form in
\Omega^{(=)}\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
.

Using the
Bianchi identities (
7.24
) and the fact that supertraces
vanish on supercommutators, we get

(8.6)

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}^{X}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)\right]=0,

\displaystyle\partial^{X}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)\right]=0,

i.e., the above form is closed.
Since

A^{E_{0},2}
is self-adjoint, it is elementary to verify that

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)
is a real form
20
20

20

In the
proof, we use explicitly the fact that
h
is of degree
0
.
.

We denote by
\left[\,\right]_{+}
the anticommutator. Since

\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)
is even, we get

(8.7)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[h^{-1}d^{\mathscr{M}^{D}}h\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)\right]=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\left[h^{-1}d^{\mathscr{M}^{D}}h,\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)\right]_{+}\right].

The
1
-form
\left[h^{-1}d^{\mathscr{M}^{D}}h,\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)\right]_{+}
takes its values in

h
self-adjoint sections of

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
and is
of degree
0
. By the same considerations as before, we deduce
that the form obtained from (
8.7
) by normalization by
\varphi
lies
in
\Omega^{(=)}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
.

In the sequel, we use (
7.23
) repeatedly. Also, we make
1
-forms on

\mathscr{M}^{D}
to anticommute with odd forms on
X
. Clearly,

(8.8)

\left[d^{\mathscr{M}^{D}},A^{E_{0}\prime}\right]=-\left[A^{E_{0}\prime},h^{-1}d^{\mathscr{M}^{D}}h\right],

so that

(8.9)

d^{\mathscr{M}^{D}}\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},A^{E_{0}\prime}\right]=\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},\left[A^{E_{0}\prime},h^{-1}d^{\mathscr{M}^{D}}h\right]\right].

By (
8.9
), we get

(8.10)

d^{\mathscr{M}^{D}}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)\right]\\
=-\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},\left[A^{E_{0}\prime},h^{-1}d^{\mathscr{M}^{D}}h\right]\right]\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)\right].

Using the Bianchi identities (
7.24
) and (
8.10
),
we get (
8.2
), from which we deduce
that the Bott-Chern cohomology class of
\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)

does not depend on
h
.

Let
g
be a smooth section of
\mathrm{Aut}^{0}\left(E_{0}\right)
, and let
h

be a smooth section of
\mathscr{M}^{D}
. Let
g^{*}
be the adjoint
of
g
with respect to
h
21
21

21

Our definition of

g^{*}
is different from the one in (
4.40
).
. The adjoint of
gA^{E_{0}\prime\prime}g^{-1}
with respect to
h
is given by
g^{*-1}A^{E_{0}\prime}g^{*}
, so that the corresponding curvature is given by

\left[gA^{E_{0}\prime\prime}g^{-1},g^{*-1}A^{E_{0}\prime}g^{*}\right]
. Observe that

(8.11)

\left[gA^{E_{0}\prime\prime}g^{-1},g^{*-1}A^{E_{0}\prime}g^{*}\right]=g\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},\left(g^{*}g\right)^{-1}A^{E_{0}\prime}g^{*}g\right]g^{-1}.

Also
\left(g^{*}g\right)^{-1}A^{E_{0}\prime}g^{*}g
is just the
adjoint of
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
with respect to the metric

g^{{\dagger}}h
as defined in (
4.40
). By (
8.11
),
since
g
is of degree
0
, we get

(8.12)

\mathrm{ch}\left(gA^{E_{0}\prime\prime}g^{-1},h\right)=\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},g^{{\dagger}}h\right).

By (
8.12
), we deduce that the Bott-Chern cohomology class of

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)
is unchanged when replacing

A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
by
gA^{E_{0}\prime\prime}g^{-1}
.

When changing the splitting in equation (
5.2
), as explained
at the end of Subsection
4.1
, we obtain a smooth
section
g
of
\mathrm{Aut}_{0}^{0}\left(E_{0}\right)
, and the new
antiholomorphic superconnection on
E_{0}
associated with the fixed

A^{E\prime\prime}
is just
gA^{E_{0}\prime\prime}g^{-1}
.
Using the above results, it is now clear that the Bott-Chern
cohomology class of
\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)
does not depend on the splitting, and so it
only depends on
A^{E\prime\prime}
.

Let
v_{0,0}^{*},A^{E_{0}\prime}_{0},A^{E_{0}}_{0}
denote the
analogues of
v^{*}_{0},A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},A^{E_{0}}
when replacing
h
by the pure metric
h_{0}
.
Proceeding as in the proof of (
7.18
), there are
smooth sections
\delta,\epsilon
of
T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right),\Lambda^{(\geq 2)}\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
of total degree
0,-1
such
that

(8.13)

A^{E_{0}\prime}=A^{E_{0}\prime}_{0}+\left[\delta,v_{0,0}^{*}\right]+\epsilon.

By (
8.13
), we get

(8.14)

A^{E_{0}}=A^{E_{0}}_{0}+\left[\delta,v_{0,0}^{*}\right]+\epsilon.

For
\ell\in\mathbf{R}
, let
A^{E_{0}}_{\ell}
be obtained from
A^{E_{0}}

by replacing
\epsilon
by
\ell\epsilon
. A Chern-Simons
transgression shows that

(8.15)

\frac{\partial}{\partial\ell}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}_{\ell}\right)\right]=-d^{X}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\epsilon\exp\left(-A^{E,2}_{\ell}\right)\right].

Since
\epsilon
is of degree
\geq 2
in

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
, the right-hand side of
(
8.15
) is of degree
\geq 4
, so that (
8.15
)
vanishes in degree
\leq 2
. We see that in degree
\leq 2
,

(8.16)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)\right]=\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}_{\ell}|_{\ell=0}\right)\right].

For
m\in\mathbf{R}
, let
B^{E_{0}}_{m}
be the analogue of
A^{E_{0}}
in
(
8.14
), in which
\delta,\epsilon
are replaced by

m\delta,0
. The same argument as before shows that

(8.17)

\frac{\partial}{\partial m}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-B^{E_{0},2}_{m}\right)\right]=-d^{X}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\left[\delta,v_{0,0}^{*}\right]\exp\left(-B^{E,2}_{m}\right)\right].

By (
8.17
), we deduce that

(8.18)

\frac{\partial}{\partial m}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-B^{E,2}_{m}\right)\right]^{(\leq 2)}=-d^{X}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\left[\delta,v_{0,0}^{*}\right]\exp\left(-\left[v_{0},v_{0,0}^{*}\right]\right)\right].

Since
\left[v_{0,0}^{*},\left[v_{0},v_{0,0}^{*}\right]\right]=0
, we
get

(8.19)

-\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\left[\delta,v_{0,0}^{*}\right]\exp\left(-\left[v_{0},v_{0,0}^{*}\right]\right)\right]=\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\left[v_{0,0}^{*},\delta\exp\left(-\left[v_{0},v_{0,0}^{*}\right]\right)\right]\right]=0.

By (
8.19
), (
8.17
) vanishes. Combining
(
8.16
) and the vanishing of (
8.17
), we get
(
8.3
).
The proof of
our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 8.3
.

The arguments of deformation over
\mathbf{P}^{1}
given in

[
BGS88a
, Section 1 f)]
and in the proof of
Proposition
2.3
can also be used to prove
some of the results established in Theorem
8.2
. In
particular, to prove independence on the splitting of
E
, one can
instead interpolate between two splittings of
E
over

\mathbf{P}^{1}
and proceed as in
[
BGS88a
]
.

In
[
Q16
, Proposition 2.24 and Corollary 3.14]
, Qiang established that in the
case where
h
is a pure metric, the form
\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)
is closed, and that its Bott-Chern class does not
depend on
h
. In
[
Q16
, Subsection 4.18]
, Qiang shows that
the
\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}\right)
is invariant when
conjugating
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
.

Definition 8.4
.

Let

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)\in H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
be
the common Bott-Chern cohomology class of the forms
\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)
.

8.2.
A trivial example

Here, we follow
[
B13
, Remark 4.5.3]
. We consider the
trivial
\mathscr{C}^{X}
in Example
5.2
, with
D=\mathbf{C}
,
and associated antiholomorphic superconnection
\overline{\partial}^{X}
. Let
\omega^{X}
be a smooth real

(1,1)
-form. Multiplication by
i\omega^{X}

is a self-adjoint operator with respect to the Hermitian form

\theta
in (
7.1
). The exponential

e^{-i\omega^{X}}
in
\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)

is also self-adjoint. In particular

h_{\omega^{X}}=\exp\left(-i\omega^{X}\right)
defines a generalized metric on

\mathbf{C}
, i.e., it can be viewed as an element of
\mathscr{M}^{\mathbf{C}}
.
Using the notation in (
7.12
), if
s,s^{\prime}\in\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
, then

(8.20)

\theta_{h_{\omega^{X}}}\left(s,s^{\prime}\right)=\theta\left(s,e^{-i\omega^{X}}s^{\prime}\right).

The adjoint
A^{\mathbf{C}\prime}
of
A^{\mathbf{C}\prime\prime}
is given by

(8.21)

A^{\mathbf{C}\prime}=\partial^{X}-i\partial^{X}\omega^{X}.

If
A^{\mathbf{C}}=A^{\mathbf{C}\prime\prime}+A^{\mathbf{C}\prime}
, the curvature

A^{\mathbf{C},2}
is given by

(8.22)

A^{\mathbf{C},2}=-\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}.

By (
8.22
), we get

(8.23)

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{\mathbf{C}\prime\prime},h_{\omega^{X}}\right)=\exp\left(-\frac{\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}}{4\pi^{2}}\right).

If we take
\omega^{X}=0
, we get the trivial form
1
.

More generally, if we make the same assumptions as in Subsection

8.1
, if
h\in\mathscr{M}^{D}
,
he^{-i\omega^{X}}

lies also in
\mathscr{M}^{D}
. Even when
h
is pure, when

\omega^{X}
is nonzero,
he^{-i\omega^{X}}
is not pure.

The above example will play a fundamental role in Section

13
. Indeed
the theory of the hypoelliptic Laplacian in
[
B13
]
can be
reinterpreted in terms of the above choice of a generalized metric on

\mathbf{C}
. in which it is crucial that
\omega^{X}
is the Kähler form of
a Hermitian metric on
X
.

8.3.
The Chern character of pull-backs

Let
Y
be a compact complex manifold, and let
f:X\to Y
be a holomorphic map as in Subsection

5.7
. Let
\left(F,A^{F\prime\prime}\right)
be an
antiholomorphic superconnection on
Y
, and let
\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right)=f^{*}_{b}\left(F,A^{F\prime\prime}\right)
be the
antiholomorphic superconnection on
X
that was defined
in Subsection
5.7
.

We fix a smooth splitting of
F
as in (
5.2
),
(
5.3
), that induces
a corresponding smooth splitting of
E
on
X
. Then we have identifications

F\simeq F_{0},E\simeq E_{0}
.
Let
h
be a smooth section of
\mathscr{M}^{D_{F}}
on
Y
. Then

f^{*}h

is a smooth section of
\mathscr{M}^{D_{E}}
on
Y
.

Proposition 8.5
.

The following identity of forms on
Y
holds:

(8.24)

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{f_{b}^{*}E_{0}\prime\prime},f^{*}h\right)=f^{*}\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right).

Also

(8.25)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{f_{b}^{*}E\prime\prime}\right)=f^{*}\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(Y,\mathbf{R}\right).

Proof.

The proof of equation (
8.24
) is easy and is left to the
reader. By taking the corresponding Bott-Chern cohomology class
in (
8.24
), we get (
8.25
).
∎

8.4.
Chern character and tensor products

Let
\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right),\left(\underline{E},A^{\underline{E}\prime\prime}\right)
be antiholomorphic
superconnections on
X
. Let
\left(E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}\underline{E},A^{E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}\underline{E}\prime\prime}\right)
denote the corresponding tensor
product, which was defined in Subsection
5.8
. Its diagonal bundle is given by
D\widehat{\otimes}\underline{D}
. We fix
splittings for
E,\underline{E}

similar to (
5.2
), (
5.3
), so that

E\simeq E_{0},\underline{E}\simeq\underline{E}_{0}
. These two splittings induce
a corresponding splitting of
E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}\underline{E}
, and

\left(E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}\underline{E}\right)_{0}=E_{0}\widehat{\otimes}_{b}\underline{E}_{0}
. If

h,\underline{h}
are
smooth sections of

\mathscr{M}^{D},\mathscr{M}^{\underline{D}}
, then
h\widehat{\otimes}\underline{h}
is a
smooth section of
\mathscr{M}^{D\widehat{\otimes}\underline{D}}
.

Proposition 8.6
.

The following identity of forms on
X
holds:

(8.26)

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{\left(E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}\underline{E}\right)_{0}\prime\prime},h\widehat{\otimes}\underline{h}\right)=\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{\underline{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\underline{h}\right).

Also

(8.27)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{E\widehat{\otimes}_{b}\underline{E}\prime\prime}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{\underline{E}\prime\prime}\right)\ \mathrm{in}\ H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right).

Proof.

The proof of (
8.26
) is easy and is left to the reader.
Equation (
8.27
) is just a consequence.
∎

8.5.
The case where
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
is locally free

We use the notation of Subsection
5.3
.
We fix a splitting of
E
as in (
5.2
), (
5.3
). We write

A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
as in (
5.6
). Let
h
be a
pure metric on
D
.

In this Subsection, we assume that the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{H}HD
is
locally free. By (
5.45
),
HD
is a smooth holomorphic vector bundle on
X
.

Put

(8.28)

\mathcal{H}D=\left\{f\in D,v_{0}f=0,v_{0}^{*}f=0\right\}.

Then
\mathcal{H}
is a smooth vector
subbundle of
D
. Using finite-dimensional Hodge theory, we have a
canonical isomorphism of smooth vector bundles,

(8.29)

\mathcal{H}D\simeq HD.

As a subbundle of
D
,
\mathcal{H}D
inherits a Hermitian metric.
Let
h^{HD}
denote the corresponding smooth Hermitian metric on
HD
.
Let
\nabla^{HD}
be the corresponding Chern connection on
HD
.

Let
P:D\to\mathcal{H}D
denote the orthogonal projection from
D

on
\mathcal{H}D
. Then
P
is a smooth section of

\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
that preserves the
\mathbf{Z}
-grading.
Let
\nabla^{\mathcal{H}D}
be the connection on
\mathcal{H}D
,

(8.30)

\nabla^{\mathcal{H}D}=P\nabla^{D}.

Then
\nabla^{\mathcal{H}D}
is a unitary connection on
\mathcal{H}D
.

Proposition 8.7
.

Via the identification
\mathcal{H}D\simeq HD
,

(8.31)

\nabla^{\mathcal{HD}}=\nabla^{HD}.

Proof.

Via the identification
\mathcal{H}D\simeq HD
, we have the identity,

(8.32)

\nabla^{\mathcal{H}D\prime\prime}=\nabla^{HD\prime\prime}.

By (
8.30
), we get

(8.33)

\nabla^{\mathcal{H}D\prime\prime}=P\nabla^{D\prime\prime}.

By (
8.33
), and taking adjoints, we obtain

(8.34)

\nabla^{\mathcal{H}D\prime}=P\nabla^{D\prime}.

By (
8.32
)–(
8.34
), we get (
8.31
). The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

8.6.
The Chern character form and the scaling of the metric

In this Subsection, we assume that
h\in\mathscr{M}^{D}
is a pure metric.
Recall that

N^{D}
is the number operator of
D
, i.e.,
N^{D}
acts by
multiplication by
k
on
D^{k}
.

Definition 8.8
.

For
T>0
, let

h_{T}\in\mathscr{M}^{D}
be the pure metric,

(8.35)

h_{T}=hT^{N^{D}}.

Let

A^{E_{0}\prime}_{T}
denote the adjoint of
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}

with respect to
h_{T}
. Put

(8.36)

A^{E_{0}}_{T}=A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}+A^{E_{0}\prime}_{T}.

If
A^{E_{0}\prime}
denote the adjoint of
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
with
respect to
h
, then

(8.37)

A^{E_{0}\prime}_{T}=T^{-N^{D}}A^{E_{0}\prime}T^{N^{D}}.

Set

\displaystyle B^{E_{0}\prime\prime}_{T}=T^{N^{D}/2}A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}T^{-N^{D}/2},

(8.38)

\displaystyle B^{E_{0}\prime}_{T}=T^{N^{D}/2}A^{E_{0}\prime}_{T}T^{-N^{D}/2},

\displaystyle B^{E_{0}}_{T}=T^{N^{D}/2}A^{E_{0}}_{T}T^{-N^{D}/2}.

Then

(8.39)

B^{E_{0}}_{T}=B^{E_{0}\prime\prime}_{T}+B^{E_{0}\prime}_{T}.

Moreover,
B^{E_{0}\prime}_{T}
is the
h
-adjoint of
B^{E_{0}\prime\prime}_{T}
.

As we saw in Subsection
8.5
, if
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}

is locally free, then
HD
is a holomorphic vector bundle, and the
pure metric
h
induces a Hermitian metric
h^{HD}
on
HD
. Let

\mathrm{ch}\left(HD,h^{HD}\right)
be the Chern form associated with the

\mathbf{Z}
-graded holomorphic Hermitian vector bundle
HD
. This Chern
form is defined using the methods of Subsection
2.4
.

Recall that the metric

h^{\det D}
on
\det D
associated with
h
was defined after equation
(
7.19
).
Let
c_{1}\left(\det D,h^{\det D}\right)
denote the first Chern form
associated with the holomorphic Hermitian line bundle
\left(\det D,h^{\det D}\right)
.

The Knudsen-Mumford determinant line bundle
\det\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
was considered in Subsection

5.4
, and we have the canonical isomorphism in
(
5.48
).

If
\alpha\in\Omega^{(=)}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
, we denote by

\alpha^{(1,1)}
its component in
\Omega^{(1,1)}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
.
If
\beta\in H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
, let

\beta^{(1,1)}
be the component of
\beta
in

H^{(1,1)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
.

If
\alpha_{T}|_{T>0}
is a family of smooth forms on
X
, we will
say that as
T\to+\infty
,

\alpha_{T}=\mathcal{O}\left(1/\sqrt{T}\right)
if for any
k\in\mathbf{N}
,
there exists
C_{k}>0
such that for
T\geq 1
, the sup of the norm of
\alpha_{T}

and its derivatives of order
\leq k
is dominated by
C_{k}/\sqrt{T}
.

Theorem 8.9
.

If
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
is locally free, as
T\to+\infty
,

(8.40)

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h_{T}\right)=\mathrm{ch}\left(HD,h^{HD}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(1/\sqrt{T}\right).

Under the same assumption,

(8.41)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(HD\right)\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right).

In particular, if
HD=0
, then

(8.42)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)=0\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right).

As
T\to 0
,

(8.43)

\mathrm{ch}^{(1,1)}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h_{T}\right)\to c_{1}\left(\det D,h^{\det D}\right).

We have the identity,

(8.44)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}^{(1,1)}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)=c_{1,\mathrm{BC}}\left(\det\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}\right)\ \mathrm{in}\ H_{\mathrm{BC}}^{(1,1)}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right).

Proof.

By (
8.1
), (
8.6
), we have

(8.45)

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h_{T}\right)=\varphi\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-B^{E_{0},2}_{T}\right)\right].

By (
5.6
), (
7.15
), and (
8.6
), we get

(8.46)

\displaystyle B^{E\prime\prime}_{T}=\sqrt{T}v_{0}+\nabla^{D\prime\prime}+\sum_{i\geq 2}T^{\left(1-i\right)/2}v_{i},

\displaystyle B^{E\prime}_{T}=\sqrt{T}v_{0}^{*}+\nabla^{D\prime}+\sum_{i\geq 2}T^{\left(1-i\right)/2}v_{i}^{*}.

By (
8.46
), we get

(8.47)

B^{E}_{T}=\sqrt{T}\left(v_{0}+v_{0}^{*}\right)+\nabla^{D}+\sum_{i\geq 2}T^{\left(1-i\right)/2}\left(v_{i}+v_{i}^{*}\right).

Using Proposition
8.7
, (
8.47
), and proceeding
as in
[
BGV92
, Corollary 9.6]
in a finite-dimensional context, we get
(
8.40
). By (
8.40
), we obtain (
8.41
),
and also (
8.42
).

Set

(8.48)

B^{E_{0},\leq 1}_{T}=\sqrt{T}\left(v_{0}+v_{0}^{*}\right)+\nabla^{D}.

Then
B^{E_{0}}_{T}-B^{E_{0},\leq 1}_{T}
is a smooth section of

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
whose
degree in
\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
is
\geq 2
. By proceeding as in
(
8.15
), we deduce that

(8.49)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-B^{E_{0},2}_{T}\right)\right]^{(2)}=\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-B^{E_{0},\leq 1,2}_{T}\right)\right]^{(2)}.

As
T\to 0
,

(8.50)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-B^{E_{0},\leq 1,2}_{T}\right)\right]^{(2)}\to\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-\nabla^{D,2}\right)\right]^{(2)}=\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[-\nabla^{D,2}\right].

Using Proposition
7.5
, we get

(8.51)

\varphi\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[-\nabla^{D,2}\right]=c_{1}\left(\det D,h^{\det D}\right).

By (
8.49
)–(
8.51
), we obtain
(
8.43
). Finally, by combining Theorem
5.9
and
(
8.43
), we get (
8.44
).
The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 8.10
.

The argument used in the proof of (
8.49
) is exactly
the same as the argument used by Bismut-Freed in a smooth
infinite-dimensional context in their proof of

[
BF86
, eq. (1.53) in Theorem 1.19]
. This argument
reappears in a holomorphic infinite-dimensional context in

[
BGS88c
, Theorems 1.13 and 1.15]
. However, in
these references, the
addition of ‘bad’ diverging terms in the considered
superconnections plays a key role in the convergence of the
superconnection forms as
T\to 0
, while here, the situation is the
opposite. Observe that in
[
Q16
, Theorem 4.21]
, when

HD=0
, Qiang had established Theorem
8.9
by similar
arguments to the ones we just gave.

8.7.
The Chern character of a cone

We use the notation of Subsections
4.3
and
5.6
. In
particular,
\phi
is a morphism
E\to\underline{E}
of degree

0
, and
\left(C,A^{C\prime\prime}_{\phi}\right)
is the
corresponding cone. Here we reobtain a result of Qiang

[
Q16
, Proposition 4.24]
.

Theorem 8.11
.

The following identity holds:

(8.52)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{C\prime\prime}_{\phi}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{\underline{E}\prime\prime}\right)-\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right).

In particular, if
\phi
is a
quasi-isomorphism, then

(8.53)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{\underline{E}\prime\prime}\right)\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right).

Proof.

For
t\in\mathbf{C}
, let
\left(C_{t},A^{C\prime\prime}_{t\phi}\right)
be
the complex of the cone associated with
t\phi
as in Subsection

5.6
. Recall that

M_{t}
is given by (
4.30
). If
t\in\mathbf{C}^{*}
,
M_{t}

is invertible, and equation (
5.68
) holds. By Theorem
8.2
, if
t\in\mathbf{C}^{*}
,

(8.54)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{C\prime\prime}_{t\phi}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{C\prime\prime}_{\phi}\right).

Moreover, as
t\to 0
, we get

(8.55)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{C\prime\prime}_{t\phi}\right)\to\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{C\prime\prime}_{0}\right).

We have the trivial identity

(8.56)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{C\prime\prime}_{0}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{\underline{E}\prime\prime}\right)-\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right).

By (
8.54
)–(
8.56
), we get (
8.52
).

If
\phi
is a quasi-isomorphism, using equation (
8.42
) in
Theorem
8.9
, we get

(8.57)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{C\prime\prime}_{\phi}\right)=0.

By combining (
8.52
) and (
8.57
), we get
(
8.53
).
The
proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

8.8.
The Chern character on
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)

By Theorem
6.7
, if
\mathscr{F}
is an object in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
, there is
an antiholomorphic superconnection
\mathscr{E}=\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right)
such
that
\mathscr{E}
is isomorphic to
\mathscr{F}
in

\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
.

Here we reobtain a result of Qiang
[
Q16
, Theorem 4.25]
.

Theorem 8.12
.

The class
\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)\in H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)

depends only on the isomorphism class of
\mathscr{F}
in

\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
.

Proof.

Let
\underline{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
be isomorphic
to
\mathscr{F}
, and let
\underline{\mathscr{E}}
be a
corresponding antiholomorphic superconnection. By Theorem

6.9
,
\underline{F}_{X}
is an equivalence of
categories. Therefore, there exists a
quasi-isomorphism
\phi:\mathscr{E}\to\underline{\mathscr{E}}
.
Our theorem follows from Theorem
8.11
.
∎

Definition 8.13
.

Let

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{F}\right)\in H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)

be the common Bott-Chern class of the above
\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)
.

In the sequel, if
\mathscr{E}=\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right)
, we will often identify
\mathscr{E}
and

\underline{F}_{X}\left(\mathscr{E}\right)
, and we will also use
the notation

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{E}\right)
instead of

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)
.

Let
X,Y
be compact complex manifolds, and let
f:X\to Y
be a
holomorphic map. If
\mathscr{F}
is an object in

\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(Y\right)
, then
Lf^{*}\mathscr{F}
is an object in

\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
.

Theorem 8.14
.

The following identity holds:

(8.58)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(Lf^{*}\mathscr{F}\right)=f^{*}\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{F}\right).

Proof.

As before, we may and we will assume that instead
\mathscr{E}
is an object in
\mathrm{B}\left(Y\right)
. Our
theorem is now a consequence of Propositions
6.10

and

8.5
.
∎

If
\mathscr{F},\underline{\mathscr{F}}
are objects in

\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
, their derived tensor product
\mathscr{F}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}^{\mathrm{L}}\underline{\mathscr{F}}
was
defined in Subsection
3.3
.

Theorem 8.15
.

The following identity holds:

(8.59)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{F}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}^{\mathrm{L}}\underline{\mathscr{F}}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{F}\right)\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\underline{\mathscr{F}}\right)\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right).

Proof.

We may as replace
\mathscr{F},\underline{\mathscr{F}}
by
antiholomorphic superconnections
\mathscr{E},\underline{\mathscr{E}}
. Our Theorem
follows from Propositions
6.11
and

8.6
.
∎

8.9.
The Chern character on
K\left(X\right)

Let

K\left(\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)\right)
denote the
K
-theory of

\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
. By definition, this is the Grothendieck group
generated by the objects in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
, with relations coming from the
construction of cones. Namely if
\mathscr{F},\underline{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
, if
\phi:\mathscr{F}\to\underline{\mathscr{F}}
is a morphism of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-complexes, let
\mathscr{C}\in\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
be the cone of
\mathscr{F},\underline{\mathscr{F}}
. Then
22
22

22

This condition is equivalent to the standard
condition using instead distinguished triangles in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)

[
St20
,
Tag 0FCN
]
.

(8.60)

\mathscr{C}+\mathscr{F}=\underline{\mathscr{F}}\ \mathrm{in}\ K\left(\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)\right).

Let

K\left(X\right)
be the Grothendieck group of coherent sheaves
on
X
. By (
3.2
), (
3.3
), we have the identity

(8.61)

K\left(\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)\right)=K\left(X\right).

The derived tensor product on
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
induces a
corresponding tensor product on
K\left(X\right)
, so that

K\left(X\right)
is a commutative ring.

By Theorems
8.11
and
8.12
, and by (
8.61
), the Chern character

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}
can be viewed as a morphism of rings from

K\left(X\right)
into
H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
.

If
\mathscr{F}
is an object in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
, we will often identify
\mathscr{F}

to its image in
K\left(X\right)
.

Since the
\mathscr{H}^{i}\mathscr{F}
are coherent sheaves, they can
be viewed as elements of
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
equipped with the
0

differential.
Put

(8.62)

\mathscr{R}\mathscr{H}\mathscr{F}=\sum\left(-1\right)^{i}\mathscr{H}^{i}\mathscr{F},

so that
\mathscr{R}\mathscr{H}\mathscr{F}\in K\left(X\right)
.

Theorem 8.16
.

If
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
, then

(8.63)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{F}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{R}\mathscr{H}\mathscr{F}\right).

Proof.

By (
3.2
), (
3.3
), we have the identity,

(8.64)

\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{R}\mathscr{H}\mathscr{F}\ \mathrm{in}\ K\left(X\right).

Taking the Chern character of (
8.64
), we get
(
8.63
). The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 8.17
.

By Theorem
8.16
, we deduce that if
\mathscr{E}=\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right)
is an antiholomorphic
superconnection, then

(8.65)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{E}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{R}\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}\right).

As we saw in Remark
5.8
,
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
depends
only on
D,v_{0},\nabla^{D}
. By (
8.65
), the same is true for

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{E}\right)
. When
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{H}HD
is locally free, this is also a consequence of Theorem

8.9
.

If
\nabla^{D\prime\prime}
defines a holomorphic structure on
D
, i.e., if
\nabla^{D\prime\prime,2}=0
, we can define
\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(D\right)
.

Proposition 8.18
.

If
\nabla^{D\prime\prime,2}=0
, then

(8.66)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{E}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(D\right).

Proof.

For
t\in\mathbf{C}
, put

(8.67)

A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}_{t}=\nabla^{D\prime\prime}+tv_{0}.

Set

(8.68)

\mathscr{E}_{t}=\left(E_{0},A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}_{t}\right).

By Remarks
5.8
and
8.17
, for
t\neq 0
,
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}_{t}
. By (
8.65
), for
t\neq 0
, we get

(8.69)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{E}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{E}_{t}\right).

Also as
t\in\mathbf{C}^{*}\to 0
,

(8.70)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{E}_{t}\right)\to\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(D\right).

By (
8.68
), (
8.70
), we get (
8.66
). The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

If
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
is locally free, our proposition is
also a consequence of Theorem
8.9
.

8.10.
Spectral truncations

We fix a splitting as in (
5.2
), (
5.3
), and also a Hermitian
metric
g^{D}
on
D
. We write
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
as in
(
5.6
). In particular,

(8.71)

A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}=v_{0}+A^{E_{0}\prime\prime(\geq 1)}.

Put

(8.72)

\mathbb{A}^{E_{0}}=A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}+v_{0}^{*}.

As in (
7.23
), (
7.24
), we get

(8.73)

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}^{E_{0},2}=\left[A^{E_{0},\prime\prime},v_{0}^{*}\right],

\displaystyle\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},\mathbb{A}^{E_{0},2}\right]=0,\qquad\left[v_{0}^{*},\mathbb{A}^{E_{0},2}\right]=0.

Note that
\mathbb{A}^{E_{0},2}
is the piece of
A^{E_{0},2}
in
which the terms that contain factors of positive degree in

\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)
have been killed.

Put

(8.74)

V_{0}=v_{0}+v_{0}^{*}.

Then

(8.75)

V_{0}^{2}=\left[v_{0},v_{0}^{*}\right].

Also
\mathrm{Sp}V_{0}^{2}\subset\mathbf{R}_{+}
.

Put

(8.76)

J=\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime(\geq 1)},v_{0}^{*}\right].

Then
J
is a smooth section of degree
0
in

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
, which
only contains terms of positive degrees in

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
.

Then

(8.77)

\mathbb{A}^{E_{0},2}=V_{0}^{2}+J.

Since
\Lambda^{(\geq 1)}\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
is
nilpotent, from (
8.77
), we get

(8.78)

\mathrm{Sp}\mathbb{A}^{E_{0},2}=\mathrm{Sp}V_{0}^{2}.

If
\lambda\in\mathbf{C},\lambda\notin\mathrm{Sp}\left(V_{0}^{2}\right)
,
then

(8.79)

\left(\lambda-\mathbb{A}^{E_{0},2}\right)^{-1}=\left(\lambda-V_{0}^{2}\right)^{-1}+\left(\lambda-V_{0}^{2}\right)^{-1}J\left(\lambda-V_{0}^{2}\right)^{-1}+\ldots

and the expansion only contains a finite number of terms.

For
a>0
, set

(8.80)

U_{a}=\left\{x\in X,a\notin\mathrm{Sp}\,V_{0}^{2}\right\}.

Then
U_{a}
is an open set in
X
.

Definition 8.19
.

Over
U_{a}
, put

(8.81)

P_{a,-}=\frac{1}{2i\pi}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\mathbf{C}\\
\left|\lambda\right|=a\end{subarray}}\frac{d\lambda}{\lambda-\mathbb{A}^{E_{0},2}}.

Then
P_{a,-}
is a projector that acts on
E_{0}
, and
commutes with the action of
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
. By
(
8.73
), we get

(8.82)

\displaystyle\left[A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},P_{a,-}\right]=0,

\displaystyle\left[v_{0}^{*},P_{a,-}\right]=0.

Put

(8.83)

P_{a,+}=1-P_{a,-}.

Then
P_{a,+}
is also a projector.

By (
8.79
),
(
8.81
), we can write
P_{a,\pm}
in the form

(8.84)

P_{a,\pm}=P_{a,\pm}^{0}+P_{a,\pm}^{(\geq 1)}.

Let
D_{a,\pm}
be the direct sums of the eigenspaces of
V_{0}^{2}
for
eigenvalues
\lambda>a
or
\lambda<a
. Then
P^{0}_{a,\pm}
is the
orthogonal projectors
D\to D_{a,\pm}
. Since
P_{a,\pm}=P_{a,\pm}^{2}
, we get

(8.85)

P_{a,\pm}^{(\geq 1)}=\left[P^{0}_{a,\pm},P_{a,\pm}^{(\geq 1)}\right]_{+}+P_{a,\pm}^{(\geq 1),2}.

In particular
P^{1}_{a,\pm}
maps
D_{a,\pm}
into
\overline{T^{*}X}\widehat{\otimes}D_{a,\mp}
.

Definition 8.20
.

On
U_{a}
, put

(8.86)

E_{0,a,\pm}=P_{a,\pm}E_{0}.

On
U_{a}
,
E_{0,a,\pm}
is a subbundle of
E_{0}
which is also a

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
-module. Let
i_{a,\pm}
be
the corresponding embedding in
E_{0}
. In the sequel, we use the
notation in Subsection
4.1
, and in particular equation
(
4.2
).

Theorem 8.21
.

On
U_{a}
, we have a splitting of

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
-modules,

(8.87)

E_{0}=E_{0,a,+}\oplus E_{0,a,-}.

Moreover,

(8.88)

F^{p}E_{0,a,\pm}=E_{0,a,\pm}\cap F^{p}E_{0}.

Also
P_{a,\pm}
induces a filtered isomorphism of

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
-modules,

(8.89)

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D_{a,\pm}\simeq E_{0,a,\pm}.

As a
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
-module,
E_{0,a,\pm}

verifies the conditions in (
4.6
), and the associated diagonal bundle is
just
D_{a,\pm}
. Also it is equipped with a splitting as in
(
5.2
),(
5.3
).

Moreover,

A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
preserves the smooth sections of

E_{0,a,\pm}
, and it induces an antiholomorphic
superconnection
A^{E_{0,a,\pm}\prime\prime}
on

E_{0,a,\pm}
, so that on
U_{a}
, we have the splitting

(8.90)

\mathscr{E}_{0}=\mathscr{E}_{0,a,+}\oplus\mathscr{E}_{0,a,-}.

Finally, on
U_{a}
,
P_{a,-}:\mathscr{E}_{0}\to\mathscr{E}_{0,a,-}
and

i_{a,-}:\mathscr{E}_{0,a,-}\to\mathscr{E}_{0}
are quasi-isomorphisms of

\mathcal{O}_{U_{a}}
-complexes, and
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,+}=0
.

Proof.

Equation (
8.87
) is obvious. Since
P_{a,\pm}
commutes
with
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
, it maps
F^{p}E_{0}
into

F^{p}E_{0,a,\pm}
, from which we get (
8.88
).

Let
Q_{a,\pm}

be the morphism
\Lambda^{p}\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D_{a,\pm}\to F^{p}E_{0,a,\pm}/F^{p+1}E_{0,a,\pm}

induced by
P_{a,\pm}
. If
e\in\Lambda^{p}\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D_{a,\pm}
, and if
P_{a,\pm}e\in F^{p+1}E_{0,a,\pm}
, by (
8.84
), we find that
e=0
, so that
Q_{a,\pm}
is injective. Let us
prove
Q_{a,\pm}
is surjective. If
e\in E_{0,a,\pm}
, by (
8.84
), we
deduce that

(8.91)

e=P^{0}_{a,\pm}e+P^{(\geq 1)}_{a,\pm}e,

so that

(8.92)

e=P_{a,\pm}P^{0}_{a,\pm}e+P_{a,\pm}P^{(\geq 1)}_{a,\pm}e.

If
e\in F^{p}E_{0,a,\pm}
, by (
8.92
),

e-P_{a,\pm}P^{0}_{a,\pm}e\in F^{p+1}E_{0,a,\pm}
, which shows that

Q_{a,\pm}
is surjective. By (
8.89
), we conclude that

E_{0,a,\pm}
verifies the conditions in (
4.6
), and it is
equipped with a splitting as in (
4.19
) which is induced
by (
8.89
).

By the first identity in (
8.82
),
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
acts on smooth sections of
E_{0,a,\pm}
. Therefore, it induces an
antiholomorphic superconnection
A^{E_{0,a,\pm}\prime\prime}
on
E_{0,a,\pm}
, and (
8.90
)
holds.

Clearly,
\mathbb{A}^{E_{0,a,+},2}
acts as an invertible operator on

E_{0,a,+}
,
v_{0}^{*}
acts on
E_{0,a,+}
, and by (
8.73
),

(8.93)

1|_{E_{0,a,+}}=\left[A^{E_{0,a,+}\prime\prime},v_{0}^{*}\left[\mathbb{A}^{E_{0,a,+},2}\right]^{-1}\right].

By (
8.93
), we find that
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}_{a,+}=0
.

By fiberwise Hodge theory, for any
x\in X
,

P_{a,-}^{0}:D\to D_{a,-}
and
i_{a,-}^{0}:D_{a,-}\to D
are
quasi-isomorphisms. By Proposition
6.8
, we conclude
that
P_{a,-}
and
i_{a,-}
are quasi-isomorphisms.

The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

We will view
A^{E_{0,a,\pm}\prime\prime}
as a superconnection

\mathsf{A}^{\prime\prime}_{a,\pm}
on

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D_{a,\pm}
. By construction, we
have the identity

(8.94)

P_{a,\pm}\mathsf{A}^{\prime\prime}_{a,\pm}=A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}P_{a,\pm}.

Let us now express
\mathsf{A}^{\prime\prime}_{a,\pm}
as in (
5.6
),
i.e.,

(8.95)

\mathsf{A}^{\prime\prime}_{a,\pm}=v_{0,a,\pm}+\nabla^{D_{a,\pm}\prime\prime}+\sum_{i\geq 2}v_{i,a,\pm}.

By (
8.84
), (
8.94
), we can determine the various terms in
(
8.95
) by recursion.

Proposition 8.22
.

The following identities hold:

(8.96)

\displaystyle v_{0,a,\pm}=v_{0|_{D_{a,\pm}}},

\displaystyle\nabla^{D_{a,\pm}\prime\prime}=P^{0}_{a,\pm}\nabla^{D\prime\prime}P^{0}_{a,\pm}.

Proof.

In degree
0
, equation (
8.94
) gives the first equation
in (
8.96
). In degree
1
, we get

(8.97)

\nabla^{D_{a,\pm}\prime\prime}+P^{1}_{a,\pm}v_{0,a,\pm}=\nabla^{D\prime\prime}P_{a,\pm}^{0}+v_{0}P_{a,\pm}^{1}.

Since
P^{1}_{a,\pm}
exchanges
D_{a,+}
and
D_{a,-}
, from
(
8.97
), we get the second equation in (
8.96
). The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

Remark 8.23
.

By Theorem
5.9
, we know that
\nabla^{D\prime\prime},\nabla^{D_{a,\pm}\prime\prime}
induce holomorphic structures on the lines

\det D,\det D_{a,\pm}
. Since
D=D_{a,+}\oplus D_{a,-}
, we
conclude that we have the smooth isomorphism

(8.98)

\det D=\det D_{a,+}\widehat{\otimes}\det D_{a,-}.

By (
8.97
), it is elementary to verify that (
8.98
)
is an isomorphism of holomorphic line bundles on
U_{a}
. As
explained in
[
BGS88a
]
, since

\left(D_{a,+},v_{0}|_{D_{a,+}}\right)
is exact,
\det D_{a,+}

has a canonical section
\tau_{a,+}
. Since
\nabla^{D_{a,+}\prime\prime}v_{0,a,+}=0
, the section
\tau_{a,+}
is holomorphic. By the above,
the map
s\in\det D_{a,-}\to s\widehat{\otimes}\tau_{a,+}\in\det D
is a
holomorphic identification of line bundles.

The above completely elucidates the arguments given in an infinite
dimensional context in
[
BGS88c
, Theorem 1.3]
, in
which
\det D_{a,-}
was shown directly to carry a holomorphic
structure, The explanation is now obvious:
E_{a,-}
carries an
antiholomorphic superconnection.

Observe that for
a<b
, on
U_{a}\cap U_{b}
,
E_{0,a,-}\subset E_{0,b,-}
, and that

(8.99)

P_{a,-}=P_{a,-}P_{b,-}.

In particular, on
U_{a}\cap U_{b}
,
P_{a,-}|_{\mathscr{E}_{0,b,-}}:\mathscr{E}_{0,b,-}\to\mathscr{E}_{0,a,-}
, and
i_{a,b}=\mathscr{E}_{0,a,-}\to\mathscr{E}_{0,b,-}

are quasi-isomorphisms.

Let
g^{D,1}
be another Hermitian metric on
D
. We
denote with an extra superscript
1
the objects we just constructed
that are associated with
g^{D,1}
.

Proposition 8.24
.

For
a>0,a^{1}>0
, on
U_{a}\cap U^{1}_{a_{1}}
, the map

P_{a,-}|_{\mathscr{E}^{1}_{0,a_{1},-}}:\mathscr{E}^{1}_{0,a_{1},-}\to\mathscr{E}_{0,a,-}
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

Clearly,

(8.100)

P_{a,-}|_{\mathscr{E}^{1}_{0,a_{1},-}}=P_{a,-}i^{1}_{a_{1},-}.

Our proposition follows from Theorem
8.21
and from
(
8.100
).
∎

9.
The case of embeddings

The purpose of this Section is to establish our
Riemann-Roch-Grothendieck theorem in the case of an embedding

i_{X,Y}:X\to Y
. Our proof uses all the properties we established
in the previous Sections on
\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}
, and also the deformation to the
normal cone. As a consequence we show that our Chern character

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}:K\left(X\right)\to H_{\mathrm{BC}}^{(=)}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
verifies the
uniqueness conditions stated by Grivaux
[
Gri10
]
.

This Section is organized as follows. In Subsection

9.1
, we recall elementary facts on embeddings, direct
images, and transversality.

In Subsection
9.2
, given an embedding
i_{X,Y}:X\to Y
, we describe the deformation to the normal cone.

In Subsection
9.3
, we establish our main theorem for
the embedding
i_{X,Y}
.

Finally, in Subsection
9.4
, from our
Riemann-Roch-Grothendieck formula for embeddings, we show that our Chern
character
\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}
verifies the uniqueness conditions of Grivaux

[
Gri10
]
.

9.1.
Embeddings, direct images, and transversality

Let
Z
be a compact complex manifold. Let
i_{X,Z}:X\to Z,i_{Y,Z}:Y\to Z
be two holomorphic embeddings of compact complex
manifolds. We assume that
U=Y\cap Z
is a compact submanifold of

Z
, and also that

(9.1)

TU=TX|_{U}\cap TY|_{U}.

We denote by
i_{U,X},i_{U,Y}
the embeddings of
U
in
X,Y
.

The excess normal bundle
N
on
U
is defined to be

(9.2)

N=TZ|_{U}/\left(TX|_{U}+TY|_{U}\right).

We have the exact sequence of vector bundles

(9.3)

0\to N_{U/Y}\to N_{X/Z}|_{U}\to N\to 0.

The manifolds
X
and
Y
are said to be transverse if
N=0
, which
is equivalent to

(9.4)

N_{U/Y}=N_{X/Z}|_{U}.

We use the notation of Section
3
.
Let
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
, and let
i_{X,Z,*}\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(Z\right)
be its direct image, which coincides with the
right derived image
Ri_{X,Z*}\mathscr{F}
. Let
Li_{U,X}^{*}\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(U\right)
be the left derived pull-back of
\mathscr{F}
. Other similar objects will be denoted in the same way.

Proposition 9.1
.

If
X
and
Y
are transverse, there exists an isomorphism
in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(Y\right)
,

(9.5)

Li_{Y,Z}^{*}i_{X,Z,*}\mathscr{F}\simeq i_{U,Y,*}Li_{U,X}^{*}\mathscr{F}.

Proof.

Let
\mathscr{E}_{X}=\left(E_{X},A^{E_{X}\prime\prime}\right),\mathscr{E}_{Z}=\left(E_{Z},A^{E_{Z}\prime\prime}\right)
be antiholomorphic
superconnections
on
X,Z
that represent
\mathscr{F}
and
i_{X,Z,*}\mathscr{F}
.
By Proposition
6.10
,
Li_{U,X}^{*}\mathscr{F}
is represented by
i^{*}_{U,X,b}\mathscr{E}_{X}
,
and
Li_{Y,Z}^{*}i_{X,Z,*}\mathscr{F}
is represented by
i_{Y,Z,b}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{Z}
.

As in Subsection
6.8
,
C^{\infty}\left(X,E_{X}\right)
can be viewed
as a

\mathcal{O}_{Z}^{\infty}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}Z}\right)\right)
-module, with
the convention that if
\alpha\in\Omega^{0,\scriptsize\bullet}\left(Z,\mathbf{C}\right),s\in C^{\infty}\left(X,E_{X}\right)
, then

(9.6)

\alpha.s=\left(i_{X,Z}^{*}\alpha\right)s.

We will denote the corresponding
\mathcal{O}_{Z}
-complex by

i_{X,Z,*}\mathscr{E}_{X}
. As we saw before,

i_{X,Z,*}\mathscr{E}_{X}
defines an object in

\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(Z\right)
, that
coincides with its right-derived direct image.

By Proposition
6.12
, there exists a morphism of

\mathcal{O}_{Z}
-complexes
r_{Z,X}:\mathscr{E}_{Z}\to i_{X,Z,*}\mathscr{E}_{X}
which induces also a morphism of

\mathcal{O}_{Z}^{\infty}\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}Z}\right)
-modules,
and
r_{Z,X}
is a quasi-isomorphism of

\mathcal{O}_{Z}
-complexes.

To make our notation simpler, we will use the notation
\mathscr{E}_{X}
instead of
i_{X,Z,*}\mathscr{E}_{X}
. A similar notation will
be used when considering the embedding
i_{U,Y}
.

The above maps can be combined in a commutative diagram
23
23

23

As
explained in Subsection
5.7
, the
subscript
b
is used to define the pull-back of antiholomorphic
superconnections.

(9.7)

\textstyle{\mathscr{E}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{Y,Z,b}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{r_{Z,X}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{E}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{U,X,b}^{*}}
\textstyle{i_{Y,Z,b}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{Y,U}}
\textstyle{i_{U,X,b}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{X}.}

in which
r_{Y,U}
is induced by
r_{Z,X}
. The first row consists
of objects in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(Z\right)
, the second row of objects in

\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(Y\right)
. Also
r_{Y,U}
is a morphism of

\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{Y}\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}Y}\right)
-modules,
and of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-complexes.

As we saw before,
r_{Z,X}
is a quasi-isomorphism of

\mathcal{O}_{Z}
-complexes. To establish our
proposition, we need to show that
r_{Y,U}
is a
quasi-isomorphism of objects in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(Y\right)
. The proof will be obtained via local arguments.

Since
r_{Z,X}
is a quasi-isomorphism, on
Z\setminus X
,
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}_{Z}=0
. By a local version of Theorem
5.7
, on

Z\setminus X
,
HD_{Z}=0
, so that on
Y\setminus U
,

Hi^{*}_{Y,Z}D_{Z}=0
. Using again Theorem
5.7
, we
find that on
Y\setminus U
,
\mathscr{H}i_{Y,Z,b}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{Z}=0
.

Take
x\in U
. If
V\subset Z
is a small neighborhood of
x
, we choose a holomorphic coordinate
system on
V
such that
x
is represented by
0\in\mathbf{C}^{n}
, and
X,Y
are
two transverse vector subspaces
H_{X},H_{Y}
of
\mathbf{C}^{n}
, so that

U
is represented by
H_{X}\cap H_{Y}
. If
K
is a
vector subspace of
H_{Y}
such that
H_{X}\oplus K=\mathbf{C}^{n}
, then
K

represents both
N_{X/Z}
and
N_{U/Y}
. Let
V_{H_{X}},V_{K}
be
open neighborhoods of
0
in
H_{X},K
so that
V_{H_{X}}\times V_{K}\subset V
. By Theorem
5.3
, if
V_{H_{X}}
is small enough, after a
gauge transformation of total degree
0
,
A^{E_{X}\prime\prime}
can be written in the form

(9.8)

A^{E_{X}\prime\prime}=\nabla^{D_{X}\prime\prime}+v_{X,0}.

Let
\pi_{H_{X}},\pi_{K}
be the projections of
\mathbf{C}^{n}
on
H_{X},K
.
Let
y
be the generic section of
K
, and let
\left(\Lambda K^{*},i_{y}\right)
denote the Koszul complex of
K
. Set

(9.9)

\mathbf{E}_{Z}=\Lambda\left(\overline{\mathbf{C}}^{n}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\pi_{H_{X}}^{*}D_{X}\widehat{\otimes}\pi^{*}_{K}\Lambda K^{*}.

Let
A^{\mathbf{E}_{Z}\prime\prime}
be the antiholomorphic superconnection,

(9.10)

A^{\mathbf{E}_{Z}\prime\prime}=\pi_{H_{X}}^{*}\left(\nabla^{D_{X}\prime\prime}+v_{X,0}\right)+\pi_{K}^{*}\left(\overline{\partial}^{K}+i_{y}\right).

Then
A^{\mathbf{E}_{Z}\prime\prime}
is an antiholomorphic
superconnection on
\mathbf{C}^{n}
near
0
. Let
\mathcal{E}_{Z}
denote
the corresponding complex of
\mathcal{O}_{V}
-modules. Let

r_{\mathbf{C}^{n},H_{X}}
be the
projection
\pi^{*}_{H_{X}}D_{X}\widehat{\otimes}\pi^{*}_{K}\Lambda K^{*}\to D_{X}
. Then
r_{\mathbf{C}^{n},H_{X}}
extends to a morphism

\mathcal{E}_{Z}\to\mathscr{E}_{X}
which has the same properties as
r_{Z,X}
.
Because of known properties of the Koszul complex, if the considered
neighborhoods are small enough,
r_{\mathbf{C}^{n},H_{X}}
is a
quasi-isomorphism.

Near
z\in Z
, both
r_{Z,X}
and
r_{\mathbf{C}^{n},H_{X}}
provide
quasi-isomorphisms of
\mathscr{E}_{Z},\mathcal{E}_{Z}
with
\mathscr{E}_{X}
, so that for
V
small
enough,
\mathscr{E}_{Z}
and
\mathbf{E}_{Z}
are isomorphic as
objects in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(V_{H_{X}}\times V_{K}\right)
. By a local
version of Theorem

6.9
, there is a corresponding quasi-isomorphism

\phi:\mathscr{E}_{Z}\to\mathcal{E}_{Z}
. As we saw in Subsection

5.7
,
\phi
induces a morphism
i_{Y,Z,b}^{*}\phi:i_{Y,Z,b}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{Z}\to i^{*}_{Y,Z,b}\mathcal{E}_{Z}
. By a
local version of Proposition
6.8
,
for
z\in V_{H_{X}}\times V_{K}
,
\phi_{z}:D_{Z,z}\to\left(\pi_{H^{X}}^{*}D_{X}\widehat{\otimes}\pi_{K}^{*}\Lambda K^{*}\right)_{z}
is a
quasi-isomorphism. In particular, this will be true for
z\in\left(V_{H_{X}\times}V_{K}\right)\cap Y
.
This shows that near
z\in Y
,
\phi
induces a quasi-isomorphism

i_{Y,Z,b}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{Z}\to i_{Y,Z,b}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{Z}
.

Since near
x\in Y
, the restriction of the above Koszul complex to

Y
is still a Koszul complex,
r_{K,U}:i_{Y,Z,b}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{Z}\to i_{U,X,b}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{X}
is a
quasi-isomorphism. This shows that
r_{Y,U}:i^{*}_{Y,Z,b}\mathscr{E}_{Z}\to i_{U,X,b}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{X}
is a quasi-isomorphism. The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

9.2.
Deformation to the normal cone

Let
i_{X,Y}:X\to Y
be a holomorphic embedding of compact complex
manifolds. Let
N_{X/Y}
be the normal bundle to
X
in
Y
, i.e.,

(9.11)

N_{X/Y}=i^{*}_{X,Y}TY/TX.

First, we construct the deformation of
X
to the normal cone to

Y
as in
[
BGS90b
, Section
4]
.
Let
W=W_{X/Y}
be the blow-up of
Y\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
along
X\times\infty
. Then

X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
embeds in
W
. Let
P
be the exceptional divisor of the blow-up, i.e.,

(9.12)

P=\mathbf{P}\left(N_{X\times\infty/Y\times\mathbf{P}^{1}}\right).

Let
p_{X},p_{\infty}
be the projections
X\times\infty\to X,X\times\infty\to\infty
. Put

(9.13)

A=p_{X}^{*}N_{X/Y}\otimes p_{\infty}^{*}N^{-1}_{\infty/\mathbf{P}^{1}}.

Then

(9.14)

P=\mathbf{P}\left(A\oplus\mathbf{C}\right),

so that
P
is the projective completion of
A
, and its divisor at

\infty
is given by
\mathbf{P}\left(A\right)=\mathbf{P}\left(N_{X/Y}\right)
.

Let
\widetilde{Y}
be the blow-up of
Y
along
X
. Then

\mathbf{P}\left(N_{X/Y}\right)
is the exceptional divisor in

\widetilde{Y}
. Let
q_{W,Y}:W\to Y,q_{W,\mathbf{P}^{1}}:W\to\mathbf{P}^{1}
be the obvious maps.
For
z\in\mathbf{P}^{1}
, put

(9.15)

Y_{z}=q^{-1}_{W,\mathbf{P}^{1}}z.

Then

(9.16)

\displaystyle Y_{z}=

\displaystyle Y\ \mathrm{if}\,z\neq\infty,

\displaystyle P\cup\widetilde{Y}\ \mathrm{if}\,z=\infty.

For
z=\infty
,
P
and
\widetilde{Y}
meet transversally along

\mathbf{P}\left(N_{X/Y}\right)
. Also the projection

q_{W,\mathbf{P}^{1}}
is a submersion except on

\mathbf{P}\left(N_{Y/X}\right)
where it has ordinary singularities.

Figure 1.

Let
U=\mathcal{O}_{P}\left(-1\right)
be the universal line bundle
on
P
. We have the exact sequence of holomorphic vector bundles
on
P
,

(9.17)

0\to U\to A\oplus\mathbf{C}\to\left(A\oplus\mathbf{C}\right)/U\to 0.

The image
\sigma
of
1\in\mathbf{C}
in
\left(A\oplus\mathbf{C}\right)/U
is a holomorphic section of

\left(A\oplus\mathbf{C}\right)/U
that vanishes exactly on
X\times\infty
. On

\mathbf{P}\left(A\right)
,
U
restricts to the corresponding universal line
bundle, the exact sequence (
9.17
) restricts to

(9.18)

0\to U\to A\oplus\mathbf{C}\to A/U\oplus\mathbf{C}\to 0,

and
\sigma
restricts to the section
1
of
A/U\oplus\mathbf{C}
.

Consider the Koszul complex

\left(\Lambda\left(\left(A+\mathbf{C}\right)/U\right)^{*},i_{\sigma}\right)
on
P
. This complex
provides a resolution of
i_{X\times\infty,P,*}\mathcal{O}_{X\times\infty}
. On

\mathbf{P}\left(N_{X/Y}\right)
, this complex is just the split
complex

\Lambda\left(A/U\right)^{*}\widehat{\otimes}\left(\Lambda\mathbf{C},i_{1}\right)
.

9.3.
A Riemann-Roch-Grothendieck theorem for embeddings

Consider the Bott-Chern class

\mathrm{Td}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(N_{X/Y}\right)\in H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
.

Let
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}\left(X\right)
, and let

i_{X,Y,*}\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}\left(Y\right)
be its direct image.

Theorem 9.2
.

The following identity holds:

(9.19)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(i_{X,Y,*}\mathscr{F}\right)=i_{X,Y,*}\frac{\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{F}\right)}{\mathrm{Td}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(N_{X/Y}\right)}\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(Y,\mathbf{R}\right).

Proof.

Let
i_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},W}:X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}\to W
be
the obvious embedding,
let
\pi_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}:X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}\to X
be
the natural projection.
Then
\pi_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}^{*}\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}\left(X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}\right)
24
24

24

By

[
St20
,
Tag 00R4
]
,
\pi_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}
is flat, so
that
\pi^{*}_{X\times\mathbf{P^{1},X}}\mathscr{F}=L\pi^{*}_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}\mathscr{F}
.
, so that

(9.20)

i_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},W,*}\pi_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}^{*}\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}\left(W\right).

Let

(9.21)

\displaystyle i_{Y,W}:Y=q^{-1}_{W,\mathbf{P}^{1}}\left(0\right)\to W,

\displaystyle i_{P,W}:P\to W,

\displaystyle i_{\widetilde{Y},W}:\widetilde{Y}\to W,

\displaystyle i_{X\times\infty,P}:X\times\infty\to P.

be the obvious
embeddings. Other embeddings will be denoted in the same way.

Note that
\pi_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}i_{X,X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}}

is the identity of
X
, so that

(9.22)

Li^{*}_{X,X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}}L\pi^{*}_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}\mathscr{F}\simeq\mathscr{F}\ \mathrm{in}\ \mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right).

Also
\pi_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}i_{X\times\infty,X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}}
is the identification of
X\times\infty
with

X
. When identifying these two spaces, we get

(9.23)

Li^{*}_{X\times\infty,X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}}L\pi^{*}_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}\mathscr{F}\simeq\mathscr{F}\ \mathrm{in}\ \mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right).

Observe that
Y=q_{W,\mathbf{P}^{1}}^{-1}\left(0\right)
and
P
are
transverse to
X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
. Using Proposition

9.1
, in which
X,Y,Z,\mathscr{F}
are replaced by

X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
,
Y
or
P
,
W
,

L\pi^{*}_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}\mathscr{F}
, and also
(
9.22
), (
9.23
), we get

(9.24)

\displaystyle\left(Li_{Y,W}^{*}\right)i_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},W*}\left(L\pi_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}^{*}\right)\mathscr{F}\simeq i_{X,Y,*}\mathscr{F}\ \mathrm{in}\ \mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(Y\right),

\displaystyle\left(Li_{P,W}^{*}\right)i_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},W,*}\left(L\pi_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}^{*}\right)\mathscr{F}\simeq i_{X\times\infty,P,*}\mathscr{F}\ \mathrm{in}\ \mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(P\right).

Using Theorem
8.14
and
(
9.24
),we get

(9.25)

\displaystyle i_{Y,W}^{*}\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(i_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},W,*}\left(L\pi_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}^{*}\right)\mathscr{F}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(i_{X,Y,*}\mathscr{F}\right)\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(Y,\mathbf{R}\right),

\displaystyle i_{P,W}^{*}\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(i_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},W,*}\left(L\pi_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}^{*}\right)\mathscr{F}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(i_{X\times\infty,P,*}\mathscr{F}\right)\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(P,\mathbf{R}\right).

Let
\alpha\in\Omega^{(=)}\left(W,\mathbf{R}\right)
be a smooth closed form on
W

representing

(9.26)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(i_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},W,*}\left(L\pi_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}^{*}\right)\mathscr{F}\right)\in H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(W,\mathbf{R}\right).

By
(
9.25
),
i_{Y,W}^{*}\alpha\in\Omega^{(=)}\left(Y,\mathbf{R}\right)
represents

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(i_{X,Y,*}\mathscr{F}\right)\in H_{\mathrm{BC}}^{(=)}\left(Y,\mathbf{R}\right)
, and
i_{P,W}^{*}\alpha\in\Omega^{(=)}\left(P,\mathbf{R}\right)
represents

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(i_{X\times\infty,P,*}\mathscr{F}\right)\in H_{\mathrm{BC}}^{(=)}\left(P,\mathbf{R}\right)
.

Let
z\in\mathbf{C}
be the canonical meromorphic coordinate on

\mathbf{P}^{1}
that vanishes at
0
. We have the Poincaré-Lelong
equation (
2.25
).

Let
\delta_{Y_{0}},\delta_{Y_{\infty}}
be the currents of
integration on
Y_{0},Y_{\infty}
. Since
q_{W,\mathbf{P}^{1}}

has ordinary singularities near
\mathbf{P}\left(N_{X/Y}\right)
,
there is a well-defined integrable current

q^{*}_{W,\mathbf{P}^{1}}\log\left(\left|z\right|^{2}\right)

on
W
, which is such that

(9.27)

\frac{\overline{\partial}^{W}\partial^{W}}{2i\pi}q^{*}_{W,\mathbf{P}^{1}}\log\left(\left|z\right|^{2}\right)=\delta_{Y_{0}}-\delta_{Y_{\infty}}.

For
z\in\mathbf{P}^{1}
, let
i_{z}
be the embedding
Y_{z}\to W
. By (
9.27
), we deduce that

(9.28)

\frac{\overline{\partial}^{W}\partial^{W}}{2i\pi}\left[\alpha q^{*}_{W,\mathbf{P}^{1}}\log\left(\left|z\right|^{2}\right)\right]=i_{0}^{*}\alpha\delta_{Y_{0}}-i_{\infty}^{*}\alpha\delta_{Y_{\infty}}.

Let
q_{Y_{\infty},Y}
be the restriction of
q_{W,Y}
to
Y_{\infty}
. By (
9.28
), we deduce the identity of currents on
Y
,

(9.29)

\frac{\overline{\partial}^{Y}\partial^{Y}}{2i\pi}q_{W,Y,*}\left[\alpha q^{*}_{W,\mathbf{P}^{1}}\log\left(\left|z\right|^{2}\right)\right]=i_{0}^{*}\alpha-q_{Y_{\infty},Y,*}i^{*}_{\infty}\alpha.

By the considerations in the paragraph following (
9.25
) and
by (
9.29
), we get

(9.30)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(i_{X,Y,*}\mathscr{F}\right)=\left\{q_{Y_{\infty},Y,*}i_{\infty}^{*}\alpha\right\}\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(Y,\mathbf{R}\right).

Let
q_{\widetilde{Y},Y},q_{P,Y}
be the restriction of
q_{Y_{\infty},Y}
to

\widetilde{Y},P
. Note that

(9.31)

q_{Y_{\infty},Y,*}i_{\infty}^{*}\alpha=q_{\widetilde{Y},Y,*}i_{\widetilde{Y},W}^{*}\alpha\\
+q_{P,Y,*}i_{P,W}^{*}\alpha.

Since
\widetilde{Y}\cap\left(X\times\infty\right)=\emptyset
, we find that

(9.32)

Li_{\widetilde{Y},W}^{*}i_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},W,*}\left(L\pi^{*}_{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1},X}\right)\mathscr{F}\simeq 0\ \mathrm{in}\ \mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(\widetilde{Y}\right).

Using Theorem
8.14
, the fact that
\left\{\alpha\right\}
is
given by (
9.26
), and (
9.32
),
we get

(9.33)

\left\{i^{*}_{\widetilde{Y},W}\alpha\right\}=0\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\widetilde{Y},\mathbf{R}\right),

so that

(9.34)

\left\{q_{\widetilde{Y},Y,*}i^{*}_{\widetilde{Y},W}\alpha\right\}=0\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(Y,\mathbf{R}\right).

Using again the considerations in the paragraph following
(
9.25
), (
9.31
), and (
9.34
), we obtain

(9.35)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(i_{X,Y,*}\mathscr{F}\right)=q_{P,Y*}\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(i_{X\times\infty,P,*}\mathscr{F}\right).

We will now evaluate the right-hand side of (
9.35
).

The Koszul complex
\left(\mathcal{O}_{P}\left(\Lambda\left(\left(A\oplus\mathbf{C}\right)/U\right)^{*}\right),i_{\sigma}\right)
is a projective resolution of
the sheaf
i_{X\times\infty,P,*}\mathcal{O}_{X\times\infty}
. By
the projection formula

[
St20
,
Tag
0B54
]
, we get

(9.36)

i_{X\times\infty,P,*}\mathscr{F}\simeq Lq_{P,X\times\infty}^{*}\mathscr{F}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{P}}^{\mathrm{L}}\left(\mathcal{O}_{P}\left(\Lambda\left(\left(A\oplus\mathbf{C}\right)/U\right)^{*}\right),i_{\sigma}\right)\ \mathrm{in}\ \mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(P\right).

By Theorems
8.14
,
8.15
and by (
9.36
), we deduce that

(9.37)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(i_{X\times\infty,P,*}\mathscr{F}\right)=q^{*}_{P,X\times\infty}\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{F}\right)\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\Lambda\left(\left(A\oplus\mathbf{C}\right)/U\right)^{*}\right).

By (
9.37
), we get

(9.38)

q_{P,Y,*}\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(i_{X\times\infty,P,*}\mathscr{F}\right)=i_{X,Y,*}\left[\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{F}\right)q_{P,X\times\infty,*}\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\Lambda\left(\left(A\oplus\mathbf{C}/U\right)^{*}\right)\right)\right].

Observe that

(9.39)

N_{\left(X\times\infty\right)/P}=A.

By
[
BGS90a
, eq. (2.8) in Theorem 2.5]
and by (
9.39
), we get

(9.40)

q_{P,X\times\infty,*}\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\Lambda\left(\left(A\oplus\mathbf{C}\right)/U\right)^{*}\right)=\mathrm{Td}_{\mathrm{BC}}^{-1}\left(A\right).

Recall that the choice of the coordinate
z
gives a canonical
trivialization of
N_{\infty/\mathbf{P}^{1}}
, so that by
(
9.13
), we have the canonical identification

(9.41)

A\simeq N_{X/Y}.

By (
9.35
), (
9.38
), and (
9.40
), we
get (
9.19
).
The
proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 9.3
.

We claim that Proposition
9.1
and Theorem

9.2
are compatible. Indeed, under the assumptions of
Proposition
9.1
, by Theorem
9.2
, we get

(9.42)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(i_{X,Z,*}\mathscr{F}\right)=i_{X,Z,*}\frac{\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{F}\right)}{\mathrm{Td}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(N_{X/Z}\right)}\ \mathrm{in}\ H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(Z,\mathbf{R}\right).

If
\beta\in\Omega^{(=)}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
is a smooth form
representing
\frac{\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{F}\right)}{\mathrm{Td}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(N_{X/Z}\right)}
, the right-hand
side of (
9.42
) is represented by the current

\beta\delta_{X}
on
Z
.

If
X
and
Y
are transverse, as explained in Subsection

2.3
,

i_{Y,Z}^{*}\beta\delta_{X}
is a
well-defined current on
Y
, and it represents
i_{Y,Z}^{*}i_{X,Z,*}\frac{\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{F}\right)}{\mathrm{Td}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(N_{X/Z}\right)}
in

H_{\mathrm{BC}}^{(=)}\left(Y,\mathbf{R}\right)
. Moreover, one has the
equality of currents on
Y
,

(9.43)

i_{Y,Z}^{*}\left(\beta\delta_{X}\right)=\left(i^{*}_{U,X}\beta\right)\delta_{U}.

By Theorem
8.14
and by (
9.4
), the
Bott-Chern class of the current in the right-hand side of
(
9.43
) is just
i_{U,Y,*}\frac{\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(Li^{*}_{U,X}\mathscr{F}\right)}{\mathrm{Td}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(N_{U/Y}\right)}
.
Using again Theorem
9.2
for the embedding
i_{U,Y}
, we
find that when taking the Chern character of both sides of
(
9.5
), we get a known equality.

9.4.
The uniqueness of the Chern character

First, we recall a version of a result of Grivaux
[
Gri10
, Theorem 2]
.

Theorem 9.4
.

There is at most a unique morphism of groups
\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}:K(X)\to H^{(=)}_{\rm BC}(X,\mathbf{R})
such that

(1)

If
E
is a holomorphic vector bundle,

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(E\right)

is defined as in Subsection
2.4
.

(2)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}
is functorial under pull-backs.

(3)

{\rm ch_{BC}}
verifies Riemann-Roch-Grothendieck
with respect to embeddings.

The Chern character
\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}
constructed in Definition

8.13
verifies the above uniqueness conditions.

Proof.

According to a modified version of Grivaux
[
Gri10
]
, for
uniqueness, we only need to check that
if
X
is a compact complex manifold, if
Y
is a compact complex
submanifold of
X
, and if
\sigma:\widetilde{X}\to X
is the
blow-up of
X
along
Y
, then

\sigma^{*}:H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)\to H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\widetilde{X},\mathbf{R}\right)
is injective. Since the exceptional
divisor in
\widetilde{X}
is negligible, if
\alpha\in\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
,

(9.44)

\int_{\widetilde{X}}\sigma^{*}\alpha=\int_{X}\alpha.

By (
9.44
), we deduce that if
\alpha\in\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
, we have the identity of currents on
X
,

(9.45)

\sigma_{*}\sigma^{*}\alpha=\alpha.

From (
9.45
), we conclude that on

H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
,
\sigma_{*}\sigma^{*}
is the identity,
and so
\sigma^{*}
is injective. This proves the uniqueness of

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}
.

By construction, if
E
is a holomorphic vector bundle on
X
, our

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(E\right)
is exactly the classical Chern character
obtained in Subsection
2.4
. By Theorems
8.14

and
9.2
, our
\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}
verifies the above two remaining
conditions.

The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 9.5
.

The original unicity theorem of Grivaux would require that we
prove if
E
is a holomorphic vector bundle on
X
, and if

\pi:P\to X
is the total space of the
projectivization
\mathbf{P}\left(E\right)
, then

\pi^{*}:H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)\to H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(P,\mathbf{R}\right)
is injective. We do not
need to check this (which would be trivial here), because

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}
is known on all holomorphic vector bundles.

If
H^{(=)}_{\rm BC}(X,\mathbf{Q})
is the rational Bott-Chern
cohomology
[
Sc07
, Section 4.e]
, Wu
[
W20
]
has
constructed
{\rm ch_{BC,\mathbf{Q}}}:K(X)\to H^{(=)}_{\rm BC}(X,\mathbf{Q})
a rational Chern character. Let
\iota:H^{(=)}_{\rm BC}(X,\mathbf{Q})\to H^{(=)}_{\rm BC}(X,\mathbf{R})
be
the canonical map
[
Sc07
, Section 7.a]
.

Corollary 9.6
.

The following identity holds:

(9.46)

\displaystyle\iota{\rm ch_{BC,\mathbf{Q}}}={\rm ch_{BC}}.

Proof.

By
[
W20
, Theorem 2]
,
\iota{\rm ch_{BC,\mathbf{Q}}}
satisfies
the uniqueness conditions of Theorem
9.4
, and so
(
9.46
) holds.
∎

10.
Submersions and elliptic superconnections

The purpose of this Section is to state our main result in the case
of a submersion
p:M=X\times S\to S
, and to define infinite
dimensional
superconnection forms on
S
, which will be used to establish this main
result.

This Section is organized as follows. In Subsection
10.1
,
we state our main result, that involves
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(M\right)
.

In Subsection
10.2
, using the results of Section

6
, we show that in the proof,
\mathscr{F}
can be
replaced by
\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{B}\left(M\right)
. We will then
view
p_{*}\mathscr{E}
as equipped with an antiholomorphic
superconnection
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}\prime\prime}
.

In Subsection
10.3
, given a splitting
\mathscr{E}\simeq\mathscr{E}_{0}
, and Hermitian metrics
g^{TX},g^{D}
on

TX,D
, we obtain the adjoint
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime}
of

A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime}
.

In Subsection
10.4
, we construct natural connections on

T_{\mathbf{R}}X
.

In Subsection
10.5
, a Lichnerowicz formula is
established for
the curvature of
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}}
, when
S
is
reduced to a point.

In Subsection
10.6
, we give a Lichnerowicz formula for

A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}
by taking a proper adiabatic limit of
the corresponding formula in the case of a single fiber. This
curvature is a second order elliptic differential operator along the
fibers
X
.

Finally, in Subsection
10.7
, by imitating the constructions of
Section
8
in an infinite-dimensional context, we
obtain infinite-dimensional Chern character forms

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},g^{D}\right)
, and we
show that they have the same formal properties as the forms

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},h\right)
that were considered in
Section
8
. In particular, their class in Bott-Chern
cohomology does not depend on the metric data.

10.1.
A theorem of Riemann-Roch-Grothendieck for submersions

Let
X,S
be compact complex manifolds of dimension
n,n^{\prime}
. Put

(10.1)

M=X\times S.

Then
M
has dimension
m=n+n^{\prime}
.
Let
p:M\to S,q:M\to X
be the projections.
Then

(10.2)

TM=p^{*}TS\oplus q^{*}TX.

Let
\mathscr{F}
be an object in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(M\right)
. As we saw in
Subsection
3.4
, by a theorem by Grauert

[
Gr60
, Theorem 10.4.6]
,
