Rp_{*}\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(S\right)
.

The purpose of the next sections is to prove a special case of our general
result.

Theorem 10.1
.

The following identity holds:

(10.3)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(Rp_{*}\mathscr{F}\right)=p_{*}\left[q^{*}\mathrm{Td}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(TX\right)\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{F}\right)\right]\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right).

Remark 10.2
.

We will exploit as much
as possible the fact that
M
is product. However, the methods of

[
B13
]
can also be used to give a direct proof of Theorem

10.1
when
p
is an arbitrary holomorphic submersion.

10.2.
Replacing
\mathscr{F}
by
\mathscr{E}

Let
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(M\right)
. By Theorem
6.7
,
there exists
\mathscr{E}=\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right)\in\mathrm{B}\left(M\right)
and a
quasi-isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-complexes
\phi:\mathscr{E}\to\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}
, so that

\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{H}\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{\infty}=\mathscr{H}\mathscr{F}
. To prove our
Theorem, we may as well assume that
\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{E}
.

We will use the notation of Sections
5
and

7
. In particular
D
denotes the diagonal vector bundle
on
M
which is associated with
E
.

Note that
p_{*}\mathscr{E}
is a

\Omega^{0,\scriptsize\bullet}\left(S,\mathbf{C}\right)
-module, and
A^{E\prime\prime}
can be viewed as an antiholomorphic superconnection

A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}\prime\prime}
on
p_{*}\mathscr{E}
.
The difference with respect to Subsections

5.1
–
5.3
is that
p_{*}\mathscr{E}
is infinite-dimensional.

As we saw in Subsection
3.1
, a
theorem of Grauert
[
GrR84
, Theorem 10.4.6]
asserts
that
Rp_{*}\mathscr{E}
is an object in

\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(S\right)
. Also by (
3.11
),

(10.4)

Rp_{*}\mathscr{E}=p_{*}\mathscr{E}.

Therefore the
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-complex
\left(p_{*}\mathscr{E},A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}\prime\prime}\right)
defines an object in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(S\right)
. This result will
be given a direct proof in Theorem
11.6
.

Let
i
be the embedding of the fibers
X
into
M
. By
Propositions
5.11
and
6.10
, the pull-back

i^{*}_{b}\mathscr{E}=\left(i^{*}_{b}E,A^{i^{*}_{b}E\prime\prime}\right)
is a family of
antiholomorphic superconnections along the fibers
X
, whose
corresponding associated element in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(X\right)
is just

Li^{*}\mathscr{E}
.

Let

\mathcal{D}
be the diagonal bundle associated with
p_{*}\mathscr{E}
. Then

(10.5)

\mathcal{D}=p_{*}i_{b}^{*}\mathscr{E},

and the corresponding

A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime}
(which is an
analogue of
v_{0}
for
p_{*}\mathscr{E}
) is given by

(10.6)

A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime}=A^{i^{*}_{b}E\prime\prime}.

The projection
P
defined in
Subsection
5.1
is given here by
i^{*}_{b}:p_{*}\mathscr{E}\to\mathcal{D}=p_{*}i^{*}_{b}\mathscr{E}
.

By (
10.2
), we get

(10.7)

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}M}\right)=p^{*}\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}S}\right)\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right).

By (
5.1
), (
10.7
), we obtain

(10.8)

E_{0}=p^{*}\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}S}\right)\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D.

Note that

(10.9)

i^{*}_{b}E_{0}=q^{*}\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D.

Over
X\times S
, we fix splittings as in (
5.2
),
(
5.3
), so that

(10.10)

E\simeq E_{0}.

These splittings induce corresponding splittings of
i_{b}^{*}E
, and we
have corresponding isomorphism,

(10.11)

i^{*}_{b}E\simeq i^{*}_{b}E_{0}.

By (
10.11
), we get the non-canonical isomorphism

(10.12)

\mathcal{D}=\Omega^{0,\scriptsize\bullet}\left(X,D|_{X}\right).

Put

(10.13)

p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}=\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}S}\right)\widehat{\otimes}p_{*}i_{b}^{*}\mathscr{E}.

By (
10.5
), (
10.8
), and (
10.9
), we
have the identification
25
25

25

It would be more appropriate here
to introduce the sub-filtration of

associated with

p^{*}\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}S}\right)
.
,

(10.14)

p_{*}\mathscr{E}\simeq p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}.

To keep in line with the previous notation, we will denote

A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}\prime\prime}

via the isomorphism (

).

Using (

), we get

(10.15)

A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime}=\nabla^{D\prime\prime}+B.

10.3.
The adjoint of
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime}

Since

, then
\nabla^{D\prime\prime}
splits as

\nabla^{D\prime\prime}=\nabla^{D\prime\prime,X}+\nabla^{D\prime\prime,S},

.

Let

, and let

-form along the
fibers

.
26
26

26

To simplify the presentation, we

.

. If

, if

Here, we choose the

(10.17)

\omega^{X}\left(U,V\right)=\left\langle U,J^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}V\right\rangle.

Let

denote the metric

on

-graded vector bundle

We equip

Definition 10.3
.

If
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}=\Omega^{0,\scriptsize\bullet}\left(X,D|_{X}\right)
, put

(10.18)

\alpha\left(s,s^{\prime}\right)=\left(2\pi\right)^{-n}\int_{X}\left\langle s,s^{\prime}\right\rangle_{g^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}\otimes g^{D}}dx.

Then
\alpha
is a Hermitian product on

\mathcal{D}
, that defines a pure metric in
\mathscr{M}^{\mathcal{D}}
.

Here we use the notation of Subsection
7.1
, where the adjoint
of an antiholomorphic superconnection with respect to a Hermitian
metric was
defined.

Definition 10.4
.

Let

A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime}
denote the
adjoint of
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime}
with respect to

\alpha
.

Although
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
and
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime}
are essentially the same
object, their adjoints
A^{E_{0}\prime}
and
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime}
are distinct. In particular, the metric
g^{TX}
plays no role
in the definition of

. Still, we will show how to

from

.

If
H
is a smooth section of

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}M}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
,

still denotes the adjoint of

associated with the metric

, as defined in Subsection

.

Then

can be viewed as a smooth section of

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}S}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right).

Definition 10.5
.

Let
H^{*}_{\alpha}
denote the adjoint of
H
with respect to the
metric
\alpha
on
\mathcal{D}
.

We use the conventions of Subsection
4.5
, with
V=TX,g^{V^{*}}=g^{T^{*}X}
.
Then

c
is a map
\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}M\right)\to\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}S\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{R}}c\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}X\right)
.
We extend
c
to a map

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}M\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)\to\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}S\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{R}}c\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{R}}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right).

Also we use the
identification in (
4.43
),

(10.19)

c\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}X\right)\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{C}=\mathrm{End}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\right).

Proposition 10.6
.

If
H
is a smooth section of

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}M}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
, then

(10.20)

H^{*}_{\alpha}={}^{c}H^{*}.

Proof.

We may as well assume that
D
is trivial. We use the notation in
(
4.42
). If
f\in T^{*}X
,
\overline{f}
and

i_{f_{*}}
are adjoint to each other with respect to the metric
of
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
. From
(
4.42
), we get (
10.20
).
∎

Recall that

\nabla^{D\prime}
was defined after (
7.14
). As in (
10.16
), we have the splitting

(10.21)

\nabla^{D\prime}=\nabla^{D\prime,X}+\nabla^{D\prime,S}.

We can extend

\nabla^{D\prime,S}
to smooth sections of

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}S\right)\widehat{\otimes}\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D
.

Let

\nabla^{D\prime\prime,X*}_{\alpha}
denote the formal adjoint of
\nabla^{D\prime\prime,X}
with respect to
\alpha
. Then
\nabla^{D\prime\prime,X*}_{\alpha}
is a classical
Hermitian adjoint.

Proposition 10.7
.

The following identity holds:

(10.22)

A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime}=\nabla^{D\prime,S}+\nabla^{D\prime\prime,X*}_{\alpha}+{}^{c}B^{*}.

Proof.

This is a consequence of (
10.15
), (
10.16
),
and of Proposition
10.6
.
∎

Definition 10.8
.

Put

(10.23)

A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}}=A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime}+A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime}.

Then
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}}
is a superconnection on
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}
.

Put

(10.24)

\nabla^{D,S}=\nabla^{D\prime\prime,S}+\nabla^{D\prime,S}.

By (
7.21
), (
10.15
), and (
10.22
), we get

(10.25)

A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}}=\nabla^{D,S}+\nabla^{D\prime\prime,X}+\nabla^{D\prime\prime,X*}+{}^{c}C.

Let

A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime*}
denote the standard fiberwise

L_{2}
adjoint of
A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime}
. We use the notation

(10.26)

A^{\mathcal{D}}=A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime}+A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime*}.

10.4.
Some connections on
TX

Put

(10.27)

\beta=\frac{1}{2}\left(\overline{\partial}^{X}-\partial^{X}\right)i\omega^{X}.

Then
\beta
is a smooth section of

\Lambda^{3}\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}X\right)
.

Let

\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X,\mathrm{LC}}
be the Levi-Civita connection on
T_{\mathbf{R}}X
, and
let

\nabla^{TX}
be the Chern connection on
TX
. Let
\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
be
the connection on
T_{\mathbf{R}}X
which is induced by
\nabla^{TX}
. The connection

\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X,\mathrm{LC}}
preserves
TX
if and only if
g^{TX}
is Kähler,
and in this case, it coincides with
\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
.

Let

\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}
be the connection on

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
which is induced by
\nabla^{TX}
.
This connection preserves the
\mathbf{Z}
-grading.

Let

\vartheta
be the canonical
1
-form on
X
with values in

T_{\mathbf{R}}X
that corresponds to the identity. Let

\tau
denote the
torsion of
\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
. Then
\tau
is the sum of a

\left(2,0\right)
-form
\tau^{1,0}
with values in
TX
, and of a

\left(0,2\right)
-form
\tau^{0,1}
with values in
\overline{TX}
.

Let
\left\langle\tau\wedge,\vartheta\right\rangle
be the
antisymmetrization of the tensor
\left\langle\tau,\vartheta\right\rangle
. By

[
B89b
, Proposition 2.1]
, or by (
4.46
), we get

(10.28)

\left\langle\tau\wedge_{,}\vartheta\right\rangle=-2\beta.

Also equation (
10.28
) determines
\tau
. More precisely,

(10.29)

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}^{X}i\omega^{X}=-\left\langle\tau^{0,1}\wedge,\vartheta^{1,0}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}=\left\langle\tau^{1,0}\wedge,\vartheta^{0,1}\right\rangle.

By (
4.47
), the connection
\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X,-1/2}
on
T_{\mathbf{R}}X
associated with
\nabla^{TX}
is such that if
U,V\in T_{\mathbf{R}}X
, then

(10.30)

\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X,-1/2}_{U}V=\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}_{U}V-\frac{1}{2}\tau\left(U,V\right).

As we saw at the end of Subsection
4.6
,
\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X,-1/2}
induces a connection
\nabla^{TX,-1/2}
on
TX
,
and the analogue of (
4.59
) holds.

Here, we follow
[
B89b
, Section 2 b)]
.

Definition 10.9
.

Let

\overline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X},\underline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
be the
Euclidean connections on

T_{\mathbf{R}}X
whose torsions
\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau}
are
such that the tensors
\left\langle\overline{\tau},\vartheta\right\rangle,\left\langle\underline{\tau},\vartheta\right\rangle

are totally
antisymmetric, and moreover,

(10.31)

\displaystyle\left\langle\overline{\tau}\wedge_{,}\vartheta\right\rangle=2\beta,

\displaystyle\left\langle\underline{\tau}\wedge_{,}\vartheta\right\rangle=-2\beta,

and let

\overline{R}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X},\underline{R}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}

be their curvatures.

By
[
B89b
, Proposition 2.5]
, the connection

\overline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
preserves the complex structure, i.e., it
induces a corresponding Hermitian connection

\overline{\nabla}^{TX}
on

TX
.

Let
A

be the
1
-form on
X
with values in antisymmetric sections of

\mathrm{End}\left(T_{\mathbf{R}}X\right)
such that if
U,V,W\in T_{\mathbf{R}}X
, then

(10.32)

\left\langle A\left(U\right)V,W\right\rangle=-\left\langle\tau\left(U,V\right),W\right\rangle+\left\langle\tau\left(U,W\right),V\right\rangle.

From the properties of
\tau
given before, we find that
for
U\in T_{\mathbf{R}}X
,
A\left(U\right)
is a complex endomorphism of

T_{\mathbf{R}}X
. By
[
B90
, Proposition 2.5 and eq.
(2.37)]
, we have the identity

(10.33)

\overline{\nabla}^{TX}=\nabla^{TX}+A.

For
t>0
, if
\omega^{X}
is replaced by
\omega^{X}/t
,
\beta

is replaced by
\beta/t
. However, the above connections are
unchanged. Also if
\omega^{X}
is closed, all the
considered connections on
T_{\mathbf{R}}X
coincide with
\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
.

By following
[
B89b
, Section 2 b)]
and
[
B13
, Section
2.5]
, let us describe the connection
\underline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
in
more detail.
Put

(10.34)

F=T^{*}X\oplus TX

Then
F
is a holomorphic Hermitian vector bundle on

X
. Let
\nabla^{F}=\nabla^{T^{*}X}\oplus\nabla^{TX}
denote the corresponding
Chern connection.

Let
b
be the smooth section of
\overline{T^{*}X}\otimes\mathrm{Hom}\left(TX,T^{*}X\right)
such that if
u,v\in TX
,then

(10.35)

b\left(\overline{u}\right)v=i\partial^{X}\omega^{X}\left(\overline{u},v,\cdot\right).

Put

(10.36)

\mathfrak{b}=\left(g^{TX}\right)^{-1}\overline{b\left(g^{TX}\right)^{-1}}.

Then
\mathfrak{b}
is a section of
T^{*}X\otimes\mathrm{Hom}\left(T^{*}X,TX\right)
.

Let

\underline{\nabla}^{F}
be the connection on
F
,

(10.37)

\underline{\nabla}^{F}=\begin{bmatrix}\nabla^{T^{*}X}&b\\
\mathfrak{b}&\nabla^{TX}\end{bmatrix}.

Then
g^{\overline{TX}}

identifies
\overline{TX}
with
T^{*}X
, so that

(10.38)

F\simeq T_{\mathbf{C}}X=\overline{TX}\oplus TX.

In
[
B89b
, Theorem 2.9]
,
[
B13
, Theorem 2.5.3]
, it was shown that via the identification
F\simeq T_{\mathbf{C}}X
, the connection
\underline{\nabla}^{F}
is real and it induces the Euclidean
connection
\underline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
on
T_{\mathbf{R}}X
. If
\underline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{C}}X}
denote the corresponding
connection on
T_{\mathbf{C}}X
, by (
10.37
), we get

(10.39)

\underline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{C}}X}=\begin{bmatrix}\nabla^{\overline{TX}}&\left(g^{\overline{TX}}\right)^{-1}b\\
\left(g^{TX}\right)^{-1}\overline{b}&\nabla^{TX}\end{bmatrix}.

By (
10.39
), we have the confirmation that

\underline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
is unchanged when replacing
\omega^{X}

by
\omega^{X}/t
.

By (
10.37
), we get

(10.40)

\underline{\nabla}^{F\prime\prime}=\begin{bmatrix}\nabla^{T^{*}X\prime\prime}&b\\
0&\nabla^{TX\prime\prime}\end{bmatrix}.

If
\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}=0
,
by
[
B89b
, Theorem 2.7]
,
[
B13
, Theorem 2.5.3]
,

\underline{\nabla}^{F\prime\prime}
induces a new holomorphic
structure on
F
. We have the exact sequence of holomorphic vector
bundles,

(10.41)

0\to T^{*}X\to F\to TX\to 0.

Equivalently,
\underline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{C}}X\prime\prime}
defines a holomorphic structure on
T_{\mathbf{C}}X
, and
the exact sequence (
10.41
)
can be rewritten in the form,

(10.42)

0\to\left(\overline{TX},\nabla^{\overline{TX}\prime\prime}\right)\to\left(T_{\mathbf{C}}X,\underline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{C}}X\prime\prime}\right)\to\left(TX,\nabla^{TX\prime\prime}\right)\to 0.

In (
10.42
),
\nabla^{\overline{TX}\prime\prime}=\overline{\nabla^{TX\prime}}
depends on the metric
g^{TX}
.

If
\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}=0
, then
\underline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{C}}X\prime\prime}
is a holomorphic
structure, so that

\underline{R}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
is of type
(1,1)
.
By
[
B89b
, Theorem 1.6]
,
[
B13
, Theorem
2.5.6]
, under the same assumption, if
A,B,C,D\in T_{\mathbf{R}}X
, then

(10.43)

\left\langle\overline{R}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}\left(A,B\right)C,D\right\rangle=\left\langle\underline{R}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}\left(C,D\right)A,B\right\rangle.

Equation (
10.43
) is compatible with the fact that

\overline{R}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
takes its values in complex endomorphisms
of
T_{\mathbf{R}}X
.

Let

\overline{\nabla}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}
be the connection on
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
induced by the unitary connection
\overline{\nabla}^{TX}
on
TX
.

Assume for the moment that
X
is spin, or equivalently that the line
bundle
\det TX
has a square root
\lambda
. Then
\lambda
is a
holomorphic Hermitian line bundle. Let
\nabla^{\lambda}
be the
corresponding Chern connection. Put

(10.44)

S^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}=\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\otimes\lambda^{-1}.

Then
S^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
is the Hermitian vector bundle of

\left(T_{\mathbf{R}}X,\,g^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}\right)
-spinors associated with the
underlying spin structure, the positive spinors
corresponding to even forms, and the negative spinors to odd
forms. Also
S^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
inherits a Hermitian
connection
\nabla^{S^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X},\mathrm{LC}}
corresponding to
\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X,\mathrm{LC}}
, that
preserves its
\mathbf{Z}_{2}
-grading. Let

\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right),\mathrm{LC}}
denote the connection on

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
that is induced by

\nabla^{S^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}},\nabla^{\lambda}
. The connection

\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right),\mathrm{LC}}
preserves the

\mathbf{Z}_{2}
-grading of
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
.

The above considerations still make sense even if
X
is not spin, since locally, a square
root
\lambda
always exists.

By proceeding the way we did for
\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X,\mathrm{LC}}
, we define the connection

\overline{\nabla}_{s}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}
to be the
connection induced by
\overline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X},\nabla^{\lambda}
on

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
.

Let
e_{1},\ldots,e_{2n}
be an orthonormal basis of
T_{\mathbf{R}}X
, and
let
e^{1},\ldots,e^{2n}
be the corresponding dual basis of

T^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}X
. Let
{}^{c}A
be the section of

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}c\left(T_{\mathbf{R}}X\right)
given by

(10.45)

{}^{c}A=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle Ae_{i},e_{j}\right\rangle{}^{c}e_{i}{}^{c}e_{j}.

By equation
(
10.33
), we get

(10.46)

\overline{\nabla}_{s}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}=\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}+{}^{c}A.

Observe that
i_{\cdot}\beta
is a
1
-form with values
in
\Lambda^{2}\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}X\right)
, so that
{}^{c}i_{\cdot}\beta

is a
1
-form with values in

\mathrm{End}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\right)
.
By
[
B89b
, eq. (2.34)]
and by (
10.28
), we
have the identity
28
28

28

When
\omega^{X}
is replaced by

\omega^{X}/t
,
\beta
is replaced by
\beta/t
. However,
c
now depends on
t
. When suitably
interpreted, (
10.47
) does not depend on the scaling.

(10.47)

\overline{\nabla}_{s}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}=\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right),\mathrm{LC}}+{}^{c}\left(i_{\cdot}\beta\right).

In general, the connections

\overline{\nabla}_{s}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}
and

\overline{\nabla}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}

do not
coincide. More precisely, we have the identity
29
29

29

This point was clear in
[
B89b
]
, and was only
stated implicitly in
[
B12
, eq. (3.72)]
. Still observe that
these two connections induce the same connection on

\mathrm{End}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\right)
.

(10.48)

\overline{\nabla}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}=\overline{\nabla}_{s}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}^{TX}\left[A\right].

Let

\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D,\mathrm{LC}}
be the
connection on
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D
that is
induced by
\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right),\mathrm{LC}},\nabla^{D}
.
Let

\overline{\nabla}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D}

denote the connection on
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D
which is associated with

\overline{\nabla}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)},\nabla^{D}
.

10.5.
A Lichnerowicz formula for
A^{\mathcal{D},2}

In this Subsection, we assume that
S
is reduced to a point.
Let

D^{X,\mathrm{LC}}
denote the classical Dirac operator acting on

C^{\infty}\left(X,\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D\right)
. Namely, if

e_{1},\ldots,e_{2n}
is a basis
T_{\mathbf{R}}X
, and if

e^{1},\ldots,e^{2n}
is the corresponding dual basis of

T^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}X
, then

(10.49)

D^{X,\mathrm{LC}}={}^{\mathrm{c}}e^{i}\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D,\mathrm{LC}}_{e_{i}}.

Now we assume that
e_{1},\ldots,e_{2n}
is an orthonormal basis of

T_{\mathbf{R}}X
. Let

K^{X}
be the scalar curvature of
X
, and let
R^{D}
be the
curvature of
\nabla^{D}
. By Lichnerowicz formula, we get

(10.50)

D^{X,\mathrm{LC},2}=-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{e_{i}}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D,\mathrm{LC},2}+\frac{1}{8}K^{X}+{}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(R^{D}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}\left[R^{TX}\right]\right).

In the right-hand side of (
10.50
),

\nabla_{e_{i}}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D,\mathrm{LC},2}

is our notation for the Bochner Laplacian. More precisely if

e_{1},\ldots,e_{2n}
is a locally defined orthonormal basis of

T_{\mathbf{R}}X
, then

(10.51)

\nabla_{e_{i}}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D,\mathrm{LC},2}=\sum\nabla_{e_{i}}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D,\mathrm{LC},2}-\nabla_{\sum\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X,\mathrm{LC}}_{e_{i}}e_{i}}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D,\mathrm{LC}}

Other Bochner Laplacians will be denoted in the same way, with the
same correction in the right-hand side.

Theorem 10.10
.

The following identities hold:

\displaystyle A^{\mathcal{D}}=D^{X,\mathrm{LC}}+{}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\beta+C\right),

\displaystyle A^{\mathcal{D},2}=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D,\mathrm{LC}}_{e_{i}}+{}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(i_{e_{i}}\left(\beta+C\right)\right)\right)^{2}+\frac{K^{X}}{8}

\displaystyle+^{\mathrm{c}}\left(A^{E,2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}\left[R^{TX}\right]-\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)+\left({}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\beta+C\right)\right)^{2}

(10.52)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum\left({}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(i_{e_{i}}\left(\beta+C\right)\right)\right)^{2}-{}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\left(\beta+C\right)^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\left({}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\beta+C\right)\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum\left({}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(i_{e_{i}}\left(\beta+C\right)\right)\right)^{2}-^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\left(\beta+C\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1<\ldots<i_{k}}\\
k\geq 2\end{subarray}}\left(-1\right)^{k\left(k+1\right)/2}\frac{\left(1-k\right)}{2^{k/2}}{}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\left(i_{e_{i_{1}}}\ldots i_{e_{i_{k}}}\left(\beta+C\right)\right)^{2}\right).

Proof.

We use
[
B89b
, Theorems 1.1, 1.3, and 2.2]
and
(
10.25
).
As explained in Subsection
4.5
, our normalization on the Clifford algebra
is different from the one
[
B89b
]
. Also
\beta+C
is an odd
section of
\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
, while
\mathrm{End}\left(D\right)
was absent in
[
B89b
, Theorem 2.3]
.
Still, for formal reasons, it is easy to check that the result of

[
B89b
]
remains correct. The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 10.11
.

Observe that
A^{\mathcal{D},2}=\left[A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime},A^{\mathcal{D}\prime}\right]
. The standard
consequences of Hodge theory hold true for

A^{\mathcal{D},2}
.

By (
10.47
), (
10.48
), we get

(10.53)

-\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D,\mathrm{LC}}_{e_{i}}+{}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(i_{e_{i}}\left(\beta+C\right)\right)\right)^{2}\\
=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\overline{\nabla}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D}_{e_{i}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}^{TX}\left[A\left(e_{i}\right)\right]+{}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(i_{e_{i}}C\right)\right)^{2}.

Equation (
10.53
) makes clear that as it should be, the
operator (
10.53
) is of total degree
0
. Equations
(
10.10
), (
10.53
) give a false sense of symmetry
between the roles of
\beta
and
C
. In particular both
c
and

\beta
do depend on
g^{TX}
.

10.6.
A Lichnerowicz formula for

A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}

Since
M=X\times S\to S
is product, the connections on
TX
defined in
Subsection
10.5
lift to
q^{*}TX
. Also the form

\beta
in (
10.27
) lifts to the form
q^{*}\beta
on
M
.

Let
A^{\mathcal{D},\mathrm{LC}}
denote the Levi-Civita
superconnection in the sense of
[
B86
]
,
[
BGS88b
, Section 2
a)]
,

(10.54)

A^{\mathcal{D},\mathrm{LC}}=\nabla^{D,S}+D^{X,\mathrm{LC}}.

Theorem 10.12
.

The following identities hold:

\displaystyle A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}}=A^{\mathcal{D},\mathrm{\mathrm{LC}}}+{}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(q^{*}\beta+C\right),

\displaystyle A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D,\mathrm{LC}}_{e_{i}}+{}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(i_{e_{i}}\left(q^{*}\beta+C\right)\right)\right)^{2}+\frac{K^{X}}{8}

\displaystyle+^{\mathrm{c}}\left(A^{E_{0},2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}\left[R^{TX}\right]-q^{*}\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)+\left({}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(q^{*}\beta+C\right)\right)^{2}

(10.55)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum\left({}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(i_{e_{i}}\left(q^{*}\beta+C\right)\right)\right)^{2}-{}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\left(q^{*}\beta+C\right)^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\left({}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(q^{*}\beta+C\right)\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum\left({}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(i_{e_{i}}\left(q^{*}\beta+C\right)\right)\right)^{2}-^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\left(q^{*}\beta+C\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1<\ldots<i_{k}}\\
k\geq 2\end{subarray}}\left(-1\right)^{k\left(k+1\right)/2}\frac{\left(1-k\right)}{2^{k/2}}{}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\left(i_{e_{i_{1}}}\ldots i_{e_{i_{k}}}\left(q^{*}\beta+C\right)\right)^{2}\right).

Proof.

By
(
10.25
), by the first identity in (
10.10
), and by
(
10.54
), we get the first identity in (
10.12
).

Let
U
be a small open set in
S
. We will view
U
as an open ball
in
\mathbf{C}^{n^{\prime}}
, and we replace
S\times X
by
U\times X
.
Given
\epsilon>0
, we equip
T\mathbf{C}^{n^{\prime}}
with the constant metric

g^{T\mathbf{C}^{n^{\prime}}}
which is
Kähler, and
TM
with the metric
p^{*}\frac{g^{T\mathbf{C}^{n^{\prime}}}}{\epsilon}\oplus q^{*}g^{TX}
. Let
\omega^{\mathbf{C}^{n^{\prime}}}
be the Kähler form of

\mathbf{C}^{n^{\prime}}
. If
f_{1},\ldots,f_{n^{\prime}}
is an orthonormal basis of

\mathbf{C}^{n^{\prime}}
, and if
f^{1},\ldots,f^{n^{\prime}}
is the corresponding dual
basis, then

(10.56)

\omega^{\mathbf{C}^{n^{\prime}}}=-if^{\alpha}\overline{f}^{\alpha}.

Also
\sqrt{\epsilon}f_{1},\ldots,\sqrt{\epsilon}f_{n^{\prime}}
is an
orthonormal basis of
T\mathbf{C}^{n^{\prime}}
for the metric

g^{T\mathbf{C}^{n^{\prime}}}/\epsilon
.

Let
A^{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}
be the operator on
U\times X
,
which is analogue of the operator

A^{\mathcal{D}}

considered in Theorem
10.10
. Since
\omega^{\mathbf{C}^{n^{\prime}}}
is closed, the analogue
of
\beta
on
U\times X
is just
q^{*}\beta
. Using Theorem

10.10
, we get a Lichnerowicz formula for

A^{\mathcal{D},2}_{\epsilon}
. In this formula,
c
now
depends on
\epsilon
, and will instead be denoted

{}^{c_{\epsilon}}
. Also the adjoint of
f^{\alpha}
is now

\epsilon i_{f_{\alpha}}
. We will take a proper limit of this formula as

\epsilon\to 0
.

Set

(10.57)

\mathsf{A}^{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}=e^{-i\omega^{\mathbf{C}^{n^{\prime}}}/\epsilon}A^{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}e^{i\omega^{\mathbf{C}^{n^{\prime}}}/\epsilon}.

The effect of the conjugation is to change
\epsilon i_{\overline{f}_{\alpha}}
into
\epsilon i_{\overline{f}_{\alpha}}-f^{\alpha}
. In particular, as
\epsilon\to 0
,
after conjugation, we have the uniform convergence together with all the derivatives,

(10.58)

{}^{c_{\epsilon}}\left(q^{*}\beta+C\right)\to{}^{c}\left(q^{*}\beta+C\right).

By (
10.49
), by the first equation in (
10.10
), by
(
10.54
), and by (
10.58
), we find that as

\epsilon\to 0
,

(10.59)

\mathsf{A}^{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}\to A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}},

in the sense that the coefficients of the considered differential
operators and their derivatives of any order converge uniformly over
compact subsets.

Now we take the limit as
\epsilon\to 0
of the analogue of the
equation for
\mathsf{A}^{\mathcal{D},2}_{\epsilon}
one derives from
(
10.10
). It is now easy to take the limit as
\epsilon\to 0
of our equation for
\mathsf{A}^{\mathcal{D},2}_{\epsilon}
and to obtain (
10.12
). The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 10.13
.

Since the fibration
\pi:M\to S
is product, none of the
subtleties contained in

[
B86
,
BGS88b
,
B12
]
appears in our
formula.

10.7.
The elliptic superconnection forms

By equation (
10.12
), the operator
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}
is elliptic along the
fibers
X
, so that
\exp\left(-A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}\right)
is a
fiberwise trace class operator.

We will now imitate Definition
8.1
in an infinite
dimensional context.

Definition 10.14
.

Put

(10.60)

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},g^{D}\right)=\varphi\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}\right)\right].

Then
\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},g^{D}\right)
is a smooth even form on
S
.

Let

\mathscr{P}
be the collection of parameters
\omega^{X},g^{D}
. Let
d^{\mathscr{P}}
be the de Rham operator on
\mathscr{P}
.

The metric
\alpha
in (
10.18
) depends on
\omega^{X},g^{D}
.
Then
\alpha^{-1}d^{\mathscr{P}}\alpha
is a
1
-form with values in

\alpha
-self-adjoint endomorphisms.

Let
w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}
be an orthonormal basis of
TX
with respect
to
g^{TX}
. Using (
10.18
), and proceeding as in

[
BGS88c
, eq. (1.109) and Proposition 1.19]
, we get

(10.61)

\alpha^{-1}d^{\mathscr{P}}\alpha=-{}^{\mathrm{c}}d^{\mathscr{P}}i\omega^{X}+\frac{1}{2}d^{\mathscr{P}}i\omega^{X}\left(w_{i},\overline{w}_{i}\right)+\left(g^{D}\right)^{-1}d^{\mathscr{P}}g^{D}.

Now we have the obvious analogue of Theorem
8.2
.

Theorem 10.15
.

The form
\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},g^{D}\right)
lies in
\Omega^{(=)}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right)
, it is
closed, and its Bott-Chern cohomology class does not depend on

\omega^{X},g^{D}
, or on the splitting of
E
.
Also

\varphi\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\alpha^{-1}d^{\mathscr{P}}\alpha\exp\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}\right)\right]
is a

1
-form on
\mathscr{P}
with values in

\Omega^{(=)}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right)
, and moreover,

(10.62)

d^{\mathscr{P}}\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},g^{D}\right)\\
=-\frac{\overline{\partial}^{S}\partial^{S}}{2i\pi}\varphi\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\alpha^{-1}d^{\mathscr{P}}\alpha\exp\left(-A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}\right)\right].

Proof.

The proof of the fact that our forms are closed and lie in

\Omega^{(=)}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right)
is formally the same
as the proof of Theorem
8.2
. By proceeding as in the
proof of Theorem
8.2
, we get (
10.62
).

Let us now prove that the Bott-Chern class of

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},g^{D}\right)
does not depend on the splitting of

E
. Let
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}_{0}=\left(E^{\prime}_{0},A^{E^{\prime}_{0}\prime\prime}\right)
be another splitting of
E
. If
M^{\prime}=M\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
, and let
p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}
be the projections
M^{\prime}\to S,M^{\prime}\to M,M^{\prime}\to\mathbf{P}^{1}
. Consider

\mathscr{E}_{M^{\prime}}=p^{*}_{2,b}\mathscr{E}
, so that
\mathscr{E}_{M^{\prime}}=\left(E_{M^{\prime}},A^{E_{M^{\prime}}\prime\prime}\right)
. The diagonal bundle
associated with
\mathscr{E}_{M^{\prime}}
is
p_{2}^{*}D
. Given
z\in\mathbf{P}^{1}
, let
j_{z}
denote the embedding

M\times\left\{z\right\}\to M^{\prime}
. For any
z\in\mathbf{P}^{1}
,
then

j_{z,b}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{M^{\prime}}=\mathscr{E}
. Let
\mathscr{E}_{M^{\prime},0}=\left(E_{M^{\prime},0},A^{E_{M^{\prime},0}\prime\prime}\right)
be a splitting of

\mathscr{E}_{M^{\prime}}
such that
j^{*}_{0,b}\mathscr{E}^{\prime}_{0}=\mathscr{E}_{0}
, and
j^{*}_{\infty,b}\mathscr{E}_{M^{\prime},0}=\mathscr{E}^{\prime}_{0}
. Let
q
be the projection

M^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}=S\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
. We equip
p_{2}^{*}D
with the
metric
p_{2}^{*}g^{D}
. We will use our previous results on the form

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{\mathscr{E}_{M^{\prime},0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},p_{2}^{*}g^{D}\right)
. The pull-backs of this form
to
S\times 0
,
S\times\infty
are just

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},g^{D}\right),\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}^{\prime}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},g^{D}\right)
. Using the Poincaré-Lelong equation
(
2.25
), we get

(10.63)

\frac{\overline{\partial}^{S}\partial^{S}}{2i\pi}p_{1,*}\left[\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{q_{*}\mathscr{E}_{M^{\prime},0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},p_{2}^{*}g^{D}\right)p^{*}_{3}\log\left(\left|z\right|^{2}\right)\right]\\
=\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},g^{D}\right)-\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}^{\prime}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},g^{D}\right),

from which we get the last statement in our theorem.
∎

Definition 10.16
.

Let

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}\prime\prime}\right)\in H_{\mathrm{BC}}^{(=)}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right)
be the common Bott-Chern cohomology
class of the forms
\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},g^{D}\right)
.

11.
Elliptic superconnection forms and direct images

The purpose of this Section is to prove that, with the notation of
Section
10
,
\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}\prime\prime}\right)

coincides with
\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(Rp_{*}\mathscr{E}\right)
.

This Section is organized as follows. In Subsection

11.1
, we apply the technique of spectral truncation
of Subsection
8.10
to the infinite-dimensional
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}
. This will permit us to extend to
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}
results we had established before only for ordinary
antiholomorphic superconnections.

In Subsection
11.2
,
we prove the critical result that there exists a classical
antiholomorphic superconnection
\underline{\mathscr{E}}
on
S
such
that
p_{*}\mathscr{E}
and
\underline{\mathscr{E}}
are
quasi-isomorphic, the main point being that
\underline{\mathscr{E}}
is
finite-dimensional.

In Subsection
11.3
, we prove our main identity.

Finally, in Subsection
11.4
, we briefly consider the
case where
\mathscr{H}Rp_{*}\mathscr{E}
is locally free, in which
case our main result has a much simpler proof.

We make the same assumptions, and we use the same notation as in
Section
10
.

11.1.
Spectral truncations in infinite dimensions

We will now proceed in infinite dimensions as in Subsection

8.10
. Recall that
A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime*}
is
the standard
L_{2}
-adjoint of
A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime}
. Put

(11.1)

\mathbb{A}^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}}=A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime}+A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime*}.

Equation (
11.1
) is the analogue of equation
(
8.72
) for
\mathbb{A}^{E_{0}}
.

As in (
8.73
), we get

(11.2)

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}=\left[A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime*}\right],

\displaystyle\left[A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\mathbb{A}^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}\right]=0,\qquad\left[A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime*},\mathbb{A}^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}\right]=0.

The operator
A^{\mathcal{D},2}
being a second order elliptic
operator along the fibers
X
, it has discrete spectrum. As in
(
8.78
), we get

(11.3)

\mathrm{Sp}\,\mathbb{A}^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}=\mathrm{Sp}\,A^{\mathcal{D},2}.

As in (
8.80
), for
a>0
, set

(11.4)

U_{a}=\left\{s\in S,a\notin\mathrm{Sp}\,A^{\mathcal{D},2}\right\}.

Then
U_{a}
is open in
S
.

Definition 11.1
.

Over
U_{a}
, put

(11.5)

P_{a,-}=\frac{1}{2i\pi}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\mathbf{C}\\
\left|\lambda\right|=a\end{subarray}}\frac{d\lambda}{\lambda-\mathbb{A}^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}}.

Then
P_{a,-}
is a fiberwise smoothing projector acting on
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}
.

By
(
8.73
), we get

(11.6)

\displaystyle\left[A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},P_{a,-}\right]=0,

\displaystyle\left[A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime*},P_{a,-}\right]=0.

Put

(11.7)

P_{a,+}=1-P_{a,-}.

Then
P_{a,+}
is also a projector.

As in (
8.84
), we get

(11.8)

P_{a,\pm}=P_{a,\pm}^{0}+P_{a,\pm}^{(\geq 1)}.

Let

\mathcal{D}_{a,\pm}
be the direct sum of the eigenspaces of

A^{\mathcal{D},2}
for eigenvalues
\lambda>a
or
\lambda<a
. Then
P^{0}_{a,\pm}
is the
orthogonal projectors
\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}_{a,\pm}
. Also the
analogue of (
8.85
) holds. Also
\mathcal{D}_{a,-}
is a
finite-dimensional vector bundle on
U_{a}
.

Definition 11.2
.

Over
U_{a}
, put

(11.9)

p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,\pm}=P_{a,\pm}p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}.

Then
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,\pm}
is a subbundle of
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}
which is also a

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}S}\right)
-module. Let
i_{a,\pm}
be
the corresponding embedding in
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}
.

We use the
notation of Subsection
4.1
. We will establish an
analogue of Theorem
8.21
. The main difference is that here,

p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}
is infinite-dimensional.

Theorem 11.3
.

On
U_{a}
, we have a splitting of

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}S}\right)
-modules,

(11.10)

p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}=p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,+}\oplus p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,-}.

Moreover,

(11.11)

F^{p}p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,\pm}=p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,\pm}\cap F^{p}p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}.

Also
P_{a,\pm}
induces a filtered isomorphism of

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}S}\right)
-modules,

(11.12)

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}S}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{D}_{a,\pm}\simeq p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,\pm}.

As a
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}S}\right)
-module,
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,-}

verifies the conditions in (
4.6
), and the associated diagonal bundle
is
just
\mathcal{D}_{a,-}
. Also it is equipped with a splitting as in
(
5.2
), (
5.3
).

Moreover,

A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime}
preserves the smooth sections of

p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,\pm}
, and it induces an antiholomorphic
superconnection
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,\pm}\prime\prime}
on

p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,\pm}
.

On
U_{a}
,
P_{a,-}:\mathscr{E}_{0}\to\mathscr{E}_{0,a,-}
and

i_{a,-}=\mathscr{E}_{0,a,-}\to\mathscr{E}_{0}
are quasi-isomorphisms of

\mathcal{O}_{U_{a}}
-complexes, and
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,+}=0
. Finally, for
s\in U_{a}
, the complex

\mathcal{D}_{a,+,s}
is exact.

Proof.

The proof is essentially the same as the proof of Theorem

8.21
. With respect to that Theorem, here, only

D_{a,-}
is finite-dimensional. Let us now prove that if

s\in U_{a}
,
\mathcal{D}_{a,+}
is exact. By proceeding
as in the proof of Proposition
8.22
, i.e., using the
analogue of
(
8.94
), the part of degree
0
over

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}S}\right)
of
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}}_{0,a,+}
is just the restriction of
A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime}
to
\mathcal{D}_{a,+}
. By Hodge theory, the complex

\mathcal{D}_{a,+,s}
is exact. The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

When considering another pair of metrics
g^{TX},g^{D}
on
TX,D
,
the obvious analogue of Proposition
8.24
still holds.
Details are left to the reader.

As a consequence of Theorem
11.3
, we give another proof of
a theorem of Grauert
[
GrR84
, Theorem 10.4.6]
.

Theorem 11.4
.

The complex of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}

defines an element in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(S\right)
. Moreover, the Leray
spectral sequence associated with the filtration of
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}
by
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}S}\right)

degenerates at
E_{2}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
11.3
, over
U_{a}
,
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}
and
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,-}
are quasi-isomorphic. By Theorem

5.7
,
\mathscr{H}p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,-}
is a
coherent sheaf. This shows that
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}
defines an
object in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(S\right)
. By Theorem
5.7
, the
spectral sequence associated with
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,-}

degenerates at
E_{2}
. Also on
D_{a,+}
, we have the identity

(11.13)

1=\left[A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime},A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime*}\left[A^{\mathcal{D},2}\right]^{-1}\right].

Equation (
11.13
) shows that on
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,+}
,
the spectral sequence stops at
E_{1}
. This concludes the proof of
our theorem.
∎

Remark 11.5
.

In the context of the theory of determinant bundles of direct
images, Theorems
11.3
and
11.4
can be used as
a substitute of many arguments in

[
BGS88c
, Section 3]
, where non-projective
Kähler manifolds are also considered.

11.2.
The existence theorem

Recall that (
10.13
), (
10.14
)
hold, and that
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime}
is given by
(
10.15
).

Recall that if
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(M\right)
,

\mathscr{R}\mathscr{H}\mathscr{F}\in K\left(M\right)
was defined in (
8.62
).

As we saw in Theorem
11.4
,
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}
is an object in
\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(S\right)
.

Theorem 11.6
.

There exists a finite-dimensional antiholomorphic superconnection

\underline{\mathscr{E}}=\left(\underline{E},A^{\underline{E}\prime\prime}\right)
on
S
, and a morphism of

\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{S}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}S}\right)\right)
-modules
\phi:\underline{\mathscr{E}}\to p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}
which is a
quasi-isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-complexes, such that
for any
s\in S
,

\phi_{s}:\left(\underline{D},\underline{v}_{0}\right)_{s}\to\left(\mathcal{D},A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime}\right)_{s}
is a quasi-isomorphism.

We have the identity

(11.14)

\underline{\mathscr{E}}\simeq Rp_{*}\mathscr{E}\ \mathrm{in}\,\mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(S\right).

In particular, we have the identity,

(11.15)

\mathscr{R}\mathscr{H}\underline{\mathscr{E}}=p_{!}\left[\mathscr{R}\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}\right]\ \mathrm{in}\ K\left(S\right).

Proof.

By Proposition
6.12
, there exists an object

\underline{\mathscr{E}}
in
\mathrm{B}\left(S\right)
and a morphism of

\mathcal{O}^{\infty}_{S}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}S}\right)\right)
-modules

\phi:\underline{\mathscr{E}}\to p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}
, which is also
a quasi-isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-complexes. Using Theorem

11.3
, over
U_{a}
, if
\phi_{a}=P_{a,-}\phi
,

\phi_{a}
induces a quasi-isomorphism
\underline{\mathscr{E}}\to p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0,a,-}
.

Over
U_{a}
, we now deal with finite
dimensional antiholomorphic superconnections. By Proposition

6.8
, for
s\in U_{a}
,

\phi_{a}
induces a quasi-isomorphism
\underline{D}_{s}\to\mathcal{D}_{a,-,s}
. By Theorem
11.3
, for
s\in U_{a}
,
the complex
\mathcal{D}_{a,+,s}
is exact. Therefore, for
any
s\in U_{a}
,
\phi_{s}:\underline{D}_{s}\to\mathcal{D}_{s}

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Since
\phi
is a quasi-isomorphism, we get (
11.14
), from which (
11.15
) follows. The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 11.7
.

By equation (
11.14
) in Theorem
11.6
, if
\underline{E}^{\prime}
has the same properties as

\underline{E}
, then

(11.16)

\underline{\mathscr{E}}\simeq\underline{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}\ \mathrm{in}\ \mathrm{D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}}\left(S\right).

Using Theorem
6.9
and (
11.16
), we deduce that
there exists a quasi-isomorphism
\psi:\underline{\mathscr{E}}\to\underline{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}
. By Proposition
6.8
, this
just says that if
\underline{D},\underline{D^{\prime}}
are the
corresponding diagonal bundles on
S
, for any
s\in S
,

\psi_{s}:\underline{D}_{s}\to\underline{D}^{\prime}_{s}
is a
quasi-isomorphism.

11.3.
The Chern character of the direct
image

We use the notation of Theorem
11.6
. In particular

\underline{\mathscr{E}}
is taken as in this Theorem.

Theorem 11.8
.

The following identity holds:

(11.17)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}\prime\prime}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)\ \mathrm{in}\ H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right).

Proof.

The proof is an adaptation of the proof of Theorems

8.9
and
8.11
. We will use the notation in
Theorem
11.6
. For
t\in\mathbf{C}
, we form the cone

\mathscr{C}_{t}=\mathrm{cone}\left(\underline{\mathscr{E}},p_{*}\mathscr{E}\right)
associated with the
morphism
t\phi
, with underlying infinite-dimensional vector bundle
C_{t}
on

S
. Note that
\phi
induces a morphism
\underline{D}\to\mathcal{D}
. The diagonal bundle
\mathbf{D}
associated
with
\mathscr{C}_{t}
is given

\mathbf{D}_{t}=\mathrm{cone}\left(\underline{D},\mathcal{D}\right)
, where the morphism is induced by
t\phi_{0}
. Also

(11.18)

\mathbf{D}^{\scriptsize\bullet}_{t}=\underline{D}^{\scriptsize\bullet+1}\oplus\mathcal{D}^{\scriptsize\bullet}.

By Theorem
11.6
, for any
s\in S
, when
t\neq 0
,
\mathbf{D}_{t}
is
exact.

We fix splittings of
E,\underline{E}
as in (
5.2
),
(
5.3
). In the sequel, we may as well replace

\underline{\mathscr{E}},\mathscr{E}
by
\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{0},\mathscr{E}_{0}
. In particular
\mathscr{C}_{t}
also splits and can
be replaced by
\mathscr{C}_{t,0}
.

Let

g^{\underline{D}}
be
a Hermitian metric on
\underline{D}
. Let

g^{\mathbf{D}}
denote the direct sum
Hermitian metric on
\mathbf{D}
associated with

g^{\underline{D}},g^{\mathcal{D}}
. We equip
C_{t,0}

with the pure metric
g^{\mathbf{D}}
. Let

\square^{\mathbf{D}}_{t}
denote
the corresponding fiberwise Hodge Laplacian acting on

\mathbf{D}
. Since for any
s\in S
,
\mathbf{D}_{t}
is
exact,

\square^{\mathbf{D}}_{t,s}
is invertible, and it has a uniform positive
lower bound over
S
. Its heat kernel is again fiberwise
trace class.

Let
A^{C_{t,0}\prime\prime}
denote the antiholomorphic
superconnection on
C_{t}
that is associated with
A^{\underline{E}_{0}\prime\prime},A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},t\phi
.

Given
t\in\mathbf{C}
, we can still define the Chern form

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{C_{t,0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\underline{D}}\right)
, for which the analogue of
Theorem
10.15
holds, with a similar proof. Let

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{C_{t,0}\prime\prime}\right)\in H_{\mathrm{BC}}^{(=)}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right)
denote the
corresponding common Bott-Chern cohomology class.

For
T>0
, let
g_{T}^{D},g_{T}^{\underline{D}}
be the
metrics on
D,\underline{D}
constructed as in (
8.35
). Let

g^{\mathbf{D}}_{T}
be the metric on
\mathbf{D}
that is associated with the
metrics
\omega^{X}/T,g^{D}_{T},g^{\underline{D}}_{T}T^{-n-1}
. If

N^{\mathbf{D}}
is the number operator of
\mathbf{D}
, then

(11.19)

g^{\mathbf{D}}_{T}=T^{-n}g^{\mathbf{D}}T^{N^{\mathbf{D}}}.

The proof of (
11.19
) is elementary. The extra factor

T^{-n}
comes from the contribution of the volume form on
X
to
the Hermitian product on
\mathcal{D}
.
This normalizing factor does not contribute to the computation of the
adjoint
A^{C_{t,0}\prime}_{T}
of
A^{C_{t,0}\prime\prime}
.
By (
11.19
), if
\square^{\mathbf{D}}_{t,T}
denotes the
associated fiberwise Laplacian, then

(11.20)

\square^{\mathbf{D}}_{t,T}=T\square^{\mathbf{D}}_{t}.

We claim
that if
t\neq 0
, as
T\to+\infty
,

(11.21)

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{C_{t,0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X}/T,g_{T}^{D},g_{T}^{\underline{D}}T^{-n-1}\right)\to 0.

The proof is an infinite-dimensional analogue of equation
(
8.40
) in Theorem

8.9
, with
HD=0
which can be established by the methods
of
[
BGV92
, Theorems 9.19 and 9.23]
,
[
B97
, Theorems 9.5 and 9.6]
.

By (
11.21
), we deduce that for
t\neq 0
,

(11.22)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{C_{t,0}\prime\prime}\right)=0.

By taking
t\to 0
in (
11.22
), we get

(11.23)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{C_{0,0}\prime\prime}\right)=0.

As in (
8.56
), we have the identity

(11.24)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{C_{0,0}\prime\prime}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime}\right)-\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right).

By (
11.23
), (
11.24
), we get (
11.17
). The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 11.9
.

By Theorem
8.12
and Remark
11.7
, we already
know that
\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)
does not
depend on the choice of
\underline{E}
. Theorem

11.8
gives a direct proof of this fact.

Theorem 11.10
.

The following identity holds:

(11.25)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}\prime\prime}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(Rp_{*}\ \mathscr{E}\right)\ \mathrm{in}\ H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right).

Proof.

Using equation (
11.14
) in Theorem
11.6
and
Theorem
11.8
, we get (
11.25
).
∎

11.4.
The case where
\mathscr{H}Rp_{*}\mathscr{E}
is locally free

By Theorem
8.16
, we know that

(11.26)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{R}\mathscr{H}\underline{\mathscr{E}}\right)\ \mathrm{in}\ H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right).

Also
\mathscr{H}Rp_{*}\mathscr{E}
is a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded coherent sheaf on

S
. Using equation (
11.17
) in Theorem
11.10
and
(
11.26
), we get

(11.27)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}\prime\prime}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{H}Rp_{*}\mathscr{E}\right)\ \mathrm{in}\ H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right).

When
\mathscr{H}Rp_{*}\mathscr{E}
is locally free, there is a simpler proof of Theorem

11.10
not relying on Theorem

11.6
. In this case, the same arguments as in Subsection

8.5
show that the cohomology of the complex

\left(\mathcal{D},A^{\mathcal{D}\prime\prime}\right)
is a
finite-dimensional holomorphic vector bundle
H
on
S
. Using
fiberwise Hodge theory, we can identify
H
with the corresponding
fiberwise harmonic sections
\mathcal{H}
. Let
g^{H}
denote the
corresponding Hermitian metric on
H
.

Under the above assumption, a strict analogue of Theorem
8.9
can be proved.
More precisely, by proceeding as in Berline-Getzler-Vergne
[
BGV92
, Theorems 9.19 and
9.23]
,
[
B97
, Theorems 9.5 and
9.6]
, and using the obvious analogue of (
11.19
),
one can show that as
T\to+\infty
, we have the uniform
convergence of forms on
S
,

(11.28)

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X}/T,g_{T}^{D}\right)\to\mathrm{ch}\left(H,g^{H}\right).

By (
11.28
), we deduce that

(11.29)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}\prime\prime}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(H\right)\ \mathrm{in}\ H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right).

Moreover,

(11.30)

\mathscr{H}Rp_{*}\mathscr{E}=H\,\mathrm{in}\,K\left(S\right).

Using (
11.29
), (
11.30
), we reobtain Theorem

11.10
.

The above strategy was used in Bismut
[
B13
, Theorems
4.10.4 and 4.11.2]
in
the case where
D
is a holomorphic vector bundle on
M
, and

\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right)=\left(D,\nabla^{D\prime\prime}\right)
, in order to
establish Theorem
11.10
.

12.
A proof of Theorem
10.1
when

\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}\omega^{X}=0
.

We make the same assumptions as in Section
10
. We will
prove Theorem
10.1
under the assumption that

\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}\omega^{X}=0
, by extending
[
B13
, Theorem
5.1.2]
. Under this assumption, by the results of
Subsections
2.4
and
10.4
,

\underline{R}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
is a
(1,1)
form, so that

\widehat{A}\left(T_{\mathbf{R}}X,\underline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}\right)
is a
closed form in
\Omega^{(=)}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
.

Theorem 12.1
.

If
\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}\omega^{X}=0
, as
t\to 0
, we have the uniform convergence of forms on
S
,

(12.1)

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X}/t,g^{D}\right)\\
\to p_{*}\left[q^{*}\left[\widehat{A}\left(T_{\mathbf{R}}X,\underline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}\right)e^{c_{1}\left(TX,g^{TX}\right)/2}\right]\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},g^{D}\right)\right].

In particular, if
\omega^{X}
is closed, as
t\to 0
,

(12.2)

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X}/t,g^{D}\right)\to p_{*}\left[q^{*}\mathrm{Td}\left(TX,g^{TX}\right)\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},g^{D}\right)\right].

If
\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}\omega^{X}=0
, then

(12.3)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(Rp_{*}\mathscr{E}\right)=p_{*}\left[q^{*}\mathrm{Td}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(TX\right)\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{E}\right)\right]\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right).

Proof.

In the proof, we will assume that

\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}\omega^{X}=0
. Also, we fix
s\in S
.
The analysis will be done in the fiber
X\times\left\{s\right\}
.

Given
t>0,x,x^{\prime}\in X
, let
P_{t}\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)
denote the
smooth kernel for
\exp\left(-A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}_{t}\right)
along the fiber
X\times\{s\}
with respect
to the volume
dx^{\prime}/\left(2\pi\right)^{n}

30
30

30

In the
proof, the parameter
s
will be usually omitted.
. Then

(12.4)

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X}/t,g^{D}\right)=\varphi\int_{X}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[P_{t}\left(x,x\right)\right]\frac{dx}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}.

To calculate the asymptotics of (
12.4
) as
t\to 0
, we will use the local index
theoretic methods of
[
B86
,
BGS88c
,
B12
]
.

We denote by

{}^{\mathrm{c}_{t}}
the map
c
associated with
the metric
g^{TX}/t
on
TX
, that induces the metric
tg^{T^{*}X}
on

T^{*}X
. By (
4.42
), if
f\in T^{*}X
, if
f_{*}\in\overline{TX}
corresponds to
f
by the metric
g^{TX}
, then

(12.5)

\displaystyle c_{t}\left(\overline{f}\right)=\overline{f}\wedge,

\displaystyle c_{t}\left(f\right)=-i_{tf_{*}}.

Let
A_{t}^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}}
denote the superconnection

A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}}
associated with
\omega^{X}/t,g^{D}
. In the right-hand
side of the equation for
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}_{t}
in
(
10.12
), by (
10.53
), in the first row
of the right-hand side, the first term is now

(12.6)

-\frac{1}{2}t\left(\overline{\nabla}_{e_{i}}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\otimes D}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}^{TX}\left[A\left(e_{i}\right)\right]+{}^{\mathrm{c}_{t}}\left(i_{e_{i}}C\right)\right)^{2}.

The second term is also scaled by the
factor
t
.
The term containing

\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}

is absent in the right-hand side. Also the
e_{i}
are replaced
by
\sqrt{t}e_{i}
.

We will compute the limit as
t\to 0
of

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[P_{t}\left(x,x\right)\right]
. We fix
x\in X
. We take geodesic
coordinates on
X
near
x
with respect to the metric

g^{TX}
. This way, for
\epsilon>0
small enough, for any
x\in X
, we identify the open ball

B^{T_{\mathbf{R},x}X}\left(0,\epsilon\right)
in
T_{\mathbf{R},x}X
to a corresponding open
geodesic ball
B^{X}\left(x,\epsilon\right)
in
X
. Given
Y\in T_{\mathbf{R},x}X
, along the geodesic
u\in\mathbf{R}\to\exp_{x}\left(uY\right)\in X
, we
trivialize
TX,D
by parallel transport with respect to the
connections
\overline{\nabla}^{TX},\nabla^{D}
. Also we trivialize
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
via the
connection

\overline{\nabla}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}
. If

w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}
is an orthonormal basis of
T_{x}X
,
it can also be viewed as an orthonormal
trivialization of
TX
near
x
. In this trivialization, we have
the identity

(12.7)

\omega^{X}=-iw^{i}\overline{w}^{i}.

If
e_{1},\ldots,e_{2n}
is an orthonormal basis of
T_{\mathbf{R},x}X
, via
the parallel transport with respect to
\overline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
along the above geodesics, we obtain an
orthonormal basis of
T_{\mathbf{R}}X
near
x
.

Using the above trivialization, near

x
, the operator
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}_{t}

acts on

H_{x,\epsilon}=C^{\infty}\left(B^{T_{\mathbf{R},x}\left(0,\epsilon\right)},\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C},s}S\right)\widehat{\otimes}\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}_{x}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D_{s,x}\right).

A first standard step is to construct an elliptic operator acting
on

H_{x}=C^{\infty}\left(T_{\mathbf{R},x}X,\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C},s}S\right)\widehat{\otimes}\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}_{x}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D_{s,x}\right),

that coincides with
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}_{t}
on

B^{T_{\mathbf{R},x}X}\left(0,\epsilon/2\right)
, the point being that
the asymptotics of
\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[P_{t}\left(x,x\right)\right]
as

t\to 0
can be computed using this new operator. This step being
standard, we will skip it, and pretend that
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}_{t}
acts on
H_{x}
, and that
P_{t}\left(Y,Y^{\prime}\right)

is now a kernel on
T_{\mathbf{R},x}X
.

For
a>0,u\in H_{x}
, put

(12.8)

F_{a}u\left(Y\right)=u\left(aY\right).

Set

(12.9)

L_{t,x}=F_{\sqrt{t}}A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}_{t}F_{1/\sqrt{t}}.

Let
Q_{t,x}\left(Y,Y^{\prime}\right),Y,Y^{\prime}\in T_{\mathbf{R},x}X
be the smooth kernel for

\exp\left(-L_{t,x}\right)
with respect to the volume form

\frac{dY^{\prime}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}
. Clearly

(12.10)

P_{t}\left(x,x\right)=t^{-n}Q_{t,x}\left(0,0\right).

Among the monomials in the
\overline{w}^{i},i_{\overline{w}_{j}}
,
observe that up to obvious permutations, the only monomial whose
supertrace on
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}_{x}X}\right)
is nonzero
is
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}_{i}}
, and
moreover

(12.11)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}_{x}X}\right)}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}_{i}}\right]=\left(-1\right)^{n}.

If
I
is a strictly ordered multi-index with values in
1,\ldots,n
,
put

\overline{w}^{I}=\prod_{i\in I}\overline{w}^{i},i_{\overline{w}_{I}}=\prod_{i\in I}i_{\overline{w}_{i}}
. Any
T\in\mathrm{End}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}_{x}X}\right)\right)
can be written uniquely as a linear combination
of operators of the form
\overline{w}^{I}i_{\overline{w}_{J}}
. Put

(12.12)

T_{t}=e^{-i\omega^{X}_{x}/t}Te^{i\omega^{X}_{x}/t}.

Conjugation by the form
e^{-i\omega_{x}^{X}/t}
leaves unchanged the operators

\overline{w}^{i}\wedge
and replaces the operators

i_{t\overline{w}_{i}}
by
i_{t\overline{w}_{i}}-w^{i}\wedge
.
Let
T_{t}^{\max}\in\mathbf{C}
be the coefficient of

\left(-i\right)^{n}\prod_{1}^{n}\overline{w}^{i}w^{i}
in the obvious expansion of

T_{t}
. By (
12.11
), (
12.12
), we get

(12.13)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}_{x}X}\right)}\left[T\right]=\frac{t^{n}}{i^{n}}T_{t}^{\max}.

If
T
is instead an element of

\mathrm{End}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T_{x}^{*}X}\right)\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(D_{s,x}\right)
, we still define
T_{t}
as in (
12.12
).
Equation (
12.13
) is replaced by

(12.14)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}_{x}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D_{s,x}}\left[T\right]=\frac{t^{n}}{i^{n}}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{D_{s,x}}\left[T_{t}\right]^{\max}.

When viewing
w^{i},\overline{w}^{i}
as differential forms on
X
at

x
, then
\left(-i\right)^{n}\prod_{1}^{n}\overline{w}^{i}w^{i}
is
just the canonical volume
2n
-form.

In
L_{t,x}
, no matrix operator of the form
w^{i}\wedge
appears.
Put

(12.15)

M_{t,x}=e^{-i\omega_{x}^{X}/t}L_{t,x}e^{i\omega_{x}^{X}/t}.

The above conjugation will play the role of
Getzler rescaling
[
G86
]
in local index theory.

Let
R_{t,x}\left(Y,Y^{\prime}\right),Y,Y^{\prime}\in T_{\mathbf{R},x}X
be the smooth kernel
for
\exp\left(-M_{t,x}\right)
with respect to the
volume
\frac{dY^{\prime}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}
.
By (
12.10
), (
12.14
), we get

(12.16)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[P_{t}\left(x,x\right)\right]=\frac{1}{i^{n}}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{D_{s,x}}\left[R_{t,x}\left(0,0\right)\right]^{\max}.

To compute the asymptotics of (
12.16
) as
t\to 0
, we use the second
equation in (
10.12
) for
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0},2}_{t}

while implementing the above trivializations and conjugations.
We will not write the conjugations explicitly.

We view
\overline{R}^{TX}_{x}
as a section of

\Lambda^{2}\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{R},x}X\right)\otimes\mathrm{End}\left(T_{x}X\right)
. Let
\overline{\Gamma}^{TX}
be the
connection form for
\overline{\nabla}^{TX}
in the above parallel
transport trivialization. It is well-known that for
Y\in T_{\mathbf{R},x}X
,

(12.17)

\overline{\Gamma}^{TX}_{Y}=\frac{1}{2}\overline{R}_{x}^{TX}\left(Y,\cdot\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\left|Y\right|^{2}\right).

By (
12.17
), we deduce that if

\overline{\Gamma}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}
is the
corresponding connection form for

\overline{\nabla}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}
, then

(12.18)

\overline{\Gamma}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}_{Y}=-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\overline{R}_{x}^{TX}\left(Y,\cdot\right)\overline{w}_{i},w_{j}\right\rangle\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}^{j}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left|Y\right|^{2}\right),

with
\mathcal{O}\left(\left|Y\right|^{2}\right)
still
being linear combinations of the

\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}_{j}}
.

Let

\Gamma^{D}
be the connection form in the considered trivialization
of
D
. Then

(12.19)

-F_{\sqrt{t}}\frac{1}{2}t\left(\overline{\nabla}_{e_{i}}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}^{TX}\left[A\left(e_{i}\right)\right]+{}^{\mathrm{c}_{t}}i_{e_{i}}C\right)^{2}F_{1/\sqrt{t}}\\
=-\frac{1}{2}\Biggl(\nabla_{e_{i}\left(\sqrt{t}Y\right)}+\sqrt{t}\left(\overline{\Gamma}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}+\Gamma^{D}\right)_{\sqrt{t}Y}\left(e_{i}(\sqrt{t}Y)\right)\\
-\frac{\sqrt{t}}{2}\mathrm{Tr}^{TX}\left[A\left(e_{i}\right)\right]_{{}_{\sqrt{t}Y}}+\sqrt{t}{}^{c_{t}}i_{e_{i}}C_{\sqrt{t}Y}\Biggr)^{2}.

We conjugate (
12.19
) by
e^{-i\omega_{x}^{X}/t}
. Using
(
12.18
), we find that as
t\to 0
,

(12.20)

\nabla_{e_{i}\left(\sqrt{t}Y\right)}+\sqrt{t}\left(\overline{\Gamma}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)}+\Gamma^{D}\right)_{\sqrt{t}Y}\left(e_{i}(\sqrt{t}Y)\right)\\
\to\nabla_{e_{i}}+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\overline{R}^{TX}_{x}\left(Y,e_{i}\right)\overline{w}_{i},w_{j}\right\rangle\overline{w}^{i}w^{j}.

Note that
{}^{c_{t}}B=B
, and
{}^{c_{t}}B^{*}
is obtained from

B^{*}
by replacing the exterior variables
w^{i}
by

-i_{t\overline{w}_{i}}
. By the considerations that follow
(
12.11
), when conjugating
-i_{t\overline{w}_{i}}
by

e^{-i\omega^{X}_{x}/t}
, we
obtain
w^{i}-i_{t\overline{w}_{i}}
, so that as
t\to 0
, after conjugation,

(12.21)

{}^{c_{t}}i_{e_{i}}C_{\sqrt{t}Y}\to i_{e_{i}}C_{x}.

By (
12.21
),
we deduce that

(12.22)

\sqrt{t}{}^{c_{t}}i_{e_{i}}C_{\sqrt{t}Y}\to 0.

By (
12.20
), (
12.22
), we find that after
conjugation, as
t\to 0
,

(12.23)

-\frac{1}{2}t\left(\overline{\nabla}_{e_{i}}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}^{TX}\left[A\left(e_{i}\right)\right]+{}^{\mathrm{c}_{t}}i_{e_{i}}C\right)^{2}\\
\to-\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla_{e_{i}}+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\overline{R}_{x}^{TX}\left(Y,e_{i}\right)\overline{w}_{i},w_{j}\right\rangle\overline{w}^{i}w^{j}\right)^{2}.

We will use the fact that
\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}\omega^{X}=0
, and also
(
10.43
). Recall that
w^{i},\overline{w}^{j}
are considered as
standard differential forms on
X
at
x
. Then (
12.23
)
can be rewritten in the form

(12.24)

-\frac{1}{2}t\left(\overline{\nabla}_{e_{i}}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}^{TX}\left[A\left(e_{i}\right)\right]+{}^{\mathrm{c}_{t}}i_{e_{i}}C\right)^{2}\\
\to-\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla_{e_{i}}+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\underline{R}_{x}^{TX}Y,e_{i}\right\rangle\right)^{2}.

Consider now the remaining terms in the right-hand side of
(
10.12
), while still using the fact that

\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}\omega^{X}=0
. By proceeding
as before, we find that after conjugation,

(12.25)

{}^{\mathrm{c}_{t}}\left(A^{E_{0},2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}\left[R^{TX}\right]\right)_{\sqrt{t}Y}\to\left(A^{E_{0},2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}\left[R^{TX}\right]\right)_{s,x}.

To obtain the behavior as
t\to 0
of the remaining terms in the
right-hand side of the second equation in (
10.12
), we will
use the third identity with
\beta
replaced by
\beta/t
,

c
by
{}^{c_{t}}
, and
e_{i}
by
\sqrt{t}e_{i}
. After
conjugation, for
k\geq 3
, the limit of the corresponding terms
vanishes. Since
\beta
is a
3
-form,

i_{e_{i_{1}}}i_{e_{i_{2}}}\beta
is a
1
-form, whose square
vanishes. Ultimately, after conjugation, the limit of all the terms
vanishes.

Put

(12.26)

M_{0,s,x}=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla_{e_{i}}+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\underline{R}_{x}^{TX}Y,e_{i}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+A^{E_{0},2}_{s,x}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}\left[R^{TX}_{x}\right].

By (
10.12
), (
12.24
), (
12.25
), and by the
considerations that follow, we see that as
t\to 0
,

(12.27)

M_{t,x}\to M_{0,s,x},

in the sense that we have a uniform convergence of the coefficients
of the considered operators together with all their derivatives over compact
subsets of
T_{\mathbf{R},x}X
.

Let
R_{0,s,x}\left(Y,Y^{\prime}\right),Y,Y^{\prime}\in T_{\mathbf{R},x}X
denote the smooth
kernel associated with the operator
\exp\left(-M_{0,s,x}\right)
with
respect to
\frac{dY^{\prime}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}
. By proceeding as in

[
B86
, Theorem 4.12]
,
[
BGV92
, Theorem 10.23]
using (
12.27
), as
t\to 0
, we have the
uniform convergence,

(12.28)

R_{t,x}\left(0,0\right)\to R_{0,s,x}\left(0,0\right).

Note that

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{D_{s,x}}\left[R_{0,x}\left(0,0\right)\right]
takes its values in

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}S\right)\widehat{\otimes}\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
,
to which the full operator
\varphi
now applies.
Let
\left\{\varphi\mathrm{Tr}^{D_{s,x}}\left[Q_{0,s,x}\left(0,0\right)\right]\right\}^{\max}\in\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}S\right)
be the coefficient of the volume
form
dx
viewed as a
2n
form on
T_{\mathbf{R},x}X
.
Using (
12.16
), (
12.28
), as
t\to 0
, we have the uniform convergence over
X
,

(12.29)

\varphi\left(2\pi\right)^{-n}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[P_{t}\left(x,x\right)\right]\to\left\{\varphi\mathrm{Tr}^{D_{s,x}}\left[R_{0,s,x}\left(0,0\right)\right]\right\}^{\max}.

In the right-hand side of (
12.27
) one recognizes the
classical harmonic oscillator in index theory
[
G86
]
,

[
BGV92
, Proposition 4.19]
. Using Mehler’s formula

[
GlJ87
, Theorem 1.5.10]
and
(
8.1
), we get

(12.30)

\varphi\mathrm{Tr}^{D_{s,x}}\left[R_{0,s,x}\left(0,0\right)\right]=q^{*}\left[\widehat{A}\left(T_{\mathbf{R}}X,\underline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}\right)e^{c_{1}\left(TX,g^{TX}\right)/2}\right]\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},g^{D}\right).

Using (
12.4
), (
12.29
), (
12.30
), and
dominated convergence, we get (
12.1
). If
\omega^{X}
is
closed,
\overline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}=\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
, so that
(
12.1
) gives (
12.2
).

If
\omega^{X}
is closed, (
12.3
) follows from
(
12.2
). We will now prove (
12.3
) when we only
assume that

\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}\omega^{X}=0
.
Recall that if
\alpha\in\Omega^{(=)}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
is closed,

\left\{\alpha\right\}
denotes its Bott-Chern cohomology class in

H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(X,\mathbf{R}\right)
. By Theorem
11.10
and by (
12.1
), we get

(12.31)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(Rp_{*}\mathscr{E}\right)=p_{*}\left[q^{*}\left\{\widehat{A}\left(T_{\mathbf{R}}X,\underline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}\right)\right\}q^{*}e^{c_{1,\mathrm{BC}}\left(TX\right)}\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathscr{E}\right)\right].

Now we
proceed as in
[
B13
, Theorem 5.2.1]
. We have the
equivalent exact sequence of holomorphic vector bundles on
X
in
(
10.41
), (
10.42
). Using the results of
Bismut-Gillet-Soulé

[
BGS88a
, Theorem 1.29]
on Bott-Chern classes, from
the exact sequence in (
10.41
), using the notation in
(
2.27
), we get

(12.32)

\left\{\widehat{A}\left(T_{\mathbf{R}}X,\underline{\nabla}^{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}\right)\right\}=\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(TX\right).

By (
2.28
), we obtain

(12.33)

\mathrm{Td}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(TX\right)=\widehat{A}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(TX\right)e^{c_{1,\mathrm{BC}}\left(TX\right)/2}.

By (
12.31
)–(
12.33
), we get (
12.3
).
The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 12.2
.

It is not possible to deform geometrically the exact
sequence (
10.42
) by scaling
\omega^{X}
. However, on the exact sequence (
10.41
),
the deformation arguments of
[
BGS88a
]
can
still be used. More precisely, that if
d\in\mathbf{C}^{*}
, replacing

\omega^{X}
by
d\omega^{X}
in the construction of

\underline{\nabla}^{F\prime\prime}
in (
10.40
)
does not change the Bott-Chern class, which ultimately allows us
to make
d=0
, which is equivalent to what we did in the proof of
Theorem
12.1
. Similar ideas will be used in a more sophisticate
context in the rest of the paper.

13.
The hypoelliptic superconnections

The purpose of this Section is to construct an antiholomorphic superconnection over
S

with fiberwise hypoelliptic curvature. This superconnection is a
deformation of the elliptic superconnections of Section

10
. Our constructions extend what was done in

[
B13
, Chapter 6]
for holomorphic vector bundles on
M
to the case of antiholomorphic
superconnections
A^{E\prime\prime}
.

This Section is organized as follows. In Subsection
13.1
,
we introduce the total space
\mathcal{X}
of an extra copy

\widehat{TX}
of
TX
, and also the antiholomorphic superconnection

\mathscr{N}_{X}=\pi_{*}\mathscr{C}^{\mathcal{X}}
on
X
.

In Subsection
13.2
, given a Hermitian metric

g^{\widehat{TX}}
on
\widehat{TX}
, we obtain a corresponding
splitting of
\mathscr{N}_{X}
.

In Subsection
13.3
, we introduce the total space

\underline{\pi}:\mathcal{M}\to M
of

q^{*}\widehat{TX}
, and we construct a natural
infinite-dimensional antiholomorphic superconnection
\mathscr{E}_{M}=\mathscr{E}\widehat{\otimes}_{b}\underline{\pi}_{*}\mathscr{C}^{M}

over
M
.

In Subsection
13.4
, we introduce the superconnection

\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}
that corresponds to
A^{E_{M}\prime\prime}
via a natural
splitting of
E_{M}
.

In Subsection
13.5
, given metrics

g^{\widehat{TX}},g^{D}
on
\widehat{TX},D
, we construct a
nonpositive Hermitian form on the diagonal bundle associated with

E_{M}
, and the corresponding adjoint superconnection
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
of

\mathcal{A^{\prime\prime}}
.

In Subsection
13.6
, if
\underline{q}:\mathcal{M}\to X

is the obvious projection, given a holomorphic section
z
of

\underline{q}^{*}TX
on
\mathcal{M}
, we construct an
antiholomorphic superconnection
A^{E_{M}\prime\prime}_{z}
on

\Lambda\left(T^{*}M\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathscr{E}_{M}
.

In Subsection
13.7
, still using the above splittings, we
obtain a superconnection
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Z}
and its
companion
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{Z}
, which also depends on the choice of a
Kähler form
\omega^{X}
on
X
.

In Subsection
13.8
, we construct a Hermitian form

\epsilon_{X}
along the fibers
\mathcal{X}
.

In Subsection
13.9
, we compute the proper adjoint of

\mathcal{A}_{Z}
.

In Subsection
13.10
, we give a Lichnerowicz formula for
the curvature

\mathcal{A}_{Z}^{2}
.

In Subsection
13.11
, we specialize our constructions
with
z
is the canonical section
y
, in which case

\mathcal{A}_{Z}
is self-adjoint, and its curvature is fiberwise
hypoelliptic.

In Subsection
13.12
, we study the behavior of

\mathcal{A}_{Y}
under the scaling of
g^{\widehat{TX}}
.

Finally, in Subsection
13.13
, we give the arguments
which show that when replacing

g^{\widehat{TX}}
by
b^{4}g^{\widehat{TX}}
, if

g^{TX}=g^{\widehat{TX}}
, as
b\to 0
, the hypoelliptic superconnection

\mathcal{A}_{Y,b}
is a deformation of
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}}
.

In this Section, we make the same assumptions as in Section

10
, and we use the corresponding notation.

13.1.
The total space of
\widehat{TX}

Let
X
be a compact complex manifold. Let

\pi:\mathcal{X}\to X
be the total space of
TX
, the fiber being denoted

\widehat{TX}

to distinguish it from the
usual tangent bundle
TX
, so that
TX
and
\widehat{TX}
are
canonically isomorphic. The conjugate bundle to

\widehat{TX}
will be denoted

\overline{\widehat{TX}}
and its dual
\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}
. The real bundle associated with
\widehat{TX}
is
denoted by

\widehat{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
.

We have the exact sequences of holomorphic vector bundles on
\mathcal{X}
,

(13.1)

\displaystyle 0\to\pi^{*}\widehat{TX}\to T\mathcal{X}\to\pi^{*}TX\to 0,

\displaystyle 0\to\pi^{*}T^{*}X\to T^{*}\mathcal{X}\to\pi^{*}\widehat{T^{*}X}\to 0.

Recall that the trivial antiholomorphic superconnection

\mathscr{C}^{X}
was defined
in (
5.12
). We define
\mathscr{C}^{\mathcal{X}}
on

\mathcal{X}
in the same way. Then

(13.2)

\mathscr{C}^{\mathcal{X}}=\pi^{*}_{b}\mathscr{C}^{X}.

Put

(13.3)

\mathscr{N}_{X}=\pi_{*}\mathscr{C}^{\mathcal{X}}.

Then
\mathscr{N}_{X}
is a

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)
-module. Let

N_{X}

be the infinite-dimensional vector bundle associated with
\mathscr{N}_{X}
. The operator

\overline{\partial}^{\mathcal{X}}
can be viewed as an antiholomorphic
superconnection
A^{N_{X}\prime\prime}
on

N_{X}
, so that
\mathscr{N}_{X}=\left(N_{X},A^{N_{X}\prime\prime}\right)
.

Put

(13.4)

\mathbf{I}=C^{\infty}\left(\widehat{TX},\pi^{*}\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)\right).

Then
\mathbf{I}
is the diagonal vector bundle
associated with
N_{X}
. Also

\mathbf{I}^{0}
is the
vector bundle on
X
of smooth complex functions along the fibre

\widehat{TX}
, and

(13.5)

\mathbf{I}=\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)\otimes\mathbf{I}^{0}.

13.2.
A Hermitian metric on
\widehat{TX}

Here, we follow
[
B08
, Section 3.12]
and
[
B13
, Section
6.1]
. The constructions given in these references will
permit us to construct a splittings of
\mathscr{N}_{X}
.

Let

g^{\widehat{TX}}
be a Hermitian metric on
\widehat{TX}
, let

\nabla^{\widehat{TX}}
be the holomorphic Hermitian connection on

\left(\widehat{TX},g^{\widehat{TX}}\right)
, and let

R^{\widehat{TX}}

be its curvature. Let
\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)}

be the induced connection on

\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)
.

Let

T^{H}\mathcal{X}\subset T\mathcal{X}

be the horizontal subbundle associated with the
connection
\nabla^{\widehat{TX}}
. We have the identification of smooth
vector bundles on
\mathcal{X}
,

(13.6)

T^{H}\mathcal{X}=\pi^{*}TX,

and also
identification of smooth vector bundles on

\mathcal{X}
,

(13.7)

T\mathcal{X}=T^{H}\mathcal{X}\oplus\pi^{*}\widehat{TX}.

By (
13.6
), (
13.7
), we get the smooth
identification,

(13.8)

T\mathcal{X}=\pi^{*}\left(TX\oplus\widehat{TX}\right).

By (
13.8
), we get the
identification of smooth vector bundles,

(13.9)

\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}\mathcal{X}}\right)=\pi^{*}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)\right).

By (
13.9
), we get the identification of smooth vector
bundles on
X
,

(13.10)

N_{X}=\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathbf{I}.

By (
13.9
), we have the identity

(13.11)

\Omega^{0,\scriptsize\bullet}\left(\mathcal{X},\mathbf{C}\right)=\Omega^{0,\scriptsize\bullet}\left(X,\mathbf{I}^{\scriptsize\bullet}\right).

Let

\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}
denote the vector bundle of elements of

\mathbf{I}
which have compact support. Then

\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}
inherits a
L_{2}
Hermitian metric

g^{\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}}
from the metric
g^{\widehat{TX}}
,
such that if
r,t\in\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}
, then

(13.12)

\left\langle r,t\right\rangle_{g^{\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}}}=\int_{\widehat{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}}\left\langle r,t\right\rangle_{g^{\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)}}\frac{dY}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}.

If
U\in T_{\mathbf{R}}X
, let

U^{H}\in T_{\mathbf{R}}^{H}\mathcal{X}
denote
the horizontal lift of
U
.

If
U\in T_{\mathbf{R}}X
, if
s
is a
smooth section of
\mathbf{I}
on
X
, set

(13.13)

\nabla^{\mathbf{I}}_{U}s=\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)}_{U^{H}}s.

Then
\nabla^{\mathbf{I}}
induces a unitary connection
\nabla^{\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}}
on

\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}
that preserves its
\mathbf{Z}
-grading. Let

\nabla^{\mathbf{I}^{0}}
be the restriction of
\nabla^{\mathbf{I}}
to

\mathbf{I}^{0}
. By
(
13.5
), the connection
\nabla^{\mathbf{I}}
is just the
connection on
\mathbf{I}
that is induced by

\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)},\nabla^{\mathbf{I}^{0}}
.

Let

\overline{\partial}^{V}
denote the
\overline{\partial}
-operator acting
along the fibers
\widehat{TX}
of
\pi
. Using
(
13.11
), we
see that
\nabla^{\mathbf{I}\prime\prime}
and
\overline{\partial}^{V}

act on
\Omega^{0,\scriptsize\bullet}\left(\mathcal{X},\mathbf{C}\right)
.

Let

\widehat{y}
be the tautological section of

\pi^{*}\widehat{TX}
on
\mathcal{X}
, let
\overline{\widehat{y}}

be
the conjugate section of
\pi^{*}\overline{\widehat{TX}}
, and let

\widehat{Y}=\widehat{y}+\overline{\widehat{y}}
be the tautological
section of
\pi^{*}\widehat{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
, so that

\left|\widehat{Y}\right|^{2}_{g^{\widehat{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}}}=2\left|\widehat{y}\right|^{2}_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}
.

Let
w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}
be a basis of
TX
, let
w^{1},\ldots,w^{n}

be
the corresponding dual basis of
T^{*}X
. We denote with a hat the
corresponding objects associated with
\widehat{TX},\widehat{T^{*}X}
.
We have results established in
[
BGS88b
, Theorem
2.8]
,
[
B13
, Propositions 6.1.2, 6.1.4, and 6.1.6]
.

Proposition 13.1
.

The following identities hold:

(13.14)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mathbf{I}\prime\prime,2}=0,\ \nabla^{\mathbf{I}\prime,2}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mathbf{I},2}=-\nabla^{V}_{R^{\widehat{TX}}\widehat{Y}}-\left\langle R^{\widehat{TX}}\overline{\widehat{w}}_{i},\overline{\widehat{w}}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{\widehat{w}}^{i}i_{\overline{\widehat{w}}_{j}}.

Also

(13.15)

\displaystyle\left[\nabla^{\mathbf{I}},\overline{\partial}^{V}\right]=0,

\displaystyle\left[\nabla^{\mathbf{I}},\overline{\partial}^{V*}\right]=0.

Finally, we have the identity of operators acting on

\Omega^{0,\scriptsize\bullet}\left(\mathcal{X},\mathbf{C}\right)
,

(13.16)

\overline{\partial}^{\mathcal{X}}=\nabla^{\mathbf{I}\prime\prime}+\overline{\partial}^{V}.

Remark 13.2
.

The decomposition (
13.16
) can be viewed as a special
case of equation (
5.6
) when
\mathscr{E}
is replaced
by
\mathscr{N}_{X}
. In (
13.16
),
\overline{\partial}^{\mathcal{X}}
does not
depend on
g^{\widehat{TX}}
, but the splitting in the right-hand
depends on
g^{\widehat{TX}}
.

By the above, it follows that
\left(\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}},\nabla^{\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}\prime\prime}\right)
is a holomorphic Hermitian vector bundle
31
31

31

Here, ‘holomorphic vector bundle’ is taken in the naive
sense.
. The holomorphic structure depends explicitly on the metric

g^{\widehat{TX}}
.

13.3.
The antiholomorphic superconnection
\mathscr{E}_{M}

We make the same assumptions as in Subsections
10.1
,

10.2
, and
13.1
. Put

(13.17)

\mathcal{M}=S\times\mathcal{X}.

Let
\underline{\pi}
be the projection
\mathcal{M}\to M
, let

\underline{p},\underline{q}
be the obvious maps
\mathcal{M}\to S,\mathcal{M}\to X
.

As in (
13.2
), we have

(13.18)

\mathscr{C}^{\mathcal{M}}=\underline{\pi}^{*}_{b}\mathscr{C}^{M}.

Put

(13.19)

\mathscr{N}_{M}=\underline{\pi}_{*}\mathscr{C}^{\mathcal{M}}.

Then

(13.20)

\mathscr{N}_{M}=\underline{q}^{*}_{b}\mathscr{N}_{X}.

Let
N_{M}
be the infinite-dimensional vector bundle on
M
that is associated with
\mathscr{N}_{M}
.

The associated diagonal vector bundle is just
q^{*}\mathbf{I}
.

Let
\mathscr{E}=\left(E,A^{E\prime\prime}\right)
be an
antiholomorphic superconnection on
M
. Put

(13.21)

\mathscr{E}^{\mathcal{M}}=\underline{\pi}^{*}_{b}\mathscr{E}.

Then
\mathscr{E}^{\mathcal{M}}=\left(E^{\mathcal{M}},A^{E^{\mathcal{M}}\prime\prime}\right)
is an antiholomorphic
superconnection on
\mathcal{M}
, and the associated diagonal bundle
is just
\underline{\pi}^{*}D
. Also
\mathscr{E}^{\mathcal{M}}
is
a
\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}\mathcal{M}}\right)
-module.

Put

(13.22)

\mathscr{E}_{M}=\underline{\pi}_{*}\mathscr{E}^{\mathcal{M}}.

Recall that the tensor product

\widehat{\otimes}_{b}
was defined in
(
5.77
). Then

(13.23)

\mathscr{E}_{M}=\mathscr{E}\widehat{\otimes}_{b}\mathscr{N}_{M}.

Observe that

\mathscr{E}_{M}=\left(E_{M},A^{E_{M}\prime\prime}\right)
is
an
antiholomorphic superconnection on
M
, with an infinite-dimensional

E_{M}
, and the associated diagonal bundle is
D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}
.

13.4.
A metric description of
A^{E_{M}\prime\prime}

We fix a splitting of
E
as in (
5.2
), (
5.3
), so
that
E\simeq E_{0}
.

We proceed as in Subsections

13.2
and
13.3
. We fix a metric
g^{\widehat{TX}}
on the vector bundle

\widehat{TX}
over
X
. This induces a
corresponding splitting of
\mathscr{N}_{X}
