, and also of

\mathscr{N}_{M}
.

The corresponding vector bundles
E_{0},N_{M,0}
are given by

(13.24)

\displaystyle E_{0}=\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}M}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D,

\displaystyle N_{M,0}=\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}M}\right)\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}.

The above two splittings induce a corresponding splitting of

\mathscr{E}_{M}
, so that

(13.25)

E_{M,0}=\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}M}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}.

Equation (
13.25
) can also be written in the form

(13.26)

E_{M,0}=E_{0}\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}.

Definition 13.3
.

Let

\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}
be the antiholomorphic
superconnection on

E_{M,0}
that corresponds to
A^{E_{M}\prime\prime}
.

We write
A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}
as in (
5.9
). In
particular,
D
is equipped with an antiholomorphic connection
\nabla^{D\prime\prime}
.

As we saw in Subsection
13.2
,
\mathbf{I}
is equipped
with a holomorphic structure
\nabla^{\mathbf{I}\prime\prime}
.

Definition 13.4
.

Let

\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}\prime\prime}
be the antiholomorphic
connection on
D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}
associated with
\nabla^{D\prime\prime},\nabla^{\mathbf{I}\prime\prime}
.

Proposition 13.5
.

The following identity holds:

(13.27)

\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}=\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}\prime\prime}+B+\overline{\partial}^{V}.

Proof.

Our proposition follows from equations (
5.9
),
(
13.16
).
∎

13.5.
The adjoint of
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}

Let
g^{D}
be a Hermitian metric on
D
. Recall that

g^{\widehat{TX}}
induces a Hermitian metric on

\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}
. Therefore
D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}
is equipped with a Hermitian metric
g^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}}
.

We are now in a situation formally similar to what was done in a
finite-dimensional context in Section
7
, and in an
infinite-dimensional context in
[
BGS88b
]
, in

[
B13
, Chapter 4]
, and in Section
10
, with

M,S
replaced by
\mathcal{M},M
, and
p
replaced by
\underline{\pi}
. We
briefly explain the construction of the adjoint of
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}
.

Let
s,s^{\prime}\in\Omega\left(M,D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)
. We can write
s,s^{\prime}

in the form

(13.28)

\displaystyle s=\sum\alpha_{i}r_{i},

\displaystyle s^{\prime}=\sum\beta_{j}t_{j},

with
\alpha_{i},\beta_{j}\in\Omega\left(M,\mathbf{C}\right)
and

r_{i},t_{j}\in C^{\infty}\left(M,D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)
. As in (
7.10
),
(
7.12
), put

(13.29)

\theta_{g^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}}}\left(s,s^{\prime}\right)=\frac{i^{m}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{m}}\sum\int_{M}\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}\wedge\overline{\beta}_{j}\left\langle r_{i},t_{j}\right\rangle_{g^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}}}.

Definition 13.6
.

Let

\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
be the formal adjoint of
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}
with
respect to
\theta_{g^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}}}
.

Then
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
is exactly the adjoint of the antiholomorphic superconnection

\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}
in the sense of Definition

7.4
with respect to the pure metric

g^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}}
.

Recall that the holomorphic connection
\nabla^{D\prime}
on
D
was
defined in (
7.15
). Since
g^{D}
is a pure metric in

\mathscr{M}^{D}
,
\nabla^{D\prime}
is just a standard holomorphic
connection.

Definition 13.7
.

Let

\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}\prime}
be the holomorphic
connection on
D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}
induced by
\nabla^{D\prime},\nabla^{\mathbf{I}\prime}
.

We use the notation in (
7.17
). Let
\overline{\partial}^{V*}

denote the formal adjoint of
\overline{\partial}^{V}
with respect to the
metric
g^{\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}}
.

Proposition 13.8
.

The following identity holds:

(13.30)

\mathcal{A}^{\prime}=\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}\prime}+B^{*}+\overline{\partial}^{V*}.

Proof.

This is a consequence of Proposition
13.5
.
∎

Then
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
act as differential operators on

\Omega\left(M,D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}\right)
, and

(13.31)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime 2}=0,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\prime 2}=0.

13.6.
The antiholomorphic superconnection
A^{E_{M}\prime\prime}_{z}

Recall that
A^{E^{\mathcal{M}}\prime\prime},A^{E_{M}\prime\prime}
are the same operator, that acts on
C^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M},E^{\mathcal{M}}\right)=C^{\infty}\left(M,E_{M}\right)
. We extend these operators to operators acting on

C^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M},\underline{\pi}^{*}\Lambda\left(T^{*}M\right)\widehat{\otimes}E^{\mathcal{M}}\right)=C^{\infty}\left(M,\Lambda\left(T^{*}M\right)\widehat{\otimes}E_{M}\right)
, that still
verify Leibniz rule with respect to multiplication by smooth forms
in
\underline{\pi}^{*}\Lambda\left(T^{*}M\right),\Lambda\left(T^{*}M\right)
.

Let
z
be a holomorphic section of
\underline{q}^{*}TX
on

\mathcal{M}
.
Since
q^{*}TX\subset TM
, the contraction operator
i_{z}
acts on the above
vector spaces.

Definition 13.9
.

Put

(13.32)

\displaystyle A^{E^{\mathcal{M}}\prime\prime}_{z}=A^{E^{\mathcal{M}}\prime\prime}+i_{z},

\displaystyle A^{E_{M}\prime\prime}_{z}=A^{E_{M}\prime\prime}+i_{z}.

Both operators have total degree
+1
. Since
z
is holomorphic, we get

(13.33)

\displaystyle A^{E^{\mathcal{M}}\prime\prime,2}_{z}=0,

\displaystyle A^{E_{M}\prime\prime,2}_{z}=0.

Also
A^{E^{\mathcal{M}}\prime\prime}_{z}
is an antiholomorphic
superconnection on
\underline{\pi}^{*}\Lambda\left(T^{*}M\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathscr{E}^{\mathcal{M}}
, and
A^{E_{M}\prime\prime}_{z}
is an
antiholomorphic superconnection on
\Lambda\left(T^{*}M\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathscr{E}_{M}
. These two operators are in fact the same operator.
In the sequel, we will deal mostly with the second one.

13.7.
The superconnection
\mathcal{A}_{Z}

We now make the same assumptions as in Subsections
13.4

and
13.6
.

As in Subsection
10.3
, let

g^{TX}
be a Hermitian metric
on
TX
with Kähler form

\omega^{X}
. The exponential

e^{-i\omega^{X}}
is taken in the exterior algebra

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
.

Let
z
denote a holomorphic section of
\underline{q}^{*}TX
on

\mathcal{M}
. Put

(13.34)

Z=z+\overline{z}.

Then
Z
is a smooth section of
\underline{q}^{*}T_{\mathbf{R}}X
.
Let
\overline{z}_{*}\in\underline{q}^{*}T^{*}X
correspond to

\overline{z}\in\underline{q}^{*}\overline{TX}
by the metric

g^{TX}
. By (
10.7
),
\overline{z}_{*}
can also be viewed
as a section of
T^{*}M
.

Note that
TX\subset TM
, and so the contraction operators

i_{z},i_{\overline{z}}
act as operators of degree

1,-1
on

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}M\right)
, and so they act on

\Omega\left(M,D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}\right)
.

Now we follow
[
B13
, Section 6.3]
.

Definition 13.10
.

Put

(13.35)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Z}=\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}+i_{z},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{Z}=e^{q^{*}i\omega^{X}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}+i_{\overline{z}}\right)e^{-q^{*}i\omega^{X}},\quad\mathcal{A}_{Z}=\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Z}+\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{Z}.

Since
z
is holomorphic,

(13.36)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{Z}^{\prime\prime 2}=0,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\prime 2}_{Z}=0.

By (
13.35
), we get

(13.37)

\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{Z}=\mathcal{A}^{\prime}-q^{*}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}+i_{\overline{z}}+\overline{z}_{*}\wedge.

Also

(13.38)

\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Z}=\left[\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Z},\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{Z}\right].

Let
\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}}
be the connection on
D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}
which
is induced by
\nabla^{D},\nabla^{\mathbf{I}}
. Recall that

C
was defined
in (
7.21
). By (
13.27
), (
13.30
), (
13.35
), and
(
13.37
), we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Z}=\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}\prime\prime}+B+\overline{\partial}^{V}+i_{z},

(13.39)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{Z}=\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}\prime}+B^{*}-q^{*}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}+\overline{\partial}^{V*}+i_{\overline{z}}+\overline{z}_{*}\wedge,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{Z}=\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}}+C-q^{*}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}+\overline{\partial}^{V}+i_{z}+\overline{\partial}^{V*}+i_{\overline{z}}+\overline{z}_{*}\wedge.

We denote by
L
the operator of multiplication by
\omega^{X}
and
by
\Lambda
its adjoint with respect to
g^{TX}
. Let

w_{1}\ldots,w_{n}
be an orthonormal basis of
\left(TX,g^{TX}\right)
. Then

(13.40)

\displaystyle L=-iw^{i}\wedge\overline{w}^{i},

\displaystyle\Lambda=ii_{\overline{w}_{i}}i_{w_{i}}.

Conjugation by
\exp\left(i\Lambda\right)

changes
w^{i},\overline{w}^{i}
into
w^{i}-i_{\overline{w}_{i}},\overline{w}^{i}+i_{w_{i}}
.

Put

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{Z}=\exp\left(i\Lambda\right)\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Z}\exp\left(-i\Lambda\right),

(13.41)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{Z}=\exp\left(i\Lambda\right)\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{Z}\exp\left(-i\Lambda\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{Z}=\exp\left(i\Lambda\right)\mathcal{A}_{Z}\exp\left(-i\Lambda\right).

Then

(13.42)

\mathcal{B}_{Z}=\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{Z}+\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{Z}.

Set

(13.43)

\mathcal{E}=\exp\left(i\Lambda\right)\left(\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}}+C-q^{*}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)\exp\left(-i\Lambda\right).

By (
13.7
), and by the considerations that follow
(
13.40
), we get

(13.44)

\mathcal{B}_{Z}=\mathcal{E}+\overline{\partial}^{V}+i_{z}+\overline{\partial}^{V*}+\overline{z}_{*}.

We still count the degree in
\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}M\right)
as the
difference of the antiholomorphic and of the holomorphic degree. Then
A^{\prime\prime}_{Z},B^{\prime\prime}_{Z}
are
operators of total degree
1
, and
A^{\prime}_{Z},B^{\prime}_{Z}
are operators of
total degree
-1
.

Observe that the above operators can be
considered as standard differential operators on
\mathcal{M}
.

13.8.
Two Hermitian forms

Here we follow
[
B13
, Sections 6.5 and 6.6]
. We will
slightly modify the construction of the Hermitian form

\theta_{g^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}}}
.

Set

(13.45)

\mathbb{F}=q^{*}\left(\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)\right)\widehat{\otimes}D.

Then
\mathbb{F}
is a vector bundle on
M
. Recall that
\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
is
\mathbf{Z}
-graded by the
difference of the antiholomorphic and the holomorphic degrees. Also

\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)
and
D
are

\mathbf{Z}
-graded. Then
\mathbb{F}
inherits a corresponding
\mathbf{Z}
-grading.
Similarly
\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}S\right)
inherits a similar
grading.

As in
[
B13
, Section 6.5]
, we will use the
identification

(13.46)

\Lambda\left(\widehat{T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X}\right)=\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right).

By (
13.45
), (
13.46
), we get

(13.47)

\mathbb{F}=q^{*}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{T^{*}X}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\Lambda\left(\widehat{T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X}\right)\right)\widehat{\otimes}D.

Let

\sigma
be the involution of
\mathcal{X}
given by

\left(x,\widehat{y}\right)\to\left(x,-\widehat{y}\right)
. Then
\sigma
induces an involution of

\mathcal{M}
.

Let

\sigma^{*}
denote the obvious action of
\sigma
on

\Lambda\left(\widehat{T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X}\right)
. Namely, if
N^{\Lambda\left(\widehat{T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X}\right)}

is the number operator of

\Lambda\left(\widehat{T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X}\right)
, then
\sigma^{*}
acts like

\left(-1\right)^{N^{\Lambda\left(\widehat{T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X}\right)}}
.
Also, we make
\sigma^{*}
act trivially on
D
. Then
\sigma^{*}

acts on
\mathbb{F}
in (
13.47
).

We fix one fiber
X
of the projection
p
, and the
corresponding fiber
\mathcal{X}
of

\underline{p}
. Note that

(13.48)

\Omega\left(X,D|_{X}\widehat{\otimes}\mathbf{I}\right)=C^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{X},\underline{\pi}^{*}\mathbb{F}|_{\mathcal{X}}\right).

Let

dv_{\mathcal{X}}
denote the volume form on
\mathcal{X}
which
is associated with the metrics
g^{TX},g^{\widehat{TX}}
. If

s,s^{\prime}\in C^{\infty,\mathrm{c}}\left(\mathcal{X},\underline{\pi}^{*}\mathbb{F}|_{\mathcal{X}}\right)
, set

(13.49)

\left\langle s,s^{\prime}\right\rangle_{L_{2}}=\left(2\pi\right)^{-2n}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\left\langle s,s^{\prime}\right\rangle_{g^{\mathbb{F}}}dv_{\mathcal{X}}.

Definition 13.11
.

If
s,s^{\prime}\in C^{\infty,\mathrm{c}}\left(\mathcal{X},\underline{\pi}^{*}\mathbb{F}|_{\mathcal{X}}\right)
, put

(13.50)

\eta_{X}\left(s,s^{\prime}\right)=\left\langle\sigma^{*}s,s^{\prime}\right\rangle_{L_{2}}.

Then
\eta_{X}
is a Hermitian form on
C^{\infty,\mathrm{c}}\left(\mathcal{X},\underline{\pi}^{*}\mathbb{F}|_{\mathcal{X}}\right)
. Note that
\eta_{X}
is non-degenerate,
it is positive on

\sigma
-invariant forms, and negative on
\sigma
-anti-invariant
forms. Also the
C^{\infty,\mathrm{c}}\left(\mathcal{X},\underline{\pi}^{*}\mathbb{F}^{i}|_{\mathcal{X}}\right)
are mutually orthogonal with respect to

\left\langle\,\right\rangle_{L_{2}}
and
\eta_{X}
.

Let

\underline{\sigma}^{*}
be the restriction of

\sigma^{*}
to
\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)
.
We make
\underline{\sigma}^{*}
act trivially on

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
and on
D
. By (
13.45
),
\underline{\sigma}^{*}
also
acts on
\mathbb{F}
. The difference with
\sigma^{*}
is that

\underline{\sigma}^{*}
acts trivially on
\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)
.
Then
\underline{\sigma}^{*}
acts on
D|_{X}\widehat{\otimes}\mathbf{I}
. Let
s,s^{\prime}\in\Omega\left(X,D|_{X}\widehat{\otimes}\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)
. Then

s,s^{\prime}
can be written in the form

(13.51)

\displaystyle s=\sum\alpha_{i}r_{i},

\displaystyle s^{\prime}=\sum\beta_{j}t_{j},

with
\alpha_{i},\beta_{j}\in\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{C}\right)
, and

r_{i},t_{j}\in C^{\infty}\left(X,D|_{X}\widehat{\otimes}\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)
.

If
\alpha\in\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
, we define

\widetilde{\alpha}
as in (
4.33
). Also we use the same
notation as in Subsection
8.2
. As we observed in that
Subsection, multiplication by
i\omega^{X}
is a self-adjoint
operator with respect to the form
\theta
in (
7.1
). Also the
exponential
e^{-i\omega^{X}}
is taken in

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
.

Definition 13.12
.

Put

(13.52)

\epsilon_{X}\left(s,s^{\prime}\right)=\frac{i^{n}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}\int_{X}\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}\wedge\overline{e^{-i\omega^{X}}\beta}_{j}\left\langle\underline{\sigma}^{*}r,t\right\rangle_{g^{D|_{X}\widehat{\otimes}\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}}}.

Equation (
13.52
) can be rewritten in the form,

(13.53)

\epsilon_{X}\left(s,s^{\prime}\right)=\frac{i^{n}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}\wedge\overline{e^{-i\omega^{X}}\beta}_{j}\left\langle\underline{\sigma}^{*}r,t\right\rangle_{g^{D|_{X}\widehat{\otimes}\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)}}\frac{dY}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}.

By
[
B13
, Section 6.5 and Theorem 6.6.1]
,

\epsilon_{X}

is a Hermitian form on
C^{\infty,\mathrm{c}}\left(\mathcal{X},\underline{\pi}^{*}\mathbb{F}|_{\mathcal{X}}\right)=\Omega\left(X,\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}\widehat{\otimes}D|_{X}\right)
. As explained in
[
B13
, Section 6.6]
,
if instead, we replace integration over
X
by integration over
M
,
while replacing
\frac{i^{n}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}
by

\frac{i^{m}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{m}}
, we obtain a
Hermitian form

\epsilon_{M}
on
\Omega\left(M,D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)
.

Remark 13.13
.

As explained in Subsection
8.2
, the term

e^{-i\omega^{X}}
in (
13.52
) can be interpreted as a
generalized metric on the trivial line
\mathbf{C}
over
M
. Equivalently,

e^{-i\omega^{X}}g^{D}|_{X}
can be viewed as a generalized
metric on
D|_{X}
.

By
[
B13
, eq. (6.5.10) and Theorem 6.6.1]
, if

s,s^{\prime}\in\Omega\left(X,D|_{X}\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)=C^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{X},\underline{\pi}^{*}\mathbb{F}|_{\mathcal{X}}\right),

we get

(13.54)

\epsilon_{X}\left(s,s^{\prime}\right)=\eta_{X}\left(e^{i\Lambda}s,e^{i\Lambda}s^{\prime}\right).

Then
\epsilon_{X}
is also a non-degenerate Hermitian form,
which can also be proved directly.
By (
13.54
), or by direct inspection, the

C^{\infty,\mathrm{c}}\left(\mathcal{X},\underline{\pi}^{*}\mathbb{F}^{i}|_{\mathcal{X}}\right)
are mutually orthogonal with respect to

\epsilon_{X}
.

We will denote with an upper
{\dagger}
the adjoint of an operator with
respect to
\epsilon_{X}
or
\eta_{X}
, the corresponding Hermitian form
being explicitly mentioned.

We have the following result established in
[
B13
, Proposition 6.5.1
and Theorem 6.6.1]
.

Proposition 13.14
.

If adjoints are taken with respect to

\epsilon_{X}
, if
e\in TX,f\in T^{*}X
, then

(13.55)

\displaystyle i_{e}^{{\dagger}}=-\overline{e}_{*}\wedge-i_{\overline{e}},

\displaystyle i_{\overline{e}}^{{\dagger}}=e_{*}\wedge-i_{e},

\displaystyle\left(f\wedge\right)^{{\dagger}}=-\overline{f}\wedge,

\displaystyle\left(\overline{f}\wedge\right)^{{\dagger}}=-f\wedge.

If
e\in TX,E=e+\overline{e}\in T_{\mathbf{R}}X
, then

e_{*}\wedge-i_{E},\overline{e}_{*}\wedge+i_{E}
are skew-adjoint with respect to
\epsilon_{X}
.

13.9.
Self-adjointness of
\mathcal{A}_{Z},\mathcal{B}_{Z}

Note that

p_{*}\left(\Lambda\left(T^{*}M\right)\widehat{\otimes}E_{M}\right)

is a
\Lambda\left(T_{\mathbf{C}}^{*}S\right)
-module.

We will use the conventions of
Subsection
10.3
when taking adjoints of antiholomorphic
superconnections over the base
S
. These adjoints will be taken with respect to the Hermitian forms

\epsilon_{X}
or
\eta_{X}
, and will be denoted
with a
†
. The adjoints of
\mathcal{A}_{Z}
and of its
components
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Z},\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{Z}
are
taken with respect to
\epsilon_{X}
, and the adjoints of

\mathcal{B}_{Z}
and of its components
\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{Z},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{Z}
are taken with respect to

\eta_{X}
. In the case of
\mathcal{A}_{Z}
or of its components, the
adjoint could be taken as well with respect to the standard Hermitian
form
\epsilon_{M}
.

Let

z_{-}
be the section of
\underline{q}^{*}TX
given by

(13.56)

z_{-}\left(x,Y\right)=-z\left(x,-Y\right).

Then
z_{-}
is still holomorphic.
Let

Z_{-}
be the corresponding section of

\underline{q}^{*}T_{\mathbf{R}}X
.

Theorem 13.15
.

The operators
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Z},\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{Z}

are of total degree
1
, and the operators

\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{Z},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{Z}
are of
total degree
-1
. Moreover, we have the identities

(13.57)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime{\dagger}}_{Z}=\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{Z_{-}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime{\dagger}}_{Z}=\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{Z_{-}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{{\dagger}}_{Z}=\mathcal{A}_{Z_{-}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{{\dagger}}_{Z}=\mathcal{B}_{Z_{-}}.

Proof.

The proof is the same as in
[
B13
, Theorem 6.5.2]
. For

\mathcal{A}_{Z}
and its components, it is an easy consequence of
(
13.52
) and of Proposition
13.14
.
∎

Remark 13.16
.

When considering
\mathcal{A}_{Z}
and its components, we may
take adjoints with respect to
\epsilon_{M}
, and the above
results would still hold. Adjoints with respect to

\epsilon_{M}
are simply classical adjoints. Using integration
along the fiber
p_{*}
to evaluate
\epsilon_{M}
is enough to
explain the coincidence of the adjoints.

-\nabla^{V}_{R^{\widehat{TX}}\widehat{Y}}-\left\langle R^{\widehat{TX}}\overline{\widehat{w}}_{i},\overline{\widehat{w}}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{\widehat{w}}^{i}i_{\overline{\widehat{w}}_{j}}+A^{E_{0},2}.

13.13.
Hypoelliptic and elliptic superconnections

In this Subsection, we assume that
\widehat{g}^{TX}=g^{TX}

with respect to

We use the identification
in (
13.46
). Set

(13.89)

\displaystyle\widehat{L}=-iw^{j}\wedge\overline{\widehat{w}}^{j},

\displaystyle\widehat{\Lambda}=ii_{\overline{\widehat{w}}_{j}}i_{w_{j}}.

In (

As in

The right-hand side of (

) is a harmonic oscillator.

Let
\mathcal{S}^{(0,\scriptsize\bullet)}\left(\widehat{TX},\pi^{*}\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\right)
be the Schwartz space of rapidly decreasing sections of

\pi^{*}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\right)
along the fibre
\widehat{TX}
.
The operator in
(
13.90
) is essentially self-adjoint on

\mathcal{S}^{(0,\scriptsize\bullet)}\left(\widehat{TX},\pi^{*}\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\right)
. By
[
B90
, Proposition 1.5 and Theorem
1.6]
34
34

34

In

]
, the operator

\overline{\partial}^{V}+i_{\sqrt{-1}y}
and its adjoint were considered

the minor discrepancies with respect to what is done here.
,

[
B13
, Section 6.9]
, its spectrum is

\mathbf{N}
, and its kernel is
1
-dimensional and spanned by

(13.91)

\delta=\exp\left(i\widehat{\omega}^{\mathcal{X},V}-\left|Y\right|^{2}_{g^{TX}}/2\right).

Recall that
dY
is the volume form along the fibers

\widehat{TX}
with respect to
g^{\widehat{TX}}
. Note that

(13.92)

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}\delta=\delta,

\displaystyle\left(2\pi\right)^{-n}\int_{\widehat{TX}}\left|\delta\right|^{2}dY=1.

Let

P
be the
L_{2}

orthogonal projection operator on vector space spanned by

\delta
.

We embed

\mathcal{D}=\Omega^{0,\scriptsize\bullet}\left(X,D|_{X}\right)
into

\Omega\left(X,D|_{X}\widehat{\otimes}\mathbf{I}\right)
by the

\alpha\to\underline{\pi}^{*}\alpha\wedge\delta
, so that

\mathcal{D}

\overline{\partial}^{V}+i_{y}+\overline{\partial}^{V*}+\overline{y}_{*}\wedge
.
More generally, we embed
p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}
into
p_{*}\left[\mathscr{E}_{0}\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\left(\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathbf{I}\right)\right]
by the same formula.

Recall that the elliptic superconnection

A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}}

was defined in Definition
10.8
, and that the differential
operator

was

). Now we give an extension of
[

, Theorem

.

Theorem 13.24
.

The following identity holds:

(13.93)

P\mathcal{E}P=A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}}.

Proof.

Observe that (
13.93
) is an identity of differential operators
over
M
, which we will prove by local methods on
M
. We will use the considerations that follow (
13.40
) to evaluate
\mathcal{E}
.

Let
\nabla^{\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)}
be the connection induced by

\nabla^{TX}
on
\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)
. Since
g^{TX}=\widehat{g}^{TX}
, we get

(13.94)

\nabla^{\Lambda\left(T^{*}X\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathbf{I}}\delta=0.

Recall that
C=B+B^{*}
.
As explained
after equation (
13.40
), conjugation by

\exp\left(i\Lambda\right)
changes
\overline{w}^{i}
into

\overline{w}^{i}+i_{w_{i}}
. Recall that
\delta
in
(
13.91
) is of total degree
0
. The effect of the
composition of the
i_{w_{j}}
is to map
\delta
into forms of total
positive degree
35
35

35

Recall that the total degree is the
difference of the antiholomorphic and of the holomorphic degree.
,
which are orthogonal to
\delta
. Using (
13.94
) and proceeding as in the proof of
[
B13
, Theorem 6.9.2]
, we
find that

(13.95)

P\exp\left(i\Lambda\right)\left(\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}\prime\prime}+B\right)\exp\left(-i\Lambda\right)P=\nabla^{D\prime\prime}+B.

In
[
B13
]
, the proof of (
13.95
) uses equations
(
13.63
), (
13.11
).

Using (

), we can rewrite (

) in the form

(13.96)

P\exp\left(i\Lambda\right)\left(\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}\prime\prime}+B\right)\exp\left(-i\Lambda\right)P=A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime}.

Conjugation by
\exp\left(i\Lambda\right)
changes

w^{i}
into
w^{i}-i_{\overline{w}_{i}}
.
By proceeding again as in
[
B13
, Theorem 6.9.2]
, we
get

(13.97)

P\exp\left(i\Lambda\right)\left(\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}\prime}+B^{*}-q^{*}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)\exp\left(-i\Lambda\right)P\\
=\nabla^{D\prime,S}+\nabla^{D\prime\prime,X*}_{\alpha}+{}^{c}B^{*}.

By (
10.22
), we can rewrite (
13.97
) in the form,

(13.98)

P\exp\left(i\Lambda\right)\left(\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}\prime}+B^{*}-q^{*}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)\exp\left(-i\Lambda\right)P=A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime}.

By (
13.96
), (
13.98
), we get (
13.93
). The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 13.25
.

Recall that

\mathcal{D}_{Y,b}
was defined in (
13.87
), and
that it splits as

(13.99)

\mathcal{D}_{Y,b}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{Y,b}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{Y,b}.

By (
13.57
), when taking adjoints with respect to

\eta_{X}
, then

(13.100)

\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime{\dagger}}_{Y,b}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{Y,b}.

By (
13.50
), (
13.92
), on

\Omega^{0,\scriptsize\bullet}\left(X,D|_{X}\right)
,

\eta_{X}
restrict to the Hermitian product
\left\langle\,\right\rangle_{L_{2}}
.
Since the contribution of
\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}\prime\prime}+B
to
P\mathcal{E}P
is
A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime}
, the
contribution of its adjoint

\nabla^{D\widehat{\otimes}q^{*}\mathbf{I}\prime}+B^{*}-q^{*}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}
has to be

A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime}
. This explains some aspects of
the proof of Theorem
13.24
.

14.
The hypoelliptic superconnection forms

The purpose of this Section is to construct the superconnection forms
associated with the hypoelliptic superconnections of Section

13
, and to prove that their class in Bott-Chern
cohomology coincides with the class of the elliptic superconnection
forms.

This Section is organized as follows. In Subsection

14.1
, we construct the hypoelliptic superconnection
forms, that depend on metrics
g^{TX},g^{\widehat{TX}},g^{D}
.

In Subsection
14.2
, when
g^{TX}=\widehat{g}^{TX}
, we
show that when replacing
g^{\widehat{TX}}
by

b^{4}g^{\widehat{TX}}
, as
b\to 0
, the hypoelliptic superconnection
forms converge to the elliptic superconnection forms, which gives the main result of
this Section.

In this Section, we make the same assumptions as in Sections

10
and
13
, and we use the
corresponding notation.

14.1.
The hypoelliptic forms

We will say that two metrics
g^{\widehat{TX}},g^{\widehat{TX}\prime}
lie in the same projective class if there exists a constant
c>0

such that
g^{\widehat{TX}\prime}=cg^{\widehat{TX}}
. Let

\mathbf{p}
be such a projective class. Once we fix

g^{\widehat{TX}}\in\mathbf{p}
, then
\mathbf{p}\simeq\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
.

We fix
\mathbf{p}
.
Let

\mathscr{Q}_{\mathbf{p}}
be the collection of parameters

\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}}
, with
g^{\widehat{TX}}\in\mathbf{p}
. We denote by
d^{\mathscr{Q}_{\mathbf{p}}}

the de Rham operator on
\mathscr{Q}_{\mathbf{p}}
. A key fact is that
when
g^{\widehat{TX}}\in\mathbf{p}
, then
\nabla^{\mathbf{I}}
does
not depend on
g^{\widehat{TX}}
.

By

[
BL08
, Section 3.3]
, the operator

\exp\left(-\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Y}\right)
is trace class, and is
given by a smooth kernel along the fibers
\mathcal{X}
that is
rapidly decreasing along the fibre
\widehat{TX}
together with its
derivatives, and this uniformly over
S
.

Definition 14.1
.

Put

(14.1)

\mathrm{ch}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y},\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}}\right)=\varphi\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Y}\right)\right].

The Hermitian form
\epsilon_{X}
was defined in Definition

13.12
, and depends on the splitting of
E
, and
also on
\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}}
.

Given
\mathbf{p}
,

\epsilon_{X}^{-1}d^{\mathscr{Q}_{\mathbf{p}}}\epsilon_{X}
is a
1
-form on
\mathscr{Q}_{\mathbf{p}}
with values in

\epsilon_{X}
-self-adjoint endomorphisms. It can be easily computed from
equation (
13.52
).

We establish an extension of
[
B13
, Theorem 7.3.2]
.

Theorem 14.2
.

The form

\mathrm{ch}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y},\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}}\right)

lies in

\Omega^{(=)}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right)
, it is closed, and its Bott-Chern cohomology
class does not depend on
\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}}
, or on
the splitting of
E
.

Given
\mathbf{p}
, then

(14.2)

d^{\mathscr{Q}_{\mathbf{p}}}\mathrm{ch}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y},\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}}\right)=-\frac{\overline{\partial}^{S}\partial^{S}}{2i\pi}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\epsilon_{X}^{-1}d^{\mathscr{Q}_{\mathbf{p}}}\epsilon_{X}\exp\left(-\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Y}\right)\right].

Proof.

The proof that the above forms are closed is formally the same
as the proof of Theorem
10.15
. Since
\mathcal{A}_{Y}
is self-adjoint with
respect to
\epsilon_{X}
, the same argument as in
the proof of Theorem
8.2
shows that
the above forms are real. The fact that
\epsilon_{X}
is non-positive is irrelevant. By proceeding as in the proof of Theorem

10.15
, i.e., by using deformation over
\mathbf{P}^{1}
, one
can prove that their corresponding class in Bott-Chern cohomology
does not depend on the parameters. Finally, if one restricts

g^{\widehat{TX}}
to vary in a class
\mathbf{p}
, the proof
of (
14.2
) is the same as the proof of Theorems

8.2
and
10.15
.
∎

Definition 14.3
.

Let

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y}\right)\in H_{\mathrm{BC}}^{(=)}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right)
denote the common
classes in Bott-Chern cohomology of the forms

\mathrm{ch}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y},\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}}\right)
.

14.2.
The limit as
b\to 0
of the hypoelliptic superconnection
forms

In this Subsection, we fix
\omega^{X},g^{D}
, and we take

g^{\widehat{TX}}=b^{4}g^{TX}
.Then
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y}
does not depend on
b>0
.

Recall that the elliptic superconnection forms

\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},g^{D}\right)
were defined in Definition

10.14
, and their Bott-Chern class

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}\prime\prime}\right)
in Definition
10.16
.

Theorem 14.4
.

As
b\to 0
,

(14.3)

\mathrm{ch}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y},\omega^{X},g^{D},b^{4}g^{TX}\right)\to\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime},\omega^{X},g^{D}\right).

Proof.

We use the notation of Subsection
13.12
. By definition,

(14.4)

\mathrm{ch}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y},\omega^{X},g^{D},b^{4}g^{TX}\right)=\varphi\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Y,b}\right)\right].

By (
13.84
)–(
13.87
), we can rewrite
(
14.4
) in the form

(14.5)

\mathrm{ch}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y},\omega^{X},g^{D},b^{4}g^{TX}\right)=\varphi\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-\mathcal{D}^{2}_{Y,b}\right)\right].

Using (
13.88
), Theorem
13.24
, and by
proceeding exactly as in the proofs of
[
BL08
, Theorem
5.2.1]
,
[
B08
, Theorem 7.13]
, and

[
B13
, Theorem 7.6.2]
, we get (
14.3
). The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Theorem 14.5
.

The following identities hold:

(14.6)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{p_{*}\mathscr{E}_{0}\prime\prime}\right)=\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(Rp_{*}\mathscr{E}\right)\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right).

Proof.

This is an obvious consequence of Theorems
11.10
,

14.2
, and
14.4
.
∎

15.
The hypoelliptic superconnection forms when

\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}\omega^{X}=0

The purpose of this Section is to prove Theorem
10.1
when

\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}\omega^{X}=0
using the hypoelliptic
superconnection forms of Section
14
. This result had
already been proved in Section
12
using elliptic
superconnection forms. While the hypoelliptic superconnection forms
do not give a better result, the techniques used in the present Section
prepare for Section
16
, where Theorem
10.1
will
be established in full generality using deformations of our
hypoelliptic superconnections.

This Section is organized as follows. In Subsection

15.1
, we compute finite and infinite-dimensional
supertraces on certain model operators. These computations will later
be used in a local index theoretic context.

In Subsection
15.2
, given
t>0
, when replacing

\left(\omega^{X},g^{\widehat{TX}},g^{D}\right)
by

\left(\omega^{X}/t,g^{\widehat{TX}}/t^{3},g^{D}\right)
, we compute
the corresponding hypoelliptic curvature, and we establish various
scaling identities.

Finally, in Subsection
15.3
, when

\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}\omega^{X}=0
, we obtain the limit as

t\to 0
of our hypoelliptic superconnections forms, and we prove
Theorem
10.1
. The local index theoretic techniques of this
Subsection will be used again in Subsection
16.6
.

15.1.
Finite and infinite-dimensional traces

Let
W
be a complex vector space of dimension
n
, and let
W_{\mathbf{R}}
be the corresponding real vector space. If

W_{\mathbf{C}}=W_{\mathbf{R}}\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{C}
is its complexification,
then
W_{\mathbf{C}}=W\oplus\overline{W}
.

Let
\mathfrak{W}
be
another copy of
W
. Observe that

(15.1)

\Lambda\left(\overline{\mathfrak{W}}\oplus\overline{\mathfrak{W}}^{*}\right)=\Lambda\left(\overline{\mathfrak{W}}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\Lambda\left(\overline{\mathfrak{W}}^{*}\right).

Let

w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}
be a basis of
W
, let
w^{1},\ldots,w^{n}
be
the associated dual basis of
W^{*}
. Let
\mathfrak{w}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{w}_{n}
be the corresponding basis of
\mathfrak{W}
, and let

\mathfrak{w}^{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{w}^{n}
the associated dual basis of

\mathfrak{W}^{*}
. The supertrace maps
\Lambda\left(\overline{\mathfrak{W}}\oplus\overline{\mathfrak{W}}^{*}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{End}\left(\Lambda\left(\overline{W}^{*}\right)\right)
into
\Lambda\left(\overline{\mathfrak{W}}\oplus\overline{\mathfrak{W}}^{*}\right)
and vanishes
on supercommutators.

Note that

\Lambda^{n}\left(\overline{\mathfrak{W}}^{*}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\Lambda^{n}\left(\overline{\mathfrak{W}}\right)
is canonically trivial, and
\overline{\alpha}=\prod_{1}^{n}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{i}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}
is the canonical section. If
\theta\in\Lambda\left(\overline{\mathfrak{W}}^{*}\right)\widehat{\otimes}\Lambda\left(\overline{\mathfrak{W}}\right)
, let
\theta^{\max}\in\mathbf{C}

be the coefficient of
\overline{\alpha}
in the expansion of
\theta
.

If
A\in\mathrm{End}\left(W\right)
, then we still denote by
A
the
conjugate action of
\overline{A}
on
\overline{W}
.

We establish a form of a result of Mathai-Quillen
[
MQu86
, eq.
(2.13)]
,
[
B08
, Proposition 6.10]
.

Proposition 15.1
.

If
A\in\mathrm{End}\left(W\right)
, then

(15.2)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-\overline{w}^{i}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}+i_{\overline{w}_{j}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{j}+\left\langle A\overline{w}_{i},\overline{w}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}_{j}}\right)\right]^{\max}=\mathrm{Td}^{-1}\left(-\overline{A}\right).

Proof.

If
h\in\overline{W}
, let
\left[h\right]

denote the element that corresponds in
\overline{\mathfrak{W}}
. A similar
notation is used for elements in
\overline{W}^{*}
.

First, we assume that
A
is invertible. Let
\widetilde{A}\in\mathrm{End}\left(W^{*}\right)
be
the transpose of
A
. Put

(15.3)

M=-\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{i}i_{A^{-1}\overline{w}_{i}}-\overline{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}\left[\widetilde{A}^{-1}\overline{w}^{i}\right].

If
f\in W,u\in W^{*}
, then

(15.4)

\displaystyle\exp\left(M\right)\overline{u}\exp\left(-M\right)=\overline{u}-\left[\widetilde{A}^{-1}\overline{u}\right],

\displaystyle\exp\left(M\right)i_{\overline{f}}\exp\left(-M\right)=i_{\overline{f}}-\left[A^{-1}\overline{f}\right].

By (
15.4
), we deduce that

(15.5)

\exp\left(M\right)\left\langle A\overline{w}_{i},\overline{w}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}_{j}}\exp\left(-M\right)=\left\langle A\overline{w}_{i},\overline{w}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}_{j}}\\
-\overline{w}^{i}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}+i_{\overline{w}_{j}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{j}+\left\langle A^{-1}\overline{w}_{i},\overline{w}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{i}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}_{j}.

Since supertraces vanish on supercommutators, we deduce from
(
15.5
) that

(15.6)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-\overline{w}^{i}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}+i_{\overline{w}_{j}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{j}+\left\langle A\overline{w}_{i},\overline{w}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}_{j}}\right)\right]\\
=\exp\left(-\left\langle A^{-1}\overline{w}_{i},\overline{w}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{i}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}_{j}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(\left\langle A\overline{w}_{i},\overline{w}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}^{j}}\right)\right].

Also,

(15.7)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(\left\langle A\overline{w}_{i},\overline{w}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}_{j}}\right)\right]=\det\left(1-\exp\left(\overline{A}\right)\right).

If
A
is diagonalizable, we get

(15.8)

\left[\exp\left(-\left\langle A^{-1}\overline{w}_{i},\overline{w}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{i}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}_{j}\right)\right]^{\max}=\left[\det\left(-\overline{A}\right)\right]^{-1}.

Equation (
15.8
) extends by continuity when
A
is only
supposed to be invertible.
By (
15.6
)–(
15.8
), we get (
15.2
) when

A
is invertible. This equation extends by continuity to arbitrary

A
. The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

Let
\widehat{W}
be another copy of
W
. Let
g^{\widehat{W}}
be a Hermitian metric on

\widehat{W}
, and let

\widehat{g}^{W}
be the corresponding metric on
W
. Let
\left(U,Y\right)
be the generic element of
W_{\mathbf{R}}\oplus\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}
. Let
dUdY
be
the associated volume form on

W_{\mathbf{R}}\oplus\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}
. Let
\Delta^{\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}}
be the Laplacian on

\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}
. Let
F\in\mathrm{End}\left(\widehat{W}\right)
be
skew-adjoint. Then
F
acts as antisymmetric endomorphism of

\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}
. Let

\nabla^{\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}}_{FY}
denote differentiation along the vector
field
FY
. Let
\nabla^{W_{\mathbf{R}}}_{Y}
denote differentiation along

W_{\mathbf{R}}
in the direction
Y
.

Let

\mathfrak{p}_{0}
be the scalar operator,

(15.9)

\mathfrak{p}_{0}=-\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}}+\nabla^{\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}}_{FY}+\nabla^{W_{\mathbf{R}}}_{Y}.

Let
s_{0}\left(\left(U,Y\right),\left(U^{\prime},Y^{\prime}\right)\right)
denote
the smooth kernel associated with
\exp\left(-\mathfrak{p}_{0}\right)
with respect to the volume

\frac{dU^{\prime}dY^{\prime}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2n}}
.

Now we state a result taken from
[
B08
, Theorem 6.8]
.

Theorem 15.2
.

If
\left|F\right|<2\pi
, the function

s_{0}\left(\left(0,Y\right),\left(0,Y\right)\right)
is
integrable, and we have the identity,

(15.10)

\int_{\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}}s_{0}\left(\left(0,Y\right),\left(0,Y\right)\right)\frac{dY}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}=\widehat{A}^{2}\left(F|_{W_{\mathbf{R}}}\right)=\mathrm{Td}\left(F|_{W}\right)\mathrm{Td}\left(-F|_{W}\right).

Proof.

We will give a self-contained proof of (
15.10
). Let

\mathfrak{p}_{0,\xi}
be the Fourier transform of
\mathfrak{p}_{0}

in the variable
U
. If
\xi\in W^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}\simeq W_{\mathbf{R}}
, then

(15.11)

\mathfrak{p}_{0,\xi}=-\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}}+\nabla^{\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}}_{FY}+2i\pi\left\langle\xi,Y\right\rangle.

Assume that
F
is invertible. Put

(15.12)

\mathfrak{q}_{0,\xi}=e^{2i\pi\left\langle\xi,F^{-1}Y\right\rangle}\mathfrak{p}_{0,\xi}e^{-2i\pi\left\langle\xi,F^{-1}Y\right\rangle}.

Since
F
is skew-adjoint, we get

(15.13)

\mathfrak{q}_{0,\xi}=-\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}}+\nabla^{\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}}_{FY-2i\pi F^{-1}\xi}+2\pi^{2}\left|F^{-1}\xi\right|^{2}

Let
\mathfrak{r}_{0,\xi}
be the operator deduced from
\mathfrak{q}_{0,\xi}

by the translation
Y\to Y+2i\pi F^{-2}\xi
.
Then

(15.14)

\mathfrak{r}_{0,\xi}=-\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}}+\nabla^{\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}}_{FY}+2\pi^{2}\left|F^{-1}\xi\right|^{2}.

Since
F
is skew-adjoint, the operators in the right-hand side of (
15.14
) are
commuting, and the smooth kernel
\mathbf{r}_{0,\xi}\left(Y,Y^{\prime}\right)
for

\exp\left(-\mathfrak{r}_{0,\xi}\right)
with respect to

\frac{dY^{\prime}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}
is given by

(15.15)

\mathbf{r}_{0,\xi}\left(Y,Y^{\prime}\right)=\exp\left(-2\pi^{2}\left|F^{-1}\xi\right|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left|e^{-F}Y-Y^{\prime}\right|^{2}\right).

By (
15.15
), if
\mathbf{q}_{0,\xi}\left(Y,Y^{\prime}\right)
is the smooth
kernel for
\exp\left(-\mathfrak{q}_{0,\xi}\right)
with respect to

\frac{dY^{\prime}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}
, then

(15.16)

\mathbf{q}_{0,\xi}\left(Y,Y^{\prime}\right)=\exp\left(-2\pi^{2}\left|F^{-1}\xi\right|^{2}\right)\\
\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left|e^{-F}\left(Y-2i\pi F^{-2}\xi\right)-\left(Y^{\prime}-2i\pi F^{-2}\xi\right)\right|^{2}\right).

By (
15.16
), we deduce that if
1-e^{-F}
is invertible,
then

(15.17)

\int_{\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}}\mathbf{q}_{0,\xi}\left(Y,Y\right)\frac{dY}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}=\frac{\exp\left(-2\pi^{2}\left|F^{-1}\xi\right|^{2}\right)}{\det\left(1-e^{-F}\right)|_{W_{\mathbf{R}}}}.

Using (
15.17
), we deduce that if
1-e^{-F}
is invertible,
then

(15.18)

\int_{\widehat{W}_{\mathbf{R}}}s_{0}\left(\left(0,Y\right),\left(0,Y\right)\right)\frac{dY}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}=\det\left(\frac{F}{1-e^{-F}}\right)|_{W_{\mathbf{R}}},

which is equivalent to (
15.10
). The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 15.3
.

Using (
15.16
) and inverting the Fourier transform, one
can give an exact formula for the kernel

s_{0}\left(\left(U,Y\right),\left(U^{\prime},Y^{\prime}\right)\right)
.

(15.52)

\mathfrak{P}_{0,s,x}=-\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{V}_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}+\overline{\widehat{w}}^{i}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}-i_{\overline{\widehat{w}}_{i}}\mathfrak{w}_{i}-\nabla^{V}_{R^{\widehat{TX}}Y}\\
-\left\langle R_{x}^{\widehat{TX}}\overline{\widehat{w}}_{i},\overline{\widehat{w}}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{\widehat{w}}^{i}i_{\overline{\widehat{w}}_{j}}+\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R},x}X}_{Y}+A^{E_{0},2},

the tensors are evaluated at
\left(s,x\right)
.

We claim that as in
[
B08
, Theorem 6.7]
and in

[
B13
, Theorem 9.1.1]
, as
t\to 0
,

(15.53)

\mathfrak{P}_{t,x}\to\mathfrak{P}_{0,s,x},

in the sense that the coefficients of the operator
\mathfrak{P}_{t,x}

converge uniformly together with all
their derivatives uniformly over compact sets towards the
corresponding coefficients of
\mathfrak{P}_{0,x}
. To establish
(
15.53
), we will use equation (
15.27
) for

\mathfrak{M}_{t}
, and we will exploit the fact that

\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}\omega^{X}=0
.

The first two terms in the right-hand side of (
15.27
)
do not cause any problem. Using the considerations that follow
(
15.47
), the limit as
t\to 0
of the contribution of the
last two terms in the first line of
(
15.27
) is given by
\overline{\widehat{w}}^{i}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}-i_{\overline{\widehat{w}}_{i}}\mathfrak{w}_{i}
.

By (
13.65
), (
13.79
), we get

(15.54)

t^{3/2}{}^{1}\widehat{\nabla}^{\mathbf{F}}_{t,Y}=t^{3/2}\widehat{\nabla}^{\mathbf{F},-1}_{Y}+t^{1/2}\left(-i_{y}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}+\overline{\gamma y_{*}}\right).

In our given trivialization, at
\left(U,Y\right)\in\mathbb{H}_{x}
, the section of
T_{\mathbf{R},x}X
that
corresponds to the section
Y
of
T_{\mathbf{R}}X
is just the tautological
section
Y\in T_{\mathbf{R},x}X
. Let
\Gamma^{\widehat{TX}}
denote the
connection form for
\widehat{\nabla}^{TX}
in the given trivialization.
In the above coordinate system the vector field
Y^{H}
, as a section
of
\mathbb{H}_{x}
, splits as

(15.55)

Y^{H}\left(U,Y\right)=Y\oplus\left(-\Gamma_{U}^{\widehat{TX}}\left(Y\right)Y\right).

Recall that
TX,\widehat{TX}
have been trivialized by parallel
transport with respect to
\widehat{\nabla}^{TX,-1},\nabla^{\widehat{TX}}
. Let

\widehat{\Gamma}^{TX,-1}
denote the connection form
for
\widehat{\nabla}^{TX,-1}
in this trivialization of
TX
. The
lift
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}X\right),-1}
of
\widehat{\Gamma}^{TX,-1}
to

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}X\right)
is given by

(15.56)

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}X\right),-1}=-\left\langle\widehat{\Gamma}^{TX,-1}w_{i},w^{j}\right\rangle w^{i}i_{w_{j}}-\left\langle\overline{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{TX,-1}\overline{w}_{i},\overline{w}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}_{j}}.

Also, near
U=0
,

(15.57)

\widehat{\Gamma}^{TX,-1}_{U}=\mathcal{O}\left(U\right).

Let
d^{T_{\mathbf{R},x}X}
be the de Rham operator on
T_{\mathbf{R},x}X
. Let

\Gamma^{\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)},\Gamma^{D}

be the connection forms associated with

\nabla^{\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)},\nabla^{D}
in the
given trivializations. By (
15.56
), we get

(15.58)

\widehat{\nabla}^{\mathbb{F},-1}=d^{T_{\mathbf{R},x}X}-\left\langle\widehat{\Gamma}^{TX,-1}w_{i},w^{j}\right\rangle w^{i}i_{w_{j}}-\left\langle\overline{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{TX,-1}\overline{w}_{i},\overline{w}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}_{j}}\\
+\Gamma^{\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)}+\Gamma^{D}.

By (
15.55
), (
15.58
), we get

(15.59)

\widehat{\nabla}^{\mathbf{F},-1}_{Y}=\widehat{\nabla}^{\mathbb{F},-1}_{Y^{H}}=\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R},x}X}_{Y}-\nabla^{\widehat{T_{\mathbf{R},x}X}}_{\Gamma_{U}^{\widehat{TX}}\left(Y\right)Y}\\
-\left\langle\widehat{\Gamma}^{TX,-1}_{U}\left(Y\right)w_{i},w^{j}\right\rangle w^{i}i_{w_{j}}-\left\langle\overline{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{TX,-1}_{U}\left(Y\right)\overline{w}_{i},\overline{w}^{j}\right\rangle\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}_{j}}\\
+\Gamma^{\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)}_{U}\left(Y\right)+\Gamma^{D}_{U}\left(Y\right).

By (
15.58
), we get

(15.60)

I_{t^{3/2}}t^{3/2}\widehat{\nabla}^{\mathbf{F},-1}_{Y}I_{t^{-3/2}}=\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R},x}X}_{Y}-t^{3/2}\nabla^{\widehat{T_{\mathbf{R},x}X}}_{\Gamma_{t^{3/2}U}^{\widehat{TX}}\left(Y\right)Y}\\
-\left\langle\widehat{\Gamma}_{t^{3/2}U}^{TX,-1}\left(Y\right)w_{i},w_{j}\right\rangle t^{3/2}w^{i}i_{w_{j}}-\left\langle\overline{\widehat{\Gamma}}_{t^{3/2}U}^{TX,-1}\left(Y\right)\overline{w}_{i},\overline{w}_{j}\right\rangle t^{3/2}\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}_{j}}\\
+t^{3/2}\Gamma_{t^{3/2}U}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}\right)}\left(Y\right)+t^{3/2}\Gamma^{D}_{t^{3/2}U}\left(Y\right).

Let us now consider the effect on (
15.60
) of the conjugation
by
\exp\left(-\mathfrak{o}/t^{3/2}\right)
, that only affects the
third and fourth terms in the right-hand side. As mentioned before,
the terms

t^{3/2}w^{i}i_{w_{j}},t^{3/2}\overline{w}^{i}i_{\overline{w}_{j}}

are replaced by
w^{i}\left(t^{3/2}i_{w_{j}}+\mathfrak{w}_{j}\right),\overline{w}^{i}\left(t^{3/2}i_{\overline{w}_{j}}+\overline{\mathfrak{w}}_{j}\right)
. Because of
(
15.57
), in (
15.60
), these terms do not survive as

t\to 0
.

Ultimately, we find that after all the conjugations and rescalings, as

t\to 0
,

(15.61)

t^{3/2}\widehat{\nabla}^{\mathbf{F},-1}_{Y}\to\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R},x}X}_{Y}.

Let us now consider the second term in the right-hand side of
(
15.54
). This term is a section of

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
. In the parallel transport
trivialization with respect to
\widehat{\nabla}^{TX,-1}
, it remains so. Also it
is unaffected by the conjugation by
\exp\left(-\mathfrak{o}/t^{3/2}\right)
. Combining (
15.54
) and (
15.61
), we
find that as
t\to 0
, after conjugations and rescalings,

(15.62)

t^{3/2}{}^{1}\widehat{\nabla}^{\mathbf{F}}_{t,Y}\to\nabla^{T_{\mathbf{R},x}X}_{Y}.

For the same reasons as before, as
t\to 0
, in
(
15.27
), the term
t^{3/2}i_{Y}C

disappears. Also we made the assumption that

\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}\omega^{X}=0
. The last three terms in
the right-hand side of (
15.27
) can be handled exactly
as before.

Putting together the previous considerations gives a proof of
(
15.53
).

Let

S_{0,s,x}\left(\left(U,Y\right),\left(U^{\prime},Y^{\prime}\right)\right)
be the
smooth kernel associated with
\exp\left(-\mathfrak{P}_{0,s,x}\right)

with respect to the volume
\frac{dU^{\prime}dY^{\prime}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2n}}
.
By proceeding as in
[
BL08
, Theorem 4.10.1]
,
there exist
C>0,c>0
such that for
t\in]0,1],x\in X
,

(15.63)

\left|S_{t,x}\left(\left(0,Y\right),\left(0,Y\right)\right)\right|\leq C\exp\left(-c\left|Y\right|^{2/3}\right).

By proceeding as in the same reference, and using (
15.53
),
we find that as
t\to 0
,

(15.64)

S_{t,x}\left(\left(U,Y\right),\left(U^{\prime},Y^{\prime}\right)\right)\to S_{0,s,x}\left(\left(U,Y\right),\left(U^{\prime},Y\right)\right).

By (
15.51
), (
15.63
), and (
15.64
), we find
that as
t\to 0
,

(15.65)

n_{t}\left(x\right)\to n_{0,s}\left(x\right)\\
=\varphi\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}\int_{\widehat{T_{\mathbf{R},x}X}}\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{\Lambda\left(\overline{\widehat{T^{*}_{x}X}}\right)\widehat{\otimes}D_{s,x}}\left[\left[S_{0,s,x}\left(\left(0,Y\right),\left(0,Y\right)\right)1\right]^{\max}\right]\frac{dY}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}.

By (
15.63
), there is
C>0
such that for
x\in X,t\in]0,1]
,

(15.66)

\left|n_{t}\left(x\right)\right|\leq C.

By (
15.39
), (
15.65
), and (
15.66
), we
conclude that as
t\to 0
,

(15.67)

\mathrm{ch}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y},\omega^{X}/t,g^{\widehat{TX}}/t^{3}\right)\to\int_{X}n_{0,s}\left(x\right)\widehat{dx}.

We will evaluate the integrand in the right-hand side of
(
15.65
). Observe that

(15.68)

\beta\overline{\beta}=\left(-1\right)^{n}\prod_{1}^{n}w^{i}\overline{w}^{i}\prod_{1}^{n}\mathfrak{w}_{i}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}.

From now on, we will view the

w^{i},\overline{w}^{i}
as sections of
\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C},x}X\right)
. Then

(15.69)

\prod_{1}^{n}w^{i}\overline{w}^{i}=\left(-i\right)^{n}\widehat{dx}.

If
\epsilon\in\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}M\right)
, let

\epsilon^{(2n)}\in\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}M\right)
be the component
of
\epsilon
which is of top vertical degree
2n
. Let

\epsilon^{\max}\in\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}S\right)
be such that

(15.70)

\epsilon^{(2n)}=\epsilon^{\max}\widehat{dx}.

We can identity
\mathfrak{w}_{i}
and

\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{i}
by the metric
\widehat{g}^{T_{x}X}
.
Since
R^{\widehat{TX}}
takes its values in skew-adjoint
endomorphisms of
\widehat{TX}
,
using Proposition
15.1
and Theorem
15.2
, by
(
15.65
), we get

(15.71)

n_{0,s}\left(x\right)=\varphi\frac{1}{\left(2i\pi\right)^{n}}\left[\widehat{A}^{2}\left(R^{\widehat{TX}}\right)\mathrm{Td}^{-1}\left(R^{\widehat{TX}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-A^{E_{0},2}\right)\right]\right]_{s,x}^{\max}.

From the last identity in (
15.10
), we get

(15.72)

\widehat{A}^{2}\left(R^{\widehat{TX}}\right)\mathrm{Td}^{-1}\left(R^{\widehat{TX}}\right)=\mathrm{Td}\left(-R^{\widehat{TX}}\right).

By (
8.1
), (
15.71
), and (
15.72
), we obtain

(15.73)

\int_{X}n_{0}\left(x\right)\widehat{dx}=p_{*}\left[q^{*}\mathrm{Td}\left(TX,\widehat{g}^{TX}\right)\mathrm{ch}\left(A^{E_{0}\prime\prime},g^{D}\right)\right].

By combining (
15.67
) and (
15.73
), we get
(
15.35
). Using Theorem
14.5

and (
15.35
), we get (
15.36
). The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

16.
Exotic superconnections and Riemann-Roch-Grothendieck

The purpose of this Section is to establish Theorem
10.1
,
which is the final step in the proof of our main result. More
precisely, we define deformed hypoelliptic superconnections, which
when introducing a scaling parameter
t>0
, have the proper asymptotics
when
t\to 0
, and this without any assumption on the Kähler form. Most of
the technical tools of local index theory which are needed were
already developed in Section
15
.

This Section is organized as follows. In Subsection

16.1
, following
[
B13
, Chapter 11]
, we
introduce a deformation
\omega^{X}_{\theta}
of the Kähler form

\omega^{X}
, that depends on
\theta=\left(c,d\right)
, with
c\in\left[0,1\right],d>0
, and coincides with
\omega^{X}
for
c=0
. This deformation
\omega^{X}_{\theta}
also depends on
the coordinate
Y\in\widehat{T_{\mathbf{R}}X}
. Given a holomorphic section

z
of
\underline{q}^{*}TX
, we construct a superconnection

A_{\theta,Z}
.

In Subsection
16.2
, we give a Lichnerowicz formula
for
A^{2}_{\theta,Z}
.

In Subsection
16.3
, we take
z=y
, in which case the
curvature is still hypoelliptic. We define corresponding deformed
hypoelliptic superconnection forms, and we show that their Bott-Chern
class is the same as the class of our previous hypoelliptic
superconnection forms.

In Subsection
16.4
, we introduce the parameter
t>0
, and
we establish various scaling identities.

In Subsection
16.5
, we make
c=1
, and we state uniform
estimates on the heat kernels associated with the curvature.

In Subsection
16.6
, we recall a result of

[
B13
]
on the explicit construction of hypoelliptic
superconnection forms of vector bundles.

In Subsection
16.7
, when
c=1
and with
d

now depending explicitly on
t
, we compute the asymptotics of the
deformed hypoelliptic superconnection forms.

Finally, in Subsection
16.8
, we prove Theorem

10.1
.

We make the same assumptions as in Sections
14
and

15
, and we
use the corresponding notation.

16.1.
A deformation of the Kähler form
\omega^{X}

Here, we follow
[
B13
, Chapter 11]
.

Definition 16.1
.

If

\theta=\left(c,d\right),0\leq c\leq 1,d>0
, put

(16.1)

\omega^{X}_{\theta}=\left(1-c+\frac{cd}{2}\left|Y\right|_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}^{2}\right)\omega^{X}.

Equivalently,

(16.2)

\omega^{X}_{\theta}=\omega^{X}+c\left(\frac{d}{2}\left|Y\right|^{2}_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}-1\right)\omega^{X}.

We repeat the constructions of Section

13
, except that
\omega^{X}
is now replaced by

\omega^{X}_{\theta}
. An extra subscript
\theta
will be introduced to
distinguish the objects constructed here from the ones in
Section
13
.
Instead of (
13.35
), we have

(16.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Z}=\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}+i_{z},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{Z,\theta}=e^{q^{*}i\omega^{X}_{\theta}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}+i_{\overline{z}}\right)e^{-q^{*}i\omega^{X}_{\theta}},\qquad\mathcal{A}_{Z,\theta}=\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Z}+\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{Z,\theta}.

The following result was established in
[
B13
, Theorem
11.1.2]
.

Theorem 16.2
.

The following identities hold:

(16.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{Z,\theta}=\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{Z}+c\left(\frac{d}{2}\left|Y\right|_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}^{2}-1\right)\left(\overline{z}_{*}-q^{*}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)+cdq^{*}i\omega^{X}i_{\overline{\widehat{y}}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{Z,\theta}=\mathcal{A}_{Z}+c\left(\frac{d}{2}\left|Y\right|_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}^{2}-1\right)\left(\overline{z}_{*}-q^{*}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)+cdq^{*}i\omega^{X}i_{\overline{\widehat{y}}}.

Proof.

This is a consequence of (
13.30
), (
13.37
), and
(
16.2
).
∎

As in (
13.53
), by replacing
\omega^{X}
by

\omega^{X}_{\theta}
, we can define a new Hermitian form

\epsilon_{X,\theta}

associated with
\omega^{X}_{\theta}
. We obtain an analogue of
Theorem
13.15
.

16.2.
A formula for
\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Z,\theta}

We use the notation of Section
13.10
. In
particular
\widehat{w}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{w}_{n}
denotes
an orthonormal basis of
\left(\widehat{TX},g^{\widehat{TX}}\right)
. Let

\widehat{y}_{*}\in\overline{\widehat{T^{*}X}}
be dual to
\widehat{y}
with respect to the
metric
g^{\widehat{TX}}
.

Now, we give a form of
[
B13
, Theorem 11.2.1]
.

Theorem 16.3
.

The following identity holds:

(16.5)

\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Z,\theta}=\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Z}+c\left(\frac{d}{2}\left|Y\right|_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}^{2}-1\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\left|Z\right|^{2}_{g^{TX}}-i_{z}q^{*}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)+cdz_{*}i_{\overline{\widehat{y}}}\\
+c\left(\frac{d}{2}\left|Y\right|^{2}_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}-1\right)\left(\nabla^{TX\prime\prime,H}\overline{z}_{*}-q^{*}\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)+cd\left(q^{*}\overline{\partial}^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)i_{\overline{\widehat{y}}}\\
+c\Biggl(d\widehat{y}_{*}\left(\overline{z}_{*}-q^{*}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)+\left(\frac{d}{2}\left|Y\right|^{2}_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}-1\right)\overline{\widehat{w}}^{i}\overline{\nabla_{\widehat{w}_{i}}z}_{*}\\
+dq^{*}i\omega^{X}\left(\nabla^{V}_{\overline{\widehat{y}}}+\overline{\widehat{w}}^{i}i_{\overline{\widehat{w}}_{i}}\right)\Biggr).

Proof.

Since our fibration is product, and the metric
g^{TX}
is
constant over
S
, the tensor
\sigma
defined in

[
B13
, Definition 2.2.3]
vanishes identically. Using
[
B13
, Theorem
11.2.1]
and equation (
13.70
), we get
(
16.5
). The extra terms in the right-hand side
of (
16.5
) do not depend on
\left(E_{0},A^{E_{0}\prime\prime}\right)
, because
C=B+B^{*}
contains only exterior
products. The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

16.3.
The superconnection
\mathcal{A}_{Y,\theta}

Here we take
z=y
. Using (
13.83
), (
16.5
), we get

(16.6)

\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Y,\theta}=\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Y}+c\left(\frac{d}{2}\left|Y\right|_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}^{2}-1\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\left|Y\right|^{2}_{g^{TX}}-i_{y}q^{*}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)+cdy_{*}i_{\overline{\widehat{y}}}\\
+c\left(\frac{d}{2}\left|Y\right|^{2}_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}-1\right)\left(\overline{\gamma y}_{*}-q^{*}\overline{\partial}^{X}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)+cd\left(q^{*}\overline{\partial}^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)i_{\overline{\widehat{y}}}\\
+c\left(d\widehat{y}_{*}\left(\overline{y}_{*}-q^{*}\partial^{X}i\omega^{X}\right)+\left(\frac{d}{2}\left|Y\right|^{2}_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}-1\right)\overline{\widehat{w}}^{i}\overline{w}_{i*}\right)\\
+cdq^{*}i\omega^{X}\left(\nabla^{V}_{\overline{\widehat{y}}}+\overline{\widehat{w}}^{i}i_{\overline{\widehat{w}}_{i}}\right).

By (
16.6
),
\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Y,\theta}
is
fibrewise hypoelliptic.

Set

(16.7)

V_{\theta}\left(Y\right)=\left(1-c\right)\frac{1}{2}\left|Y\right|^{2}_{g^{TX}}+\frac{cd}{4}\left|Y\right|^{2}_{g^{TX}}\left|Y\right|^{2}_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}.

By (
13.81
), (
13.82
), and (
16.6
), the potential
V_{\theta}

appears in
\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Y,\theta}
.
Given
d>0
, there
exists
C_{d}>0
such that for
c\in\left[0,1\right]
,

(16.8)

V_{\theta}\left(Y\right)\geq C_{d}\left(\left|Y\right|^{2}_{g^{TX}}-1\right).

The condition
d>0
is
crucial for (
16.8
) to hold also at
c=1
.

The same arguments as in
[
B13
, Section 11.3]
and
(
16.6
), (
16.8
) show that
\exp\left(-\mathcal{A}^{2}_{{Y,\theta}}\right)
is fibrewise trace class.

We use the same notation as in Subsection
14.1
. Given

\mathbf{p}
, let

\mathscr{R}_{\mathbf{p}}
be the collection of parameters

\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}},\theta
with
g^{\widehat{TX}}\in\mathbf{p}
. Let
d^{\mathscr{R}_{\mathbf{p}}}
be
the de Rham operator on
\mathscr{R}_{\mathbf{p}}
. Then

\epsilon_{X,\theta}^{-1}d^{\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{p}}}\epsilon_{X,\theta}
is a
1
-form
on
\mathscr{R}_{\mathbf{p}}
with values in
\epsilon_{X,\theta}
-self-adjoint
endomorphisms.

Definition 16.4
.

For
\theta=\left(c,d\right)
,
c\in\left[0,1\right],d>0
, put

(16.9)

\mathrm{ch}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y},\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}},\theta\right)=\varphi\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Y,\theta}\right)\right].

Theorem 16.5
.

The form

\mathrm{ch}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y},\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}},\theta\right)

lies in

\Omega^{(=)}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right)
, it is closed, and its Bott-Chern cohomology
class does not depend on
\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}},\theta
,
or on the splitting of
E
.

Given
\mathbf{p}
,
\varphi\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\epsilon_{X,\theta}^{-1}d^{\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{p}}}\epsilon_{X,\theta}\exp\left(-\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Y,\theta}\right)\right]
is a
1
-form on
\mathscr{R}_{\mathbf{p}}
with values in
\Omega^{(=)}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right)
, and
moreover,

(16.10)

d^{\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{p}}}\mathrm{ch}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y},\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}},\theta\right)=-\frac{\overline{\partial}^{S}\partial^{S}}{2i\pi}\varphi\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\epsilon_{X,\theta}^{-1}d^{\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{p}}}\epsilon_{X,\theta}\exp\left(-\mathcal{A}^{2}_{Y,\theta}\right)\right].

The class of the forms
\mathrm{ch}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y},\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}},\theta\right)
in Bott-Chern cohomology coincides with

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y}\right)
.

Proof.

The proof follows the same lines as the proof of
[
B13
, Theorem
11.3.1]
and of Theorem
14.2
. The arguments
of
[
B13
]
show that they depend smoothly on all the
parameters. Also for
c=0
, by (
16.4
), we get

(16.11)

\mathrm{ch}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y},\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}},\theta\right)=\mathrm{ch}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y},\omega^{X},g^{D},g^{\widehat{TX}}\right),

which gives the last statement in our theorem, and concludes its
proof.
∎

16.4.
The scaling identities

For
c\in\left[0,1\right],d>0,t>0
, put

(16.12)

\theta_{t}=\left(c,dt\right).

As in (
15.20
), by (
16.4
), we have a splitting

(16.13)

\mathcal{A}_{Y,\theta}=\mathsf{A}_{Y,\theta}+C.

Let

\mathcal{A}_{Y,\theta,t}
be the superconnection
\mathcal{A}_{Y,\theta}

associated with

Proposition 16.6
.

The following identity holds:

(16.14)

t^{3N^{\widehat{V}}/2+N^{V}/2}K_{t}\mathcal{A}_{Y,\theta_{t},t}K_{t}^{-1}t^{-3N^{\widehat{V}}/2-N^{V}/2}\\
=t^{-N^{H}/2}\left(\sqrt{t}\mathsf{A}_{Y,\theta}+t^{N/2}Ct^{-N/2}\right)t^{N^{H}/2}.

Proof.

By combining Proposition

and Theorem

16.2
, we get (
16.14
). The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

16.5.
The uniform estimates

In the sequel, we will make
c=1
, so that for
d>0,t>0
,

(16.19)

\theta_{t}=\left(1,dt\right).

Put

(16.20)

\mathcal{M}_{Y,\theta,t}=K_{\left(dt\right)^{-1/4}}\left(\sqrt{t}\mathsf{A}_{Y,\theta}+t^{N/2}Ct^{-N/2}\right)^{2}K_{\left(dt\right)^{1/4}}.

Recall that

d\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)
denotes the Riemannian distance on

X
with respect to
\widehat{g}^{TX}
.
Here is an important result, which is an analogue of
[
B13
, Theorem
11.5.1]
.

Theorem 16.7
.

Given
k>0
, there exist
m\in\mathbf{N},c>0,C>0
such that for
t\in]0,1],d\in\left[kt^{3},1\right],z=\left(x,Y\right),z^{\prime}=\left(x^{\prime},Y^{\prime}\right)\in\mathcal{X}
, we have

(16.21)

\left|\exp\left(-\mathcal{M}_{Y,\theta,t}\right)\left(z,z^{\prime}\right)\right|\\
\leq\frac{C}{t^{m}}\exp\left(-c\left(\left|Y\right|_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}^{2}+\left|Y^{\prime}\right|_{g^{\widehat{TX}}}^{2}+\left(d/t^{3}\right)^{1/2}d^{2}\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)\right)\right).

Proof.

If we make
C=0

39
39

39

As the reader may have guessed, here

C
refers to the tensor defined in (
7.21
), and not to the
constant appearing in our theorem.
, the proof of our theorem was already given in

[
B13
, Theorem 11.5.1]
. Let us explain why the
introduction of the extra term containing
t^{N/2}Ct^{-N/2}
in
(
16.20
) does not change
anything to the estimates. Indeed this term remains uniformly
bounded. When taking the square in the right-hand side of
(
16.20
), this introduces, after scaling, a term which is
of the form

\left(t/d\right)^{1/4}i_{Y}t^{N/2}Ct^{-N/2}
.
In
C
, the component of total degree
0
in

\Lambda\left(T^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}X\right)
is
necessarily disregarded, which introduces an extra factor which
is at least

\sqrt{t}
. The factor that appears is then of the
order at most
\left(t^{3}/d\right)^{1/4}Y
. Under our
assumptions on
d
,
t^{3}/d
remains uniformly bounded, so that
this term is of order
\left|Y\right|
.
Under the indicated rescaling, a potential of the order of

\left|Y\right|^{4}
appears in

K_{\left(dt\right)^{-1/4}}t\mathsf{A}^{2}_{Y,\theta}K_{\left(dt\right)^{1/4}}
. The term
\left|Y\right|^{4}
being much bigger than
\left|Y\right|
, there is no difficulty in extending these
estimates to the present situation.
∎

Remark 16.8
.

Using (

) and (
16.20
), Theorem

will play the role of Proposition

15.6
.

16.6.
The exotic superconnection forms of
vector bundles

Let

In

, Definition 10.2.1]
, hypoelliptic superconnection forms

40
40

40

With the
notation of
[

]
, these forms are instead

.
on

•

They depend smoothly on the parameters.

•

They lie in

the parameters.

They are unchanged by constant rescaling of

.

•

The following identity holds:

(16.22)

a\left(F,0,g^{F}\right)=\mathrm{Td}\left(F,g^{F}\right).

If

(16.23)

a_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(F\right)=\mathrm{Td}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(F\right).

Theorem 16.10
.

The following identity holds:

(16.43)

\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}_{Y}\right)=p_{*}\left[q^{*}\mathrm{Td}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(TX\right)\mathrm{ch}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(A^{E\prime\prime}\right)\right]\,\mathrm{in}\,H^{(=)}_{\mathrm{BC}}\left(S,\mathbf{R}\right).

Proof.

Using Theorem
16.5
and either (
16.23
)
combined with the first equation in (
16.26
), or the second
row in (
16.26
), we get (
16.43
). The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

16.8.
A proof of Theorem
10.1

By combining Theorems
14.5
and
16.10
, we get
Theorem
10.1
, when
\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{E}
. As explained in
Subsection
10.2
, this is enough to establish
Theorem
10.1
in full generality. This also completes the
proof of our main result stated in Theorem
1.1
.
